# Round 5 findings (BUG-090 … BUG-098)

Found by the fifth adversarial pass against `integration` @ `3eb15e2`,
aimed at scanner v3, durability and locks after the latest fixes, the
materialisation record and the hardened SFTP client (and the Git bridge,
since removed). Each reproduction is an `#[ignore = "BUG-NNN: …"]` test in
`crates/*/tests/adversarial5_*.rs`;
`cargo test -p veil-core -p vl --test 'adversarial5_*' -- --ignored` runs
them. Line numbers refer to `3eb15e2`.

| Severity | Count | IDs |
|---|---|---|
| high | 1 | 098 |
| medium | 4 | 090, 091, 092, 096 |
| low | 4 | 093, 094, 095, 097 |

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-098 | high (**fixed**, policy wave 2) | isolation / auto-snapshot | **Fixed:** editor scratch files are ignored by a built-in rule, and every editor or backup artefact of a protected file (`.x.swp`, `x~`, `#x#`, `.#x`, `x.bak`, …) takes that file's protection (ARCHITECTURE §4 rule 2; `editor_artefacts.rs`, `artefacts_cli.rs`); the scanner also reads NUL-separated text. Editor artifacts of isolated files are ordinary public files. Vim's swap file of an isolated `id_ed25519` or `server.pem` (`.id_ed25519.swp`, `.server.pem.swp`) is recorded by the next `vl` command (every command snapshots) and pushed to every remote, public mirrors included, with the private key in plaintext. The scanner does not catch it: the PEM header sits in binary data, where a newline and key material must follow it, and Vim ends lines with NUL. Emacs's default backup `.env~` (Vim with `backup`) carries `.env`'s values out the same way; `.env~` matches neither `.env` nor `.env.*`, and the scanner misses typical `.env` values (`APP_KEY`, `RAILS_MASTER_KEY`, see BUG-092). Any user-isolated file (`notes/private.md` → `.private.md.swp`) leaks with no scanner rule at all. Git users are protected by `git add`; Veil records the swap file whenever any `vl` command (the user's, or an agent's) runs while the editor is open. | `policy.rs:60` (`BUILTIN_SECRETS`): no rule for editor swap and backup files of isolated paths (`.<name>.swp`, `.<name>.sw?`, `<name>~`, `#<name>#`), and isolation is per path, not per content. `secrets.rs:348` (`pem_body_follows`) and `secrets.rs:465` (`Check::PemHeader`): in binary data the header must be followed by `\n`; NUL-separated lines (and the NUL-stripped pass, `secrets.rs:552`, which glues them) never qualify. | `adversarial5_visibility`: `a_swap_file_of_an_isolated_key_is_not_recorded`, `an_editor_backup_of_dotenv_is_not_recorded` (control: `the_key_and_its_text_backups_stay_local`) |
| BUG-096 | medium (**fixed**, durability/lock wave) | durability / crash recovery | A crash recovery that fails hands the next holder a recovery window that skips the original dead holder's objects. A's barrier fails (EIO) and its voided objects cannot be removed, so A leaves the lock flagged `"recover": true` (the documented path). B takes over; its recovery fails on the first rewrite (disk full), so B abandons the lock in turn, but with **B's own** `since_stamp`. C's recovery then only looks at files modified after B took over and never rewrites A's remaining objects, which still read back intact from the page cache. C's retried snapshot reuses them, its barrier succeeds, the commit is acknowledged, and after the next power loss the head operation references corrupt blobs (`fsck`: "content does not match its hash", 15 objects in the test). On disk the window has a five-minute margin, which a retry after freeing space easily exceeds. | `lock.rs:712–714` (`acquire_in`): `recover_interrupted_writes` fails → `lock.abandon()`. `lock.rs:857` (`Drop`) writes the flagged lock with `self.extra`, whose `since` was set to the new holder's own acquisition stamp in `held()` (`lock.rs:744`), instead of carrying over the window it was recovering (`recovery_window`, `lock.rs:587`). | `adversarial5_durability`: `a_failed_recovery_does_not_narrow_the_next_recovery_window` (control: `a_successful_recovery_makes_the_retry_durable`) |
| BUG-090 | medium (**fixed**, scanner v4) | scanner / availability | **Fixed** (scanner v4): scanning is linear in the input size. Repetitions that could hold another match are bounded, so each search reads at most ~1 KiB past its match; work redone after a restart inside a rejected match is counted, and past 16× the input the search ends with a `scan-limit` finding (the file is quarantined). Crafted inputs: 24+ MiB/s in a release build (1 MiB of `xsk-` was about an hour). Originally: The scanner takes quadratic time on crafted input. 64 KiB of `xgithub_pat_` repeated takes ~5 s in a release build, `xsk-` ~14 s; 256 KiB takes minutes, 1 MiB about an hour. Every snapshot (`vl status`, `vl commit`, any command) and `vl scan` in CI stall on one such public file. It is remotely triggerable: files a Git commit adds are scanned on import, so anyone who can push a branch to the team's GitHub repository stalls `vl git fetch` / `vl git pull` for every member, holding the repository lock throughout (128 KiB: still running after 30 s in the test). | `secrets.rs:447` and `secrets.rs:455` (`search_with`): a match rejected for the byte before it (`Edge::Alnum`) or for its key (`credential_key`) restarts the search at `whole.start() + 1`. The unbounded token patterns (`github_pat_[A-Za-z0-9_]{60,}`, `sk-…{32,}`, `glpat-…{20,}`) and the unquoted assignment value (`secrets.rs:206`, class includes `=`) can contain their own prefix, so each repetition yields one rejected match as long as the rest of the run, and its capture groups are computed every time (`secrets.rs:443`). The token pass is not chunked. | `adversarial5_scanner`: `crafted_token_runs_do_not_make_the_scanner_quadratic` (control: `ordinary_text_scans_quickly`); `adversarial5_cli`: `fetching_a_branch_with_a_crafted_fixture_is_quick` (control: `fetching_a_branch_with_an_ordinary_fixture_is_quick`) |
| BUG-091 | medium (**fixed**, scanner v4) | scanner | **Fixed** (scanner v4): references count only as whole values or closed templates with a name made of words; quoted values take escaped quotes and YAML `''`; the rest of a configuration line is the value (`.properties`); long words are judged by letter-pair frequencies; the entropy bar depends on length and alphabet; Google app passwords are found with and without spaces. Measured over 20,000 passwords per cell, 16+ characters: ≤ 0.04 % missed (alphanumeric, `A-Za-z0-9!@#$%^&*`, printable ASCII, lower-case), 0 % at 24+ except lower-case (0.05 %). Originally: Strong random passwords are missed often. Measured with 2,000 generated passwords per cell in dotenv, YAML, JSON and `.properties` files (escaped as each format requires): passwords from `A-Za-z0-9!@#$%^&*` (a password manager with symbols on) are missed 6–8 % of the time in every format; passwords from all printable ASCII ~28 % in dotenv/YAML/JSON and ~90 % in `.properties`. Even 16 random alphanumerics are missed ~0.6 % of the time, and a Google app password (16 random lower-case letters) always. These are not the documented "weak passwords" or "word-only passphrases". | `secrets.rs:280` (`plausible_value`): any value starting with `$ % & * @ < {` or containing `${`, `{{`, `#{`, `%(` is taken for a reference. `secrets.rs:206`: the quoted value class `[!#-&(-~\x80-\xff]` excludes `"`, `'` and space, so a (correctly escaped) quote inside the password prevents any match; the unquoted class excludes `,;()[]{}<>'"\` and backquote and must be followed by a terminator. `secrets.rs:259` (`wordlike`): a mixed-case run with no two capitals in a row, or any all-lower-case run, counts as a word whatever its entropy. | `adversarial5_scanner`: `strong_random_passwords_are_found` (control: `long_random_alphanumeric_passwords_are_found`) |
| BUG-092 | medium (**fixed**, scanner v4) | scanner | **Fixed** (scanner v4): all 17 shapes are found (comma and `]` after a quoted key, Kubernetes `value:`, plural/numbered keys, six suffix words, element attributes and CDATA, plist, SQL, `*_KEY`/`*_SALT` names), plus `key="…" value="…"` attributes. Originally: Common credential shapes are never matched (17 of 17 in the test): WordPress `wp-config.php` (`define( 'DB_PASSWORD', '…' )`, and its eight `AUTH_KEY`/`…_SALT` secrets), `os.environ["API_KEY"] = "…"`, `config['password'] = '…'`, `ENV.fetch('SECRET_KEY', '…')`, `System.setProperty("db.password", "…")`, Kubernetes `- name: DB_PASSWORD` / `value: "…"`, numbered and plural keys (`PASSWORD2=`, `API_KEYS=`), four suffix words (`DB_PASSWORD_PROD_EU_WEST_1`), XML elements with an attribute or CDATA (`<password type="plain">…`), plist `<key>Password</key><string>…`, SQL `WITH PASSWORD '…'`, and `*_KEY` names (`APP_KEY` of Laravel, `RAILS_MASTER_KEY`, `ENCRYPTION_KEY`). | `secrets.rs:206`: the separator (`=`, `:`, `:=`, `=>`, `>`) must follow the keyword, at most one quote and up to three suffix words directly; a quote plus `,` / `]` / `)` between key and value, a digit or `s` after the keyword, or attributes in the element, never match. `secrets.rs:113` (`KEYWORDS`): `key` only counts as `api_key`, `secret_key`, `private_key`. | `adversarial5_scanner`: `common_credential_shapes_are_found` (control: `documented_assignment_shapes_are_found`) |
| BUG-093 | low (**fixed**, scanner v4) | scanner | **Fixed** (scanner v4): percent-decoded, `:` allowed in the password, `:\/\/` matched, user names up to 128 characters. Originally: URL passwords are missed when the first character is percent-encoded (`postgres://app:%21Xk9mQ2vL7@db`, as RFC 3986 requires for a leading `!`), in JSON that escapes `/` as `\/` (PHP's `json_encode` default: `postgres:\/\/app:…@db`), when the password contains `:` (`redis://:Xk9mQ2:vL7pR4wZ@cache`; userinfo may), and after a user name longer than 64 characters. | `secrets.rs:190`: the rule is anchored on the literal `://`, its password class excludes `:` and the user part is `{0,64}`. `secrets.rs:331` (`plausible_url_password`): a leading `%` is taken for a reference. | `adversarial5_scanner`: `url_password_variants_are_found` (control: `url_passwords_in_the_documented_form_are_found`) |
| BUG-094 | low (**fixed**, scanner v4) | scanner / UTF-16 | **Fixed** (scanner v4): where the NUL positions show UTF-16/32 (either byte order), a non-ASCII character next to ASCII text becomes a space in the stripped view. Originally: In UTF-16LE (and UTF-32), a token directly after kana or about half the CJK ideographs is missed. CJK text puts no space before a Latin token (`令牌ghp_…`, `アクセスキーAKIA…`), and Windows tools write UTF-16LE (Notepad "Unicode", Excel "Unicode text", PowerShell 5 redirection). The same text in UTF-8, or with a space, is found. | `secrets.rs:552–554`: the NUL-padded pass deletes NUL bytes instead of decoding, so the non-NUL byte of the preceding code unit (its high byte in UTF-16LE: `0`–`9` for U+30xx–39xx, `N`–`Z` / `a`–`z` for U+4Exx–5Axx / U+61xx–7Axx) becomes the token's "ASCII" neighbour, and the boundary check (`Edge::Alnum`, `Edge::UpperDigit`) rejects the token. | `adversarial5_scanner`: `cjk_text_glued_to_a_token_in_utf16_is_found` (control: `cjk_text_before_a_token_is_found_in_utf8_and_spaced_utf16`) |
| BUG-095 | low (**fixed**, scanner v4) | scanner | **Fixed** (scanner v4): in binary data an escaped `\n` before key material counts, so the key is found in a `.tar` or stored `.zip` (scanned as their bytes are). Compressed archives are not decompressed (documented limit). Originally: A Google Cloud service-account key (JSON with `"private_key": "-----BEGIN PRIVATE KEY-----\n…"`) is found as a file but missed inside an uncompressed archive (`.tar`: the header's NUL padding comes right before the content), and likewise in a SQLite database or core dump. | `secrets.rs:465` (`Check::PemHeader`): with NUL bytes within 256 bytes, the header must be followed by a real newline and key material (`pem_body_follows`, `secrets.rs:348`); JSON has the two characters `\n`. The assignment rule cannot match the value either (it contains spaces). | `adversarial5_scanner`: `a_service_account_key_inside_a_tar_is_found` (control: `a_service_account_key_file_is_found`) |
| BUG-097 | low (**fixed**, durability/lock wave) | locks | A `vl` killed with `kill -9` (or crashed) whose parent has not reaped it yet is a zombie, and keeps the repository locked for as long as the parent lives: the expired lock is judged "still running but no longer renews its lease" and never taken over, contrary to the instant recovery ARCHITECTURE §9 promises for `kill -9`. Parents that do this: an IDE, an agent harness or a script that killed a hung `vl` and has not waited for it. | `lock.rs:1388–1391` (`process_alive`): `/proc/<pid>` existence counts zombies as running (documented as intended); `pid_reused` sees the same start time, and `assess_full` returns `Verdict::Stuck`. The state field (`Z`) of `/proc/<pid>/stat` is never read. | `adversarial5_durability`: `a_killed_but_unreaped_holder_is_taken_over` (control: `a_killed_and_reaped_holder_is_taken_over`) |

## Round 5 hypotheses (not demonstrated)

* **Locks under `hidepid=2` / `ProtectProc=invisible`.** `process_alive` (`lock.rs:1391`) only checks that `/proc/<pid>` exists. When `/proc` hides other users' processes (`mount -o hidepid=2`, systemd's `ProtectProc=invisible` for a service such as a CI runner), a live holder that belongs to another user on the same machine (a shared team remote on local disk, a service and a user on one repository) looks dead, and its lock is taken over at once: two writers, a lost operation (the BUG-013 class). The same happens when `/proc` belongs to another pid namespace than the processes (`unshare -p` without `--mount-proc`), where `/proc/<pid>` names a different process. Not tested: needs root or user namespaces, neither available here. **Settled (durability/lock wave): it was real, and is fixed.** A missing `/proc/<pid>` is no longer proof of death: `kill(pid, 0)` decides (`EPERM`: another user's live process), and `/proc` is only read when `/proc/self` names our own pid (else it belongs to another pid namespace, and neither its entries nor its start times are used). Tested by injecting what such a `/proc` shows through `LockContext::probe` (`veil-core/tests/core_durability/lock.rs`: `a_holder_hidden_by_hidepid_is_not_taken_over`, `a_foreign_proc_falls_back_to_kill`, `liveness_needs_proof_of_death`); a real `hidepid` mount or pid namespace is still not exercised.
* **Stale local edits after a release revert the fix.** A non-holder's edit of a file shown as its embargo fallback (or an untracked file at a path the fix adds) is kept at fill-in and recorded on top of the released fix, so the next commit silently drops the fix from that file. `materialise_cli::a_local_edit_over_an_embargo_fallback_survives_the_release` asserts exactly this as intended. The warning shown (`kept … local edit not in history, e.g. quarantined`) does not say the release changed the file underneath, and nothing asks for a merge.
* **Redaction edge cases.** `secret_of` (now `remote.rs`) ends the authority at the first `/`, `?` or `#`, so a password containing one of them unencoded is printed in full by Veil's own messages (`Added Git remote …`, `remote list`, fetch errors); `git` rejects such URLs, so they never work. For `http(s)` only the whole `user:password` is replaced, so a server message that echoes the password alone would not be redacted. Credentials in the URL also reach `git`'s command line (`/proc/<pid>/cmdline`), as with plain `git`.
* **Assignment values longer than the chunk overlap.** A quoted value that starts within the last 16 KiB of a 256 KiB chunk and runs past the overlap cannot close inside the window, and the next chunk never sees the keyword. Needs a value over 16 KiB; not tested.
* **SFTP slow drip.** A server that answers each request just before the 300 s timeout keeps an operation alive for up to (requests × 300 s): each reply to an earlier request extends the later ones, and there is no overall deadline. A request that is never answered still fails after at most (earlier outstanding requests + 1) × 300 s, so it cannot be kept alive forever. Consistent with the documented design.

## Round 5 coverage notes

* **Scanner v3** (`adversarial5_scanner`, 13 tests): property-style generation of random passwords in four file formats (miss rates above), about 130 hand-made shapes (keywords, suffix exclusions, URL forms, XML, PEM variants, UTF-16/32 with and without BOM, mixed scripts, odd offsets, chunk boundaries), and timing of crafted runs. **False positives**, measured on `~/.cargo/registry/src` (329 crates, 12,941 files, 155 MiB, scanned in about 1 s in release): 22 files flagged (0.17 %). 13 of them hold real test or documentation private keys (`pkcs8`, `ed25519` fixtures), which are correct hits; the other 9 (0.07 %) are genuine false positives: `windows-sys` constants such as `D3DVSD_TOKENTYPE` and `WSMAN_OPTION_USE_INTEARACTIVE_TOKEN`, an `X509RequestInheritOptions` type, and `gix-url` test URLs (`ssh://jörg:passwörd@…`). Rerun with `VEIL_FP_DIR=<dir> … scanner_false_positive_rate`.
* **Durability and locks** (`adversarial5_durability`): a simulated disk with Linux's EIO semantics, ENOSPC on rewrites and power loss, shared by several simulated processes; failed barrier → `"recover": true` lock → failed recovery → retry → power loss; zombie and reaped holders. Reviewed without a finding: the store's discard-or-abandon path, the takeover guard and its nested `lock.steal.<hash>` sub-guards (each dead guard has one winner; a leftover sub-guard is cleaned by the next contender), fencing renewals and the 165 s skew rule (as documented), pack writes after a failed barrier (an index is only written after a successful one). `since_stamp` tampering by mtime alone is caught (recovery also requires an old `ctime`); a clock step of more than five minutes backwards is the documented margin.
* **Materialisation record:** `fill_in`, `absorb`, `holds`, the builder's `carry_unreadable` and the BUG-050 backstop, and the crash ordering in `Ctx::record`/`finish` were reviewed. A tampered or stale `checkout.json` cannot overwrite a local edit (fill-in always compares content), write outside the repository or into `.veil/` (paths are validated and reserved names refused), or keep a fallback across a release (fill-in materialises it first and drops it from the record). A `hidden` entry does make the builder carry a readable file that is absent, but fill-in removes every readable hidden entry first. No new bug beyond the hypothesis above.
* **SFTP client:** reviewed the per-request timeout rule, the packet, listing, depth and stray-reply limits, and the writer thread. No new finding (see the slow-drip hypothesis).
* **Not covered this round:** a real clock step on a DiskBackend; `hidepid`/pid-namespace setups (no root); an SFTP server test harness for the timeout rule (reviewed only); concurrent fill-in against a live editor; macOS.

---

# Round 6 findings (Fable)

Found by the sixth adversarial pass against `integration` @ `b0f5544`,
with fresh eyes on the engine's visibility rules under hostile tree
metadata, the working-copy and workspace commands of the user guide
(undo, switch, restore, checkout), the policy file as a file, embargo
records, and `ws exec --with-isolated`. Each reproduction is an
`#[ignore = "BUG-NNN: …"]` test in `crates/*/tests/adversarial6_*.rs`;
`cargo test -p veil-core -p vl --test 'adversarial6_*' -- --ignored` runs
them (every one fails; the controls next to them pass). Line numbers refer
to `b0f5544`.

| Severity | Count | IDs |
|---|---|---|
| high | 6 | 099, 101, 103, 106, 109, 110 |
| medium | 10 | 100, 104, 105, 108, 111, 113, 116, 117, 119, 120 |
| low | 6 | 102, 107, 112, 114, 115, 118 |

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-103 | high (**fixed**) | builder / hostile remote | **Fixed:** visibility labels and the snapshot `cloak` field are claims: the audience of new content comes from the accepted policy, a cloak that names a scope, and the parent entry's sealing as verified against its object's envelope label; a mismatch warns (`BuildReport::relabelled`) and is decided on the object; released embargoes, key generations and `local` never seal new content. Same rule in merges/rebases, normalisation and declassification, embargo wrapping, squash and descriptions (ARCHITECTURE §4 rule 5, threat model; `hostile_labels_props.rs`). Originally: A tree entry's visibility label from a remote is trusted. Mallory (write access to the team remote, not in `team`) pushes `main` forward with Alice's tree, except that the entry of a cloaked file (`secrets/db.yml`) says `cloaked:embargo/<id>` for an embargo Mallory created under a scope of her own and released, or `cloaked:mal` for a scope she granted Alice into. The sealed object is Alice's (opened through its own label), so after `vl pull` + `vl new main`/`vl rebase -d main` (the guide's post-pull step) her working copy shows the right content and nothing warns. Her next edit of the file is sealed under the embargo key, which is public: the public mirror accepts the object as "released" and an anonymous reader decrypts her new secret. With `cloaked:mal`, her edit is re-keyed to Mallory's scope (BUG-009 by another route; `vl merge` refuses it, `vl new`/`rebase` do not). The same relabelling freezes any public file for everyone (edits held back for "no key for scope 'mal'"). | `builder.rs:777–791` (`file_entry`, "sealed content keeps its audience"): the *parent entry's* `visibility` decides the audience of the new content, without checking that the sealed object's envelope label matches it, and without refusing `embargo/…` labels, which the policy parser refuses (BUG-008). `keychain.rs:1663–1666` (`embargo_key`): a released embargo's key is handed to everyone, so `sealing_key("embargo/<released>")` seals new content under a public key. `vl fsck` does warn about the relabelled entry ("entry says sealed under 'embargo/…' but object … is sealed under 'team'"); pull, checkout, `vl files` and the builder do not. | `adversarial6_visibility`: `a_relabelled_entry_cannot_seal_the_victims_edit_under_a_public_key`, `a_relabelled_entry_cannot_rekey_the_victims_edit_to_an_attacker_scope`, `a_hostile_cloak_field_cannot_seal_the_victims_work_under_a_public_key` (the snapshot's `cloak` field naming a released embargo seals every new file and message under the public key) |
| BUG-106 | high (**fixed**, policy wave) | policy file / declassification | **Fixed:** A symlinked `.veilpolicy` is now followed (to a regular file ≤ 1 MiB named `*veilpolicy*`, FIFOs refused, non-blocking open) but never taken as the member's edit; a missing one is not an edit either; both stay guarded by the pin (`policy::read_policy_file`, ARCHITECTURE §4). `vl cloak` & co. refuse to write over the link; `vl policy accept` reads through it. When `.veilpolicy` is a symlink (a dotfiles manager, `stow`, `chezmoi`, `cp -s`, a monorepo pointing it at a shared file), `DiskBackend` reports nothing at it, `Policy::load` finds no policy, and every user rule stops applying. The "deleted" policy counts as the member's own edit, the accepted policy follows it, and because the policy changed the builder lifts "sealed content keeps its audience": the next snapshot re-stores every cloaked file **in plaintext** (`secrets/db.yml`: `cloaked:team` → `public`), new secrets are quarantined instead of cloaked, and the next push sends the plaintext to the public mirror. The only message, `.veilpolicy is tracked but is now a symlink …; Veil never follows it and keeps the recorded version`, says the opposite of what happens (the recorded *entry* is kept, the rules are not). | `vfs/disk.rs:586` (`metadata`: nothing at a symlink) with `policy.rs:495` (`Policy::load`: `None` → built-in only); `wc_policy.rs:46–57` (`snapshot_policy`: the member's "edit" is folded); `builder.rs:788` (`reclassified` lifts the audience rule). | `adversarial6_cli`: `a_symlinked_policy_file_still_applies` |
| BUG-109 | high (**fixed**, embargo wave) | undo / embargo | **Fixed:** `vl embargo release` previews what becomes public (merge, withheld history, tags, remotes) and asks (`--yes` skips; required off a terminal). The release is *staged* in device-local state (`.veil/local/releases/`, read by no sync, honoured only by the identity that signed it) and written into the synced record only by the next push (`Keychain::publish_staged_releases`). Until then `vl undo` deletes it ("Embargo … is unreleased again: its key had not left this repository"); afterwards undo is refused and changes nothing, and so is undoing the undo of a release. `vl op restore` takes back staged releases made after the target and warns about published ones. Was: `vl embargo release` records an operation, so `vl undo` prints `Undid: release embargo <id>`. The release lives in the embargo record (the published key), not in the view the operation log restores, so nothing is undone: `vl embargo list` still says `released`, and the next `vl push mirror` publishes the security fix. The guide promises "Undo anything" and lists every repository-changing command as an operation; this one is the one whose accidental use publishes a vulnerability. | `commands.rs:550` (`release_embargo`): `keys().release_embargo(id)` writes the record, then an op with `record_always` and an unchanged view; `Repo::undo_keeping_edits` only reinstates a view. | `adversarial6_cli`: `undo_of_an_embargo_release_keeps_the_fix_embargoed` |

The snapshot compares `.veilpolicy` with the tree the directory was checked out from, so a policy that arrived by `restore --from` counts as **the member's own edit** and is folded into the accepted policy at once: a teammate's `.env public!` applies without `vl policy accept`, `.env` is recorded in plaintext (`A .env`, `public 27 .env`), and the next commit and push publish it. The guide names merge, pull, checkout, rebase, Git import and undo as the guarded routes; `restore --from` is a seventh. | `main.rs:873–923` (`cmd_restore`: `ctx.fs.write` of every differing path, then `snapshot_as`); `wc_policy.rs:46` (`snapshot_policy(fs, Some(&wc.tree))`: any difference from the checked-out tree is the member's edit, `policy::fold_edit`). | `adversarial6_cli`: `restore_from_a_revision_does_not_accept_its_policy` (control: `a_pulled_policy_is_held_back_after_a_rebase`) |

ARCHITECTURE §4: "Any new or changed file, of any tier, that contains one of the injected values is quarantined". | `builder.rs:133` (`InjectedSecrets::from_files`: lossy text) and `builder.rs:195–196` (`secret_like`: `v.contains('\u{fffd}')` → not a needle); no raw-bytes needle. | `adversarial6_visibility`: `an_injected_binary_secret_is_recognised_in_a_copy`, `a_copy_of_an_injected_keystore_is_quarantined`; `adversarial6_cli`: `a_copied_keystore_is_quarantined_in_the_sandbox` (controls: `an_injected_text_secret_is_recognised_in_a_copy`, `a_copied_text_secret_is_quarantined_in_the_sandbox`) |

Was: Bob was in `team` when the embargo was created, so he holds the embargo key. Alice revokes him; revocation is forward-only, so he keeps reading the fix (documented). A `.veil/lock` that is a symlink (dangling or not) spins every command at full CPU for ever with no error, locally (another user on a shared checkout, a restore that turned files into links) and on an SSH remote (one hostile or mangled file wedges every `vl push`/`vl pull` client; measured 25 s of CPU per 40 s, still running after 5 min), although ARCHITECTURE §1 says a hostile or broken server "can neither hang the client nor exhaust its memory". They survive only in the op log (`vl op restore`). | `workspace.rs:437–443` (`last_command_op`: compares the description with `snapshot working copy of '<ws>'` for the current workspace only). | `adversarial6_cli`: `undo_after_a_workspace_switch_does_not_discard_the_previous_workspaces_edits` (control: `undo_after_a_commit_keeps_the_edits_made_since`) |
| BUG-111 | medium (**fixed**, working-copy wave) | checkout / availability | A *file* in the working copy (a quarantined `cfg`, never recorded; any untracked file) where the target tree has a directory `cfg/`: checkout writes `cfg/settings.json`, `DiskBackend::write` fails with `File exists` on the parent, the checkout aborts half way, and the error is returned after the view already moved. Every later command fails the same way (`vl status`, `vl log`: each finds the working copy stale and retries the same checkout). Only `vl --no-snapshot undo` or deleting the file by hand gets out. The documented behaviour for a file in the way is to keep it and report it (`CheckoutReport::blocked`/`kept_local`). | `checkout.rs:535` (`apply_changes`: `fs.write` with no check that the parent chain is made of directories; `SpecialGuard` only knows symlinks and special files). | `adversarial6_cli`: `a_file_in_the_way_of_a_directory_is_kept_and_reported` |

The retired copy is created the right way (`create_new`, mode `0600`). Originally: Git, `scp` and `sftp` take `host:~/path` as the home directory. The guide's CI recipe (`vl init` in the Git checkout, `vl --no-snapshot scan`; "this fails the job on a hit") takes its rules from the checkout it scans: `vl init` keeps the checked-out `.veilpolicy`, and `vl scan` skips `public!` and `ignore` paths. ## Round 6 fixes: working-copy wave

* **BUG-110, BUG-111:** checkout removes a directory in the way of a file only once it has emptied it, and keeps a file in the way of a directory; both are reported (`CheckoutReport::obstructed`, `kept X (…)`) and the rest of the checkout goes ahead. `prune_empty_parents` no longer deletes a directory holding only a symlink or a non-UTF-8 name. The vault keeps the backup of a vanished isolated file for 30 days (`vl secret files`, `vl secret recover`). Tests: `checkout_obstacles.rs`.
* **BUG-113:** moving the directory is journalled in `checkout.json` (`pending`) and finished by the next command (`working_copy.rs`); swept by `crash.rs` `crash_during_workspace_switch`.
* **BUG-117:** `vl watch` re-reads `checkout.json` every tick and yields while the directory is behind the view; every snapshot is checked against the working copy it was checked out from under the lock (`SnapshotOptions::expected_wc`).
* **BUG-100:** `vl ws switch` is an operation; `vl undo` right after it switches back. Property test: `workspace_undo_props.rs`.
* **BUG-116:** `vl scope create|grant|revoke|rotate`, `vl remote add|rm`, `vl secret set|rm|import` and `vl policy accept` record operations marked `manual_undo`; `vl undo` refuses them with instructions, `vl op restore` warns. `vl embargo release` (BUG-109) is left to the embargo wave, which can mark it the same way (`Transaction::manual_undo`).
* **BUG-102:** `vl ws write` fails, saying why, when the builder did not record the path.

## Round 6 hypotheses (not demonstrated)

* **Policy trust on first use.** A clone, or `vl init` in a Git checkout (the CI recipe), accepts the checked-out `.veilpolicy` as the device's accepted policy, `public!` rules included, and nothing at clone time lists them. A user who clones a hostile repository and then drops a `.env` into it has it recorded in plaintext (the `A .env` line of `vl status` is the only signal). Documented as "first use"; the same mechanism as BUG-115.
* **Legacy embargo records without `key_id`.** `release_consistent` accepts *any* published key for a record that has no `key_id` (format-1 records made before BUG-012's fix). A forged release of such a record would be accepted and lock key holders out of the content, as in BUG-012. The golden fixtures all carry `key_id`; not demonstrated against a real old record.
* **`Snapshot.embargo` naming an unknown embargo.** A snapshot from a remote whose `embargo` field names an id with no record counts as "unreleased and unknown" (`carried_embargo`), so no `--embargo` merge is possible on that line until it is rewritten, and its merged-in parents are withheld from every push. A hostile writer can thus disable embargoes on `main`; a legitimate record that failed to sync has the same effect. Reasoned from `commands.rs` `carried_embargo` and `remote.rs` `embargo_withheld`; not tested.
* **`vl ws exec` and a concurrent command.** Like `vl watch` (BUG-117), a long `ws exec` finishes with the checkout pointer it read at start. Its `finish` re-applies the diff between the stale pointer and the current working copy onto a directory the other command already updated; `holds` makes that benign (files already hold the target content), but edits the user made meanwhile at paths that differ between the two snapshots are reported as `kept` and the "Updated N files" line is wrong. Not demonstrated as data loss.
* **`vl op restore` to before the attached workspace existed** ends with `error: not found: workspace '<ws>'` after the restore is recorded; the next command attaches to another workspace and checks the directory out from the removed workspace's snapshot (recoverable, as the removed snapshot is in the op log). Cosmetic.
* **`VEIL_HOME=` (set but empty)** puts `identity.json` in the current directory (`PathBuf::from("")`); in a working copy the scanner quarantines it (the JSON holds 64-hex keys). `VEIL_FS_FOLDING=garbage` is silently ignored. Remote names are not validated (`vl remote add '' …`, `../evil`, `origin/main` all work; the last makes `origin/main` both a remote-tag key of `origin` and a remote name).
* **`sftp_sync::concurrent_pushers_serialise_on_the_remote_lock`** (since demonstrated and fixed as BUG-120) failed in both full-workspace runs (`unwrap` on a push error at `sftp_sync.rs:206`) and passed three times alone: flaky under load (five clients racing a fencing lock on a stand-in server), not a regression of this round.

## Round 6 coverage notes

* **Visibility rules under hostile metadata** (`adversarial6_visibility`): tree entries and snapshot fields (`visibility`, `cloak`, `change_id`) crafted by a team-remote writer and pulled by a member; what the builder, `vl new`, `vl rebase`, `vl edit` and a public push then do with them. `vl merge` was reviewed and holds (`normalize_tree` keeps the destination's audience). `vl fsck` reports the relabelled entry (`entry says sealed under … but object … is sealed under …`); nothing on the pull/checkout/edit path does.
* **Embargo records**: creation, release and merge rules against revoked members, non-members and public-mirror clones; the wrapper's plaintext `listed` set. Timed release and the `until` field were reviewed (signed, so tamper-proof) and not re-tested.
* **The user guide's workflows through the real binary** (`adversarial6_cli`): undo after `ws switch`, `ws rm`, `op restore`, scope/remote/secret commands and `embargo release`; `restore --from`; checkout over local-only directories and files in the way; interrupted `ws switch`; `vl watch` next to another command; `ws write`/`ws exec` messages; the policy file as a symlink, unreadable, a directory, cloaked by its own rule (stays public), and with pathological globs (fast); the CI scan recipe; odd revisions, tags, workspace and remote names (no panics); environment variables (`VEIL_FS_FOLDING`, `VEIL_SFTP_*`, `VEIL_USER`, empty `VEIL_HOME`) and broken state files (garbage lock, garbage identity, unreadable `VEIL_HOME`): clean errors except as listed.
* **Injected secrets** (`ws exec --with-isolated`): text, binary, UTF-16, `.netrc`/`.pgpass` shapes were reviewed; only the binary case fails (BUG-099). `.netrc` lines and other whitespace-separated formats yield only a whole-file needle, so transformed copies (`awk`, `grep`) of them are not caught; documented as "values that look like secrets".
* **Property tests** (`adversarial6_props`): the policy fold's own invariant over random accepted/parent/member policies and overlapping patterns (600 cases, no counterexample); never-panic fuzzing of `SshUrl::parse` (also: no option-like host or user reaches `ssh`), `parse_public_identity`, `parse_public_key`, `ChangeId`/`ObjectId` parsing, tag/workspace/scope name validation and `VPath`; byte-mutated signed rings (a mutated ring that still verifies derives the same state) and embargo records.
* **SSH remotes** through a fake `ssh` serving the local `sftp-server`: tilde paths, a hostile server's lock file (symlink: BUG-119; garbage: handled; directory: refused), `.veil/objects` replaced by a symlink to `/etc` (clean error), clone over it.
* **Locks** (`adversarial6_lock`): only the absent-but-existing lock path; the lock's takeover rules, fencing and recovery windows were left to the parallel fix wave for BUG-096/097.
* **Not covered this round:** `vl upgrade`; `gc` beyond cloaked-fallback reachability (kept) and the wrapper's listed set; the scanner (BUG-090…095 in progress elsewhere); macOS; concurrent processes beyond `vl watch`; the identity-file race itself (only the creation mode is shown).

## Findings from the round-6 fix wave (not yet reproduced)

Reported by the fix agents while working on round 6 (`integration` @
`a0c3d71`). They are inferred from the code and have no reproduction test
yet; the next fix wave writes one first.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-121 | medium (**fixed**, working-copy wave 2) | policy / hostile remote | **Fixed:** Reproduced (`hostile_policy_dir.rs`: checkout, the snapshot after it, and a merge all failed with `cannot read .veilpolicy: it is a directory`). Checkout and merge now load a tree's policy with `Policy::load_or_accepted`: an unreadable `.veilpolicy` (a directory, a symlink, sealed) falls back to the policy accepted on this device (the built-in rules if none) with a warning (`CheckoutReport::warnings`, `MergeResult::warnings`); the builder and `policy_text_in` treat a directory entry as no readable policy. Originally: A recorded *directory* named `.veilpolicy` (pushed by a hostile teammate) probably makes `Policy::load` on that tree fail during checkout or merge, blocking `vl new main` for everyone who pulls it. A member's own working copy never records one (an empty directory records the file as deleted). Found by the policy wave. | `Policy::load` on a tree expects a blob at `.veilpolicy`. | `veil-core`: `hostile_policy_dir.rs` (checkout, snapshot after it, merge), `git.rs::a_git_commit_whose_policy_is_a_directory_is_refused_with_the_reason` |
| BUG-122 | medium (**fixed**, audience wave; feature **removed**, format 8) | declassification / hostile remote | **Fixed:** a merge publishes a cloaked line of `s` only if every snapshot it brings in (ancestors of the merged-in heads, not of the destination) is signed by a current member of `s` (`Keychain::signed_by_member`, `Repo::merged_in_signed_by`), and only content the merge brings in: an entry equal to the destination's stays sealed. Otherwise it warns and the files stay cloaked. Rule and its justification: ARCHITECTURE §4 rule 5 ("True labels are not enough to publish"). Tests: `audiences.rs` (the attack unsigned, signed by an outsider and by a member of another scope; Mallory's snapshot on top of Alice's genuine line; objects planted on the destination; control: a teammate's signed line is still published). Originally: A merged-in line can declassify a teammate's genuine sealed objects: Mallory writes a snapshot with `cloak: "team"` whose tree puts Alice's real `cloaked:team` objects at new paths; when Alice merges that "team line" into a public line, those files are published (after a secret scan). Label verification (BUG-103) cannot stop it, because the labels are true. Found by the visibility wave. | Declassification on merge publishes every cloaked entry of the merged line, not only the content that line introduced. Limit it to introduced content, or to lines signed by scope members. | `audiences::a_claimed_team_line_cannot_declassify_a_teammates_sealed_objects`, `audiences::an_unvouched_snapshot_on_a_genuine_team_line_blocks_declassification`, `audiences::a_genuine_declassifying_merge_publishes_only_what_its_line_brings_in` (control: `audiences::a_teammates_cloaked_line_is_still_declassified_by_a_merge`) |
| BUG-123 | medium (**fixed**, audience wave) | merge / audiences | **Fixed:** when the two sides are (verifiably) sealed under different scopes, `merge_file` seals nothing for either: the file is a conflict and one side's entry is kept unchanged, the side whose scope is the base's, else ours (so an auto-rebased pending `team` edit stays `team`). Merge normalisation no longer reseals a merged-in edit sealed for another scope under the destination's scope; the destination keeps its entry, with a warning. Tests: `audiences.rs`, and `hostile_labels_props.rs` extended with a genuine `mal` object through rebase and merge. Originally: When the two sides of a merge are sealed under different scopes, the result is sealed under ours' scope. If Mallory replaces a file's object with a genuinely `mal`-sealed one and Alice is a `mal` member, Alice's pending `team` edit is merged and resealed under `mal` on auto-rebase, moving her content to another audience. Should be a conflict when neither scope is narrower. Found by the visibility wave. | `merge_file` picks ours' audience whenever both sides are sealed. | `audiences::a_rebase_never_moves_a_pending_edit_to_another_scope`, `audiences::a_merge_of_two_audiences_is_a_conflict_not_a_rekey`, `audiences::a_merge_never_reseals_another_scopes_content_for_the_destination`, `hostile_labels_props::a_genuine_other_scope_object_is_neutralised_by_rebase_and_merge`, `hostile_labels_props::a_merge_of_two_scopes_never_moves_the_pending_edit` |
| BUG-124 | low (**fixed**, audience wave; feature **removed**, format 8) | workspaces | **Fixed:** `create_workspace` calls `validate_scope_name` before the membership check, so `embargo/<id>`, `local`, key generations and malformed names are refused. Originally: `vl ws create --cloak embargo/<id>` is accepted, because it only checks `is_member` (true for any embargo whose key you hold). The builder and descriptions now ignore such a cloak (BUG-103), but the command should refuse non-scope names. Found by the visibility wave. | `create_workspace` does not call `validate_scope_name`. | `audiences::a_workspace_cannot_be_cloaked_under_an_embargo_or_a_reserved_name` |
| BUG-125 | medium (**fixed**, builder audience fix) | builder / audiences / hostile remote | **Fixed:** "sealed content keeps its audience" never takes a scope the policy does not name. When the policy (pinned by the accepted policy) cloaks the path under `P` and the parent's verified sealing is another scope `Q`, and this snapshot does not re-key the path by editing `.veilpolicy`, an edit is held back (`cloak_failures`, the file stays local, history keeps the recorded entry) instead of being sealed for `Q`; unchanged content keeps its entry. Holding back rather than sealing for `P` follows BUG-123 (the edit holds `Q` content, and membership is not ordered) and leaves an unaccepted incoming re-key to `vl policy accept`; the report names `vl cloak --scope P PATH` as the deliberate way out. Rationale in ARCHITECTURE §4 rule 5. Tests: `hostile_labels_props.rs` with `Label::GenuineMal` and `Label::GenuineMalPolicy` (the same object plus `.veilpolicy` re-keyed to `mal`, held back by the accepted policy) through `vl new`/`vl edit`, the way out, and rebase/merge. The builder's "sealed content keeps its audience" rule trusts the parent's *verified* scope over the policy. Mallory (who can push to the team remote) replaces `secrets/db.yml` on `main` with a genuine object sealed under `mal`, a scope Alice is also a member of; `.veilpolicy` still says `secrets/ cloak team`. Alice pulls, `vl new main`, and writes her new secret into the file: it is recorded sealed under `mal`, silently (no `relabelled` report, no warning), so Mallory reads it. BUG-123 closed the merge and rebase routes; `vl new`/`vl edit` + edit goes through the builder. Alice does see the file's content change on checkout. Found by the audience wave (probe test, not committed). | `builder.rs` (sealed content keeps its audience): a parent entry sealed under `s` keeps `s` unless this snapshot edits `.veilpolicy`, even when the parent tree's own policy classifies the path as cloaked under another scope `t`. No honest route produces that state (a re-key edits the policy in the same snapshot), so such an entry could be sealed under the policy's scope, or held back with a warning. | `crates/veil-core/tests/core_props/hostile_labels_props.rs` (`a_genuine_other_scope_object_is_never_the_audience_of_an_edit`) |
| BUG-126 | low to medium (**fixed**, builder audience fix) | merge / audiences | **Fixed:** a conflict's markers show the merge base's version (the `||||||| original` section) only when the base is public, under a released embargo, or verifiably sealed exactly as the merged result; otherwise they show the two sides only (Git's `merge` style). Rationale in ARCHITECTURE §4 rule 5. Reproduced (orchestrator's suspicion, confirmed by test): a conflicted text merge writes diff3 markers, and the merged file takes the *sides'* visibility. A file sealed for `team` at the merge base, exposed on both sides with two edits of the same line, merged into a public conflict whose markers held the `team` line; `vl push` to a public remote and `vl git push` published it. Same for a base sealed for `ops` under two `team` sides (the `team` result showed `ops` text). Anyone who can make two public lines on top of a teammate's sealed snapshot and get them merged can read the sealed version. | `merge::merge_file` rendered conflicts with `diffy::merge_bytes`, whose default style is diff3, whatever the base's audience; the result's audience came from `merged_visibility(ours, theirs)` only. A clean merge never holds a base-only line, so only the markers leaked. | `crates/vl/tests/cli_security/cloaked_work_cli.rs` (`a_conflict_never_shows_a_base_sealed_for_another_audience`: snapshot and public mirror clone); `crates/veil-core/tests/core_security/audiences.rs` (`a_conflict_never_shows_a_sealed_base_in_a_public_result`, `a_conflict_never_shows_another_scopes_base`, and the regression guard `a_conflict_shows_the_base_to_its_own_audience`) |
| BUG-127 | medium (**fixed**, at integration of the merge and audience waves) | merge / audiences | **Fixed:** where the two merge bases of a criss-cross merge disagree, the virtual base keeps their common ancestor's version only when that ancestor's audience is the merge bases' (`base_shown`); otherwise it keeps nothing there, so the two later versions still conflict. Was: the virtual merge base (BUG-244 fix) kept the common ancestor's lines where the merge bases disagree, but was sealed from the two merge bases' visibility, so with an ancestor sealed for `team` and two public merge bases the `team` text was stored as a public object and, with the BUG-126 check passing on the public virtual base, shown in the conflict markers of the public criss-cross merge result. Found by the builder-audience agent while reviewing the merge wave. | `merge.rs` (`merge_file`, `Mode::VirtualBase`): the kept base text and the result's visibility came from different inputs. | `audiences`: `a_virtual_base_never_stores_a_sealed_ancestors_text_for_a_wider_audience` (control: `a_virtual_base_keeps_a_public_ancestors_text`) |
| BUG-128 | low, was high until shown otherwise (**fixed**, BUG-128 fix; feature **removed**, format 8) | cloaked workspaces / confidentiality | **Fixed:** not a leak. The public object is never new content: it is the *fallback* (the last public version) of a sealed edit, so it was already public and already what readers without the key were shown at that path. The builder's "edited back to its last public version → record it public" shortcut (`builder.rs`, `file_entry`) fired on a sealed edit whose fallback the line's base no longer holds: the cloaked change had been rebased (or its parent abandoned) onto a base without the file, leaving a sealed entry with the old fallback, or a modify/delete conflict, and the workspace write put exactly those bytes back. On a cloaked line the shortcut now applies only where the line's base (the parents' merged tree, `BuildOptions::line_base`, computed lazily) shows that version at that path, as a public entry or as a sealed entry's fallback; otherwise the edit is sealed (with the fallback). The property check now also accepts the BUG-074 case it used to flag in principle: a child of a sealed edit edited back to that edit's fallback, which readers without the key see unchanged. `Repo::build_amending` rebuilds a snapshot with its line's base (`vl`'s stale-working-copy check uses it). Tests: the reproduction; `cloaked_fallback`: `a_cloaked_edit_reverted_to_a_fallback_its_base_lacks_stays_sealed` (control: `a_cloaked_edit_reverted_to_the_bases_public_version_is_the_bases_entry`); property test clean at `PROPTEST_CASES=2000` (×3), the reproduction ×10 (100 replays each). Originally: A change on a cloaked line of work can record a new file as **public**: after the sequence in the test, `dir/sub/c.txt` appears in a cloaked change (sealed description, `cloak` set, one parent without that path) with `visibility: Public`. The property `cloaked_lines_of_work_never_hold_public_entries` finds it rarely, and the same operations reproduce it in about 1 run in 12, because which changes the indices pick depends on random ids. Present before the first-class-conflicts merge (16 of 200 runs at `e69eed9`). Whether the public object is new content or an existing public blob (copied or moved) is not yet known; if new, it would reach public remotes. | unknown: `vl ws write` into a cloaked workspace, or an auto-rebase of the cloaked change, records the entry without sealing it. | `adversarial_props`: `bug128_cloaked_line_records_a_public_file` |

## Conformance findings: history, diff, merge and rewriting

Found by porting Git's t4xxx (diff, log), t6xxx (revision walking,
merging) and t34xx (rebase) scenarios, and jj's rebase, squash, abandon,
diff, log, evolog, revset and conflict tests, to Veil (`integration` @
`8dbdb61`). Each reproduction is an `#[ignore = "BUG-NNN: …"]` test in
`crates/vl/tests/cli_conformance/conformance_history/`;
`cargo test -p vl --test cli_conformance -- --ignored conformance_history::` runs them (every
one fails). The coverage map is `docs/conformance/history.md`. Line numbers
refer to `8dbdb61`.

| Severity | Count | IDs |
|---|---|---|
| high | 1 | 251 |
| medium | 4 | 243, 244, 247, 249 |
| low | 7 | 240, 241, 242, 245, 246, 248, 250 |

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-251 | high (**fixed**, merge wave) | auto-rebase / merges | **Fixed:** a moved snapshot is re-merged: `Repo::rebase_tree` merges its tree over the merge of its old parents onto the merge of its new parents (used by the auto-rebase and `vl rebase`), so a carried merge keeps what its other parents hold; conflicts of the new parents' own merge are recorded when they reach the result (this also makes `vl rebase -d X -d Y` record an X/Y conflict, previously dropped). A merge whose first parent is rebased loses files that its *other* parent still has. `a ← b ← c`, `a ← side`, `m = merge(c, side)`; `vl rebase -s b -d nb` (where `nb` doesn't contain `a`) leaves `m` with parents `c'` and `side`, but `a` is gone from `m`'s tree, and from every descendant and the working copy on top of it. No conflict or warning. The natural merge of `c'` and `side` contains `a`, as jj and `git rebase --rebase-merges` keep it. The content is still in `side`'s history and in the op log. | `rebase_descendants` (`transaction.rs:234`) rebases a merge one parent at a time: `merge_trees(old parent tree, new parent tree, tree)` for each moved parent. That replays "`b`'s line lost `a`" onto the merge, without the other parents. It should rebase the merge onto the merge of its new parents against the merge of its old parents (as `parents_tree` computes). | `conformance_history::rewrite::rebase_carries_a_descendant_merge` (control: `rebase_carries_a_descendant_merge_with_disjoint_files`) |
| BUG-244 | medium (**fixed**, merge wave) | merge | **Fixed:** `Repo::merge_bases` returns every best common ancestor, and `Repo::merge_base_tree` combines several into a virtual merge base (recursively), where whatever the bases disagree on keeps their common ancestor's version (per text hunk), so disagreeing resolutions conflict again. The reproduction's setup was corrected: `vl merge left --into right` moves the `right` tag to R2, so L2 merged R2 and there was no criss-cross; it now merges the original `right` by id (and checks L2's parents). A criss-cross merge picks one merge base and silently takes one side's earlier resolution. `left` and `right` change the same line (`B`/`C`). `R2 = merge(right, left)` is resolved as `B`, and `L2 = merge(left, right)` as `C`. `vl merge` of `R2` and `L2` produces `B` with no conflict, which drops `L2`'s resolution. Git (virtual merge base) and jj report a conflict. Which side wins depends on parent order. | `Repo::common_ancestor` (`repo.rs:466`) returns the first common ancestor a BFS meets, and `parents_tree` (`repo.rs:503`) merges against that single base. With two merge bases, that base equals one side's version, so the other side's resolution looks like the only change. | `conformance_history::merge::criss_cross_merge_with_different_resolutions_conflicts` (control: `criss_cross_merge_with_agreeing_resolutions_is_clean`) |
| BUG-243 | medium (**fixed**, merge wave) | merge / file-directory conflict | **Fixed:** both sides survive: the directory keeps the name and the file moves aside to `NAME~ours`/`NAME~theirs` (the side it came from, `~2`… if taken); both paths are recorded as conflicted. Two directories (or two files) now merge as such even when the base had the other kind at that name (two added directories over a deleted file used to drop one). Documented in ARCHITECTURE §5 and the guide. When one side replaces file `thing` with a directory `thing/inner.txt` and the other side edits `thing`, `vl merge` reports `conflicts in: thing`, but the result keeps only the file. `thing/inner.txt` is in neither the merged tree nor the working copy, and nothing (no marker, no renamed copy) shows that it existed. Once the user edits `thing`, the conflict clears and the directory side is gone from the line. Git keeps both (`thing~HEAD`); jj records a file/directory conflict. | `merge_dir` (`merge.rs:226`): "modify/delete or file/directory conflict: keep the surviving side (ours first) and flag it", i.e. `oe.or(te)`. For a file/directory clash both sides survive, and the other one is dropped. | `conformance_history::merge::file_directory_conflict_keeps_both_sides_content` (controls: `file_directory_conflict_is_flagged`, `directory_file_conflict_is_flagged`) |
| BUG-247 | medium (**fixed**, CLI wave) | undo | **Fixed:** repeated `vl undo` now goes further back and the new `vl redo` re-applies what the latest undos took back, as in jj; any other command clears what could be redone. Undo and redo operations record `undo_of` / `redo_of` (optional op-log fields; FORMAT.md), and `Repo::undo_target` / `redo_target` walk past them. Undo and redo also carry recorded edits of every workspace, and refuse to delete a workspace that holds some. Property tests: `veil-core/tests/core_props/undo_redo_props.rs` (jj model), `workspace_undo_props.rs` (now with redo). The guide says "`vl undo` undoes the last operation. Run it again to go further back." The second `vl undo` instead undoes the first undo, re-applying the operation the user just took back (`Undid: undo: describe change …`). Two undos after two rewrites leave the repository after the *second* rewrite, not before the first. Recoverable with `vl op restore`, but surprising when cleaning up a botched rebase. **Update (`a0c3d71`):** the guide now says a second undo is a redo, so the docs match the code; the fix still to make is the jj-style design (repeated `vl undo` goes further back, plus a `vl redo`). | `undo_keeping_edits` (`workspace.rs:413`) restores the parent of the newest non-snapshot operation. After an undo, that operation is the undo itself, whose parent is the state it undid. Nothing skips earlier undos (jj's `undo` tracks what was already undone). Either implement "further back" or document undo-as-toggle. | `conformance_history::rewrite::repeated_undo_walks_back_through_rewrites` (control: `op_restore_walks_back_several_rewrites`) |
| BUG-249 | medium (**fixed**, audience wave; feature **removed**, format 8) | embargo / encryption at rest | **Fixed:** for an embargoed merge (and a plain merge into a line under an unreleased embargo), normalisation defers declassification (`NormalizeOptions::defer_declassify`: scanned and reported, entry left sealed), and `embargo::seal_declassified` seals the content straight from the line's scope to the embargo key, with the pre-merge version as fallback. No plaintext is written. Originally: `vl merge fix@ --into main --embargo`, with the fix developed in a cloaked workspace as the guide says, writes the merged file's **plaintext** to `.veil/objects` as a public blob. Nothing references it (`vl fsck` reports "2 unreachable object(s) … harmless"), so it is not pushed, but it stays on disk until `vl gc` prunes it after the grace period. Before the merge, the fix existed only sealed (control test). Copies of `.veil` (backups, a synced folder, a disk image) then hold the embargoed fix unencrypted. | `Repo::merge` (`commands.rs:417`): `normalize_tree` declassifies the cloaked line's files (the "published from the cloaked line" step) and stores them as public blobs. `apply_embargo` (`commands.rs:452`) then seals them, which leaves the public blobs written but unused. For an embargoed merge, the declassified content should be sealed under the embargo key directly. | `conformance_history::veil_angles::embargoed_merge_stores_no_plaintext_of_the_fix` (controls: `cloaked_fix_line_is_encrypted_at_rest_before_the_merge`, `embargo_fixture_hides_the_fix_from_outsiders`) |
| BUG-240 | low (**fixed**, merge wave) | merge / graph invariants | **Fixed:** `Transaction::new_change` and `vl rebase` record each parent once; `vl merge X --into X` is refused ("nothing to merge: … is the change being merged into"). `vl merge main --into main` and `vl new X X` (with work in progress in `@`) record a snapshot whose parent list holds the same snapshot twice. `vl show` lists `Parent :` twice (Git export with such a parent list was not checked). The duplicate disappears only if a later transaction happens to auto-rebase the snapshot. The same happens transiently to `vl merge X` where `X` is the destination; then `Merged <id>` names a snapshot id that the auto-rebase replaced in the same command. `vl rebase -d X -d X` is fine (control). | `Repo::merge` (`commands.rs:371`) and `Transaction::new_change` take the parent list as given. Only `rebase_descendants` deduplicates, and only for snapshots it rewrites. Refuse or deduplicate repeated parents when a snapshot is created. | `conformance_history::merge::merging_a_snapshot_into_itself_records_no_duplicate_parent`, `…::new_with_a_repeated_parent_records_one_parent` (control: `rewrite::rebase_with_a_repeated_destination_records_one_parent`) |
| BUG-246 | low (**fixed**, merge wave) | rewrite | **Fixed:** `Transaction::rewrite` compares without predecessors, timestamp and signature; `vl rebase` onto the current parents returns at once; `vl describe` with the current message (sealed for the same audience) changes nothing and records no operation. Rewrites that change nothing still rewrite. `vl rebase -s b -d a` where `a` is already `b`'s parent, and `vl describe -r b -m <the same message>`, create new snapshots of `b` and of every descendant, each re-signed by the current user ("Rebasing someone else's change: … the signature becomes ours"). They record an operation and add a version to `vl evolog`. jj reports "Nothing changed" / skips the rebase. | `Repo::rebase` and `Repo::describe` (`commands.rs:270`, `commands.rs:165`) set `predecessors = [old]` before `Transaction::rewrite`, whose "content identical to `old`" check (`transaction.rs:135`) compares the unsigned snapshot including predecessors, so it never matches. Compare without predecessors (and timestamp) instead. | `conformance_history::rewrite::rebase_onto_the_current_parent_is_a_no_op`, `…::describe_with_the_same_message_is_a_no_op` |
| BUG-241 | low (**fixed**, CLI wave) | diff | **Fixed:** the diff prints `old mode 100644` / `new mode 100755` (and `new file mode` / `deleted file mode` for executables), as Git does. `vl diff` and `vl show` print only `diff --veil a/run.sh b/run.sh` for a `chmod +x`: no line says that the executable bit changed. `--stat` lists `M run.sh`, and the bit is recorded (control), but the full diff is indistinguishable from an empty change. Git prints `old mode 100644` / `new mode 100755`; jj prints a mode-change line. | `print_diff` (`vl/src/render.rs:653`) labels additions, deletions and visibility changes (`render.rs:684`) but never compares `executable`. | `conformance_history::diff::mode_only_change_is_described_in_the_diff` (controls: `mode_only_change_is_listed_by_stat`, `removing_the_executable_bit_is_recorded`) |
| BUG-245 | low (**fixed**, CLI wave) | show | **Fixed:** `vl show` prints a `Conflicts :` line listing the recorded conflicts. `vl show REV` of a snapshot with recorded conflicts never mentions them: no `(conflict)` in the header and no list of paths. `vl log` marks the same snapshot `×`/`(conflict)`, and `vl status` lists conflicts only for `@` and its parents. The diff of a conflicted merge is also empty (relative to the auto-merged parents, as in jj), so `show` gives no hint at all. | `cmd_show` (`vl/src/main.rs:827`) prints change, snapshot, author, signature, parents, tags, cloak and embargo, but not `Snapshot::conflicts`. | `conformance_history::diff::show_of_a_conflicted_merge_names_the_conflicts` |
| BUG-242 | low (**fixed**, CLI wave) | diff / CLI | **Fixed:** `-r` conflicts with `--from` / `--to` (clap), as in jj. `vl diff -r A --from B` silently ignores `-r` and diffs `B` against `@`. jj refuses the combination; a user who means "the diff of A" gets something else without a warning. | `Cmd::Diff` (`vl/src/main.rs:147`) uses `rev` only when neither `--from` nor `--to` is given; clap does not declare the conflict. | `conformance_history::diff::diff_rev_with_from_is_refused` |
| BUG-248 | low (**fixed**, CLI wave) | remote tags / revisions | **Fixed:** `vl push` records `origin/<tag>` for every tag the remote holds at the pushed target (`remote::record_pushed_tags`, one local `push to <remote>` operation), as Git and jj update remote-tracking refs on push. `vl undo` of it says the remote keeps what was pushed. The guide defines `origin/main` as "where `main` was on remote `origin` at the last pull or push" and says it is "updated by `vl pull`/`vl push`". After `vl push origin -t main`, `origin/main` does not resolve (`revision 'origin/main' not found`) and `vl log` shows no remote tag until the next `vl pull`. | Only `pull_with` records remote tags (`remote.rs:1265`); `push_with` never updates `view.remote_tags`. | `conformance_history::revisions::remote_tags_are_recorded_by_push` (control: `remote_tags_resolve_after_a_pull`) |
| BUG-250 | low (**fixed**, merge wave) | docs | **Fixed:** the guide now says squashing a resolution into the merge clears the mark for the files it edited (behaviour kept, as ARCHITECTURE §5 describes). The getting-started guide ("Conflicts") says: "Resolving in `@` and then running `vl squash` gives the right content but leaves the merge marked `(conflict)` in `vl log`." `vl squash` clears the mark (squash keeps a conflict only when the squashed change left the file untouched, ARCHITECTURE §5), and `vl status`'s own hint recommends "Fold the resolution into the merge with `vl squash`". | Stale text at `docs/guide/getting-started.md:329`. | `conformance_history::log::guide_describes_squashing_a_resolution_correctly` (behaviour: `merge::squashing_a_resolution_clears_the_conflict`) |
