## Conformance findings: working copy and everyday commands

Found by the conformance pass that ports Git's and Jujutsu's working-copy
tests to Veil (Git t0xxx basics, t15xx revisions, t2xxx checkout, restore
and worktrees, t3xxx ls-files, branch, add/rm/mv and stash, t7xxx commit,
status, reset, clean and tag; jj's status, commit, new, describe, edit,
restore, workspace, working-copy, file, tag, bookmark and operation tests),
against `integration` @ `8dbdb61`. Each reproduction is an
`#[ignore = "BUG-2NN: …"]` test in `crates/vl/tests/cli_conformance/conformance_worktree/`;
`cargo test -p vl --test cli_conformance -- --ignored conformance_worktree::` runs them
(every one fails). Coverage is in `docs/conformance/worktree.md`. Line
numbers refer to `8dbdb61`.

| Severity | Count | IDs |
|---|---|---|
| high | 1 | 200 |
| medium | 5 | 201, 202, 206, 207, 208 |
| low | 5 | 203, 204, 205, 209, 210 |

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-200 | high (**fixed**, working-copy wave 2) | builder / nested repositories | **Fixed:** A directory holding a `.veil` directory (or a symlink named `.veil`) is a nested repository: the builder leaves it out as a whole (`vfs::holds_repository`, `BuildReport::nested`), whatever the outer policy says, and `vl status` lists it once (`N inner/`). Every walker goes through it: snapshots, `vl watch`, `ws exec` sandboxes, the Git import (a Git subdirectory holding `.veil/` is left out too); `vl scan` and `vl policy check` skip it and say so; checkout and `vl restore` never write or remove anything inside one (`CheckoutReport::nested`). Tests: `init::a_nested_repositorys_plaintext_never_leaves_the_outer_one` (objects, push and clone, Git export), `init::checkout_never_touches_a_nested_repository`, `git.rs::a_nested_veil_repository_in_a_git_commit_is_not_imported`. Originally: A Veil repository inside another one (a vendored project, a monorepo member, or `vl init` run by mistake in `~/projects`) is recorded file by file by the enclosing repository: only the inner `.veil/` is skipped. The inner repository's **cloaked** files are plaintext on disk, and the outer policy does not cloak them, so the outer repository records them as `public` (`public 30 inner/secrets/plan.txt`), and the next push sends them to every remote and to Git. The same holds for the inner repository's own `isolate` and `ignore` rules (only the built-in secret names stay protected). Git records an embedded repository as a gitlink, never its files. | `builder.rs:442` (`is_reserved_name(&child.name)` → `continue` drops `.veil` alone; the directory that contains it is walked like any other). | `conformance_worktree`: `init::outer_repository_does_not_record_a_nested_repositorys_cloaked_files` (control: `init::nested_repository_is_independent_of_the_outer_one`) |
| BUG-201 | medium (**fixed**, working-copy wave 2) | `vl restore` / working copy | **Fixed:** `vl restore` is a checkout of the selected paths (`checkout::restore_paths`): removals before writes, nothing in the way deleted, the same obstruction rules and reports. Originally: `vl restore` across a file ↔ directory swap fails half way. Directory in the source, file on disk (`f` vs `f/g.txt`): it writes `f/g.txt` before removing `f`, and fails with `File exists`. File in the source, directory on disk: it deletes `f/g.txt`, then fails to write `f` (`Is a directory`), so the directory's files are gone from disk and every retry fails the same way; only `rm -r f` by hand gets out. Checkout handles both orders. | `main.rs:874–901` (`cmd_restore`: one `fs.write`/`fs.remove_file` per diff entry, in path order, with no removal of a file or directory in the way). | `conformance_worktree`: `restore::restore_a_directory_over_a_file`, `restore::restore_a_file_over_a_directory` (control: `checkout::file_to_directory_and_back_on_checkout`) |
| BUG-202 | medium (**fixed**, working-copy wave 2) | checkout / file names | **Fixed:** `DiskBackend`'s temp name keeps only the first 32 bytes of the file's name, so any legal name has a temp name that fits. A path the filesystem still refuses (a name over 255 bytes from a remote, a character it cannot hold) is reported per file (`CheckoutReport::failed`), left out and recorded as such (`Materialised::hidden`), so the next snapshot carries its entry and the working copy never wedges; a full or read-only filesystem still stops the checkout. Tests: also `checkout_obstacles.rs::a_name_the_filesystem_refuses_is_reported_and_the_checkout_goes_on`. Originally: A file whose name is 241–255 bytes long (legal on ext4, APFS, btrfs, XFS) is recorded, but can never be written back: every write goes through a temporary `.<name>.vltmpXXXXXXXX`, 15 bytes longer, which fails with `ENAMETOOLONG`. `vl new`/`vl edit` of such a snapshot fails after the view moved; every later command finds the working copy stale, saves another `recovered working-copy edits` change, retries the checkout and fails again, until `vl --no-snapshot undo`. `vl restore` of the file fails the same way. | `vfs/disk.rs:177` (`temp_path`: `format!(".{}.vltmp{}", name, 8 hex)` with no length check or fallback name). | `conformance_worktree`: `filesystem::a_name_of_250_bytes_round_trips`, `filesystem::restore_of_a_250_byte_name` (control: `filesystem::a_name_of_240_bytes_round_trips`) |
| BUG-206 | medium (**fixed**, CLI wave; duplicate of BUG-247) | undo / docs | **Fixed** with BUG-247. The guide says "`vl undo` undoes the last operation. Run it again to go further back." A second `vl undo` undoes the first one instead (`Undid: undo: commit change …`): repeated undos toggle between two states. A user stepping back over two mistakes (a bad rebase, then a bad commit) re-applies the first one and continues on the state they meant to leave. `vl op restore` works. | `workspace.rs:413` (`undo_keeping_edits`: the newest non-snapshot operation is undone, and an undo is such an operation); `docs/guide/getting-started.md:270`. | `conformance_worktree`: `undo::undo_again_goes_further_back` (control: `undo::undo_of_a_commit_reopens_the_change`) |
| BUG-207 | medium (**fixed**, CLI wave) | revsets / recovery | **Fixed:** after the visible snapshots, a snapshot-id prefix is matched against the hidden snapshots in the store that the op log reaches (through parents and predecessors), so every id `vl evolog` prints resolves (`revset::recorded_snapshots_with_prefix`). Change ids still name the visible version. The guide: "Because every command snapshots, a change gets a new snapshot id each time … `vl evolog` shows the versions … Use snapshot ids when you mean one exact version (to compare with `vl diff --from` …)". The ids `vl evolog` prints for earlier versions do not resolve (`error: revision '0d503b6f' not found`) in `vl show`, `vl cat -r`, `vl diff --from` or `vl restore --from`, because prefixes are only looked up among visible snapshots, and nothing prints the full 64-hex id of a hidden one. Getting back a file as it was two snapshots ago needs `vl op restore`. | `revset.rs:62–96` (`resolve_base`: change-id and snapshot-id prefixes are matched against `repo.visible_snapshots()` only; only a full 64-hex id reaches hidden snapshots). | `conformance_worktree`: `revisions::evolog_snapshot_ids_resolve_as_revisions` (control: `revisions::snapshot_id_prefixes_need_four_hex_digits`) |
| BUG-208 | medium (**fixed**, working-copy wave 2) | `vl restore` / merges | **Fixed:** `--from` now defaults to what `vl status` compares with: the parent of `@`, or for a merge its merged parents (`parents_tree`), as in jj. An explicit `--from REV` is unchanged. Originally: With a merge as the working copy (`vl new A B`), `vl status` compares with the merged parents, but `vl restore` (default `--from @-`) restores from the **first** parent alone: to drop a stray edit it deletes every file that came from `B` (`Restored 2 path(s)`, then `D b.txt`). A `vl commit` afterwards records a merge that silently discards `B`'s work. jj restores from the merged parents. | `cli.rs:596` (`--from` defaults to `@-`); `main.rs:875` (`cmd_restore` takes `snapshot(src).tree`, not `parents_tree(&s.parents)` as `cmd_status` does). | `conformance_worktree`: `restore::restore_in_a_merge_goes_back_to_the_merged_parents` (control: `status::status_of_a_merge_shows_both_parents`) |
| BUG-203 | low (**fixed**, working-copy wave 2) | policy / ignore | **Fixed:** Git's rule, documented: a snapshot never looks inside an ignored directory, and `vl policy check` now says so (`build/keep.txt: ignored via rule 'build/' (on its directory build/: …)`). The guide explains the exception to "the last matching rule wins" and how to re-include (`build/** ignore`). Originally: "The last matching rule wins": with `build/ ignore` then `build/keep.txt public`, `vl policy check build/keep.txt` says `public via rule 'build/keep.txt'`, but no snapshot ever records the file, because the walk never enters an ignored directory, and nothing reports it. (Git documents that a file cannot be re-included below an excluded directory; Veil documents the opposite.) The user believes the file is in history. | `builder.rs:457` (an `Ignored` directory is skipped before its entries are classified); `vl policy check` classifies the path alone. | `conformance_worktree`: `ignores::policy_check_and_the_snapshot_agree_below_an_ignored_directory` (control: `ignores::a_later_public_rule_re_includes_a_file`) |
| BUG-204 | low (**fixed**, working-copy wave 2) | path arguments | **Fixed:** `Ctx::repo_path` no longer rewrites `\` (an ordinary character on Linux and macOS), and refuses an absolute path, or a relative one climbing out with `..`, that is outside the repository (symlinked roots are resolved first). Relative paths given with `-R` from outside the repository are still relative to its root. Originally: `Ctx::repo_path` rewrites every `\` to `/` (a Windows habit; Veil 1.0 runs on Linux and macOS, where `\` is an ordinary name character) and turns an absolute path outside the repository into a path relative to its root. `vl cat 'back\slash'` prints `back/slash`; `vl restore 'back\slash'` discards the edit of `back/slash` and leaves the named file alone; `vl restore /etc/passwd` restores the repository's own `etc/passwd`. Recoverable with `vl undo`. | `ctx.rs:403–407` (`strip_prefix(root)` failure falls back to the argument as given; `.replace('\\', "/")`). | `conformance_worktree`: `filesystem::a_backslash_in_a_path_argument_names_that_file`, `restore::restore_of_an_absolute_path_outside_the_repository_is_refused` (control: `restore::restore_paths_relative_to_the_current_directory`) |
| BUG-205 | low (**fixed**, working-copy wave 2) | messages | **Fixed:** `vl cat` of a directory or of the root, and `vl ws cat W <directory>`, say "is a directory, not a file". (The test's last assertion was wrong: `vl fsck`'s own "No corruption found" contains "corrupt"; it now checks for that line.) Originally: `vl cat <directory>` (and `vl cat .`, `vl cat ''`, `vl ws cat W <directory>`) fails with `error: corrupt object <id>: expected blob, found tree`. The repository is fine (`vl fsck` agrees); the message sends the user hunting for corruption. | `main.rs:166–172` (`Cmd::Cat`: `read_file` on the entry whatever its kind; the store reports a kind mismatch as `Corrupt`); `WsCmd::Cat` likewise. | `conformance_worktree`: `files::cat_of_a_directory_is_a_clear_error` (control: `files::cat_of_a_missing_path_fails`) |
| BUG-209 | low (**fixed**, working-copy wave 2) | `vl restore` | **Fixed:** `vl restore` goes through checkout, which removes the directories it empties. Originally: Directories that `vl restore` empties stay on disk (`newdir/a/b/`), unlike a checkout, which removes them, and Git's `reset --hard` / `clean -d`. Empty directories are not recorded, so `vl status` shows nothing, but the tree on disk no longer matches the snapshot. | `main.rs:898` (`cmd_restore`: `ctx.fs.remove_file` only; no pruning of emptied parents as `checkout` does). | `conformance_worktree`: `restore::restore_removes_directories_it_empties`, `clean::restore_removes_the_new_directories_it_empties` (control: `filesystem::empty_directories_are_not_recorded`) |
| BUG-210 | low (**fixed**, audience wave; feature **removed**, format 8) | tags / scopes | **Fixed:** `set_tag` validates an explicit `--cloak` scope with `validate_scope_name` and requires membership, as `vl ws new --cloak` does. Originally: `vl tag set NAME --cloak SCOPE` accepts any scope string: one that does not exist or that you are not a member of (a typo: `--cloak tema`), reserved names (`local`, `embargo/x`), path-like and upper-case names, and the empty string. `vl cloak --scope` and `vl ws new --cloak` refuse a scope you hold no key for. The tag is withheld from public remotes as intended, but `vl commit` in a cloaked workspace only advances tags cloaked under *its* scope, so a mistyped tag never moves, silently. | `commands.rs:515` (`set_tag`: `validate_tag_name` only; the cloak scope is neither validated with `validate_scope_name` nor checked for membership); `main.rs:1128` (`TagCmd::Set`). | `conformance_worktree`: `tags::a_cloaked_tag_needs_a_valid_scope` (control: `tags::a_cloaked_tag_is_marked_in_the_list`) |

### Conformance (working copy) hypotheses and notes

* **Git-incompatible tag names.** `validate_tag_name` accepts `-x`, `.hidden`, `x.lock`, `trail/`, `HEAD` and `origin/main` (the last is also a remote-tag key; a local tag of that name shadows the remote one in revsets). Tags export to Git as branches, where all of these are invalid ref names (`git check-ref-format`). Not tested here (the Git bridge belongs to the interop conformance pass).
* **`vl ws new --cloak local`** is accepted (`local` is a reserved scope name for policy rules); what is recorded there was not examined.
* **`VEIL_FS_FOLDING=macos` on Linux** rehearses the checkout refusal for colliding names but cannot rehearse NFC recording of new names (a decomposed name is recorded as is); the macOS CI run is the only real test of `t3910`-style behaviour.
* **Checkout from inside a directory it removes** (`cd sub && vl new root`) deletes the current directory; Git keeps it (t2501). Harmless but surprising; not filed.

---

## Conformance findings: remotes and storage

Found by porting Git's t5xxx (clone, fetch, push, pull, remote), t1050,
t1450, t5304, t6500 and t9300/t9350, and Jujutsu's `test_git_*`,
`test_bookmark_command`, `test_concurrent_operations` and `test_gc`, to
Veil, against `integration` @ `8dbdb61`. Each reproduction is an
`#[ignore = "BUG-NNN: …"]` test in `crates/vl/tests/cli_conformance/conformance_sync/`;
`cargo test -p vl --test cli_conformance -- --ignored conformance_sync::` runs them (every one
fails). Coverage: `docs/conformance/sync.md`. Line numbers refer to
`8dbdb61`.

| Severity | Count | IDs |
|---|---|---|
| medium | 2 | 280, 288 |
| low | 8 | 281, 282, 283, 284, 286, 289, 290, 293 |

(BUG-285, 287, 291 and 292 were about the removed Git bridge; their rows are
gone.)

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-280 | medium (**fixed**, sync wave) | sync / tags | **Fixed:** a plain push still never deletes remotely (control test); `vl push --delete TAG` deletes a tag already deleted locally, leased (refused if the remote moved it to unseen work, unless `--force`). Pull prunes `REMOTE/TAG` for tags gone from the remote (the local tag is kept, with a warning). `vl tag delete` records the deletion in `.veil/local/deleted_tags.json` (no format change), and pull/`vl git pull` leave the tag deleted while the remote's tag has not moved past it; if it has, the tag comes back with a warning. The two reproductions that expected a plain `vl push`/`vl git push` to delete remotely now use `--delete` (a plain push deleting would contradict the control test). Docs: teams-and-remotes "Deleting a tag". A tag can never be deleted anywhere but locally, and the local deletion does not stick. `vl tag delete feature` then `vl push` leaves `feature` on the remote; the next `vl pull` recreates it (`updated feature → …`), and so does `vl git pull` for a tag the Git bridge exported (the Git branch is never deleted either). A teammate's `origin/feature` is never pruned. A tag pushed by mistake (an experiment, to the public mirror, to GitHub) stays published for good, and every member's cleanup is undone by their next pull. Git (`push --delete`, `fetch --prune`) and jj (`bookmark delete` + `git push --deleted`) both support this. | `remote.rs:988` (`push_with`): only local tags are selected, a missing local tag is never a deletion; `remote.rs:1266` (`pull_with`): a remote tag with no local tag is inserted (`None =>`), whether or not it was deleted here; `remote.rs:1265`: `remote_tags` are only ever inserted, never removed for tags gone from the remote. The Git bridge's `pull` has the same create-if-absent rule and pushes no delete refspecs. | `push_pull::{a_deleted_tag_stays_deleted_after_push_and_pull, pull_prunes_remote_tags_that_are_gone_from_the_remote}`, `git_bridge::a_deleted_tag_deletes_the_git_branch_or_at_least_stays_deleted` (control: `push_pull::deleting_a_local_tag_never_removes_history_from_the_remote`) |
| BUG-288 | medium (**fixed**, sync wave) | sync / storage | **Fixed:** `push_with` decides which tags the remote takes (under its lock, on the local graph) before transferring, and sends only the accepted tags' history. Changes the order inside `push_with` only; the remote-tag (BUG-248/281) part is untouched. A rejected push still sends the rejected tag's objects, as a new pack, every time it is retried: `Pushed 9 object(s)` on each attempt, one more pack per retry. The remote's committed view never references them (the tag was refused), so the incremental push cannot know the remote has them, and since a remote cannot be garbage-collected (BUG-284) the duplicates stay forever. A user retrying a rejected push in a loop, or CI pushing all tags while one is stale, grows the remote without bound. | `remote.rs:1011`–`1037` (`push_with`): the closure of every selected tag is sent before the fast-forward check at `remote.rs:1043` rejects some; `push_incremental` only skips what the remote's committed view reaches. | `push_pull::a_retried_rejected_push_sends_nothing_new` |
| BUG-281 | low (**fixed** with BUG-248, CLI wave; duplicate) | sync / remote tags | `vl push` does not update the local `REMOTE/TAG` remote tags. After a successful push `origin/main` still names the old remote state until the next pull; after `vl push --force` it still names the overwritten history, so `vl log` shows a remote state that no longer exists and `vl merge origin/main` (the documented recovery) would merge the overwritten work back. Git (t5404) and jj update tracking refs on push. | `remote.rs:963`–`1060`: `push_with` writes only the remote's view; nothing records what was pushed in the local view. | `push_pull::push_updates_the_remote_tags` (controls: `push_pull::a_rejected_push_leaves_the_remote_tag_alone`, `git_bridge::git_push_updates_the_remote_tags`) |
| BUG-282 | low (**fixed**, sync wave) | remotes | **Fixed:** `remote::remove_remote` drops `NAME/*` remote tags in one operation, then removes the remote from `repo.json`. `vl remote rm origin` keeps every `origin/*` remote tag. They keep showing in `vl tag list` and `vl log` and keep their history alive through `gc`, and a later `vl remote add origin <another repo>` mixes the old remote's tags with the new one's. Git's `remote rm` removes the tracking branches (t5505); `vl git remote rm` tries to (BUG-291). | `main.rs:1821` (`RemoteCmd::Rm`): only `repo.json` is edited; `view.remote_tags` is untouched. | `remotes::remote_rm_drops_its_remote_tags` |
| BUG-283 | low (**fixed**, sync wave) | sync / push | **Fixed:** `open_or_init_remote` refuses a directory that holds anything but a leftover `.veil/` and is not a repository. `vl push` to an existing, non-empty directory that is not a Veil repository silently turns it into one: a typo such as `vl remote add origin ../proj` (for `../proj.veil`) puts a bare `.veil/` inside another project, after which every `vl` command run there fails with `not found: workspace 'default'`. The guide's "the first `vl push` creates it" is about a remote that does not exist yet; Git refuses to push to a non-repository. | `remote.rs:982`–`986` (`push_with`): `is_repo` false → `Repo::init_bare`, without checking that the directory is absent or empty. | `push_pull::push_to_a_non_empty_directory_that_is_not_a_repository_is_refused` (control: `push_pull::the_first_push_creates_the_remote_including_parent_directories`) |
| BUG-284 | low (**fixed**, sync wave) | maintenance / remotes | **Fixed:** `Ctx::open` opens a repository without workspaces as bare: `log`, `show`, `tag list`, `op log` and `gc` run (no snapshot, no checkout, no embargo auto-release), other commands are refused with a clear message. `vl -R <bare remote>` works for `fsck` only. `gc`, `log`, `tag list` and `op log` on a path remote fail with `not found: workspace 'default'`, so a team remote can never be garbage-collected (force-pushed history, rejected pushes (BUG-288) and pruned experiments accumulate) or inspected. `git gc` and `git log` work in a bare repository. | `ctx.rs:166`: `Ctx::open` needs a workspace (`repo.workspace_wc(DEFAULT_WORKSPACE)?`) even for commands that never touch a working copy; bare repositories have none. | `push_pull::read_only_commands_and_gc_work_on_a_bare_remote` |
| BUG-286 | low (**fixed**, sync wave) | remotes | **Fixed:** `remote::validate_remote_name` is checked by `vl remote add`. Remote names are not validated: `vl remote add '' …`, `'has space'`, `origin/main`, `../evil` and `-x` are all accepted. `origin/main` then makes `origin/main/<tag>` remote tags that collide with `origin`'s `main/<tag>` ones; an empty name gives `/main`. First noted, unnumbered, in the round-6 hypotheses. | `main.rs:1789` (`RemoteCmd::Add`): no `validate_*_name` call for the remote name. | `remotes::remote_add_refuses_invalid_names` |
| BUG-289 | low (**fixed**, sync wave) | fsck | **Fixed:** `fsck` verifies and counts every packed object the walk did not reach, and reports packs that cannot be opened (as a warning: a reachable object they held is reported missing anyway). Once `gc` has packed them, unreachable objects are invisible to `fsck`: after `vl abandon` and `vl gc --keep-ops-days 0` (which reports `6 pending deletion`), `fsck` says `0 unreachable` and does not verify them. Git's `fsck` reports dangling objects and verifies every pack. Harmless for history (nothing references them), but `fsck`'s summary is wrong, and damage to a pack's unreachable entries goes unseen until `gc` trips over it. | `fsck.rs:747`–`790` (`scan_loose_objects`) counts and verifies only loose files; packed objects are reached only through reachable references, and `ObjectStore::verify_packs` (`store.rs:580`) is never called by `fsck`. | `integrity::fsck_counts_unreachable_objects_after_gc_packed_them` |
| BUG-290 | low (**fixed**, sync wave) | storage / gc | **Fixed:** above 2 MiB of base plus object, deltas use the base as a zstd prefix with long-distance matching and a window sized to it (capped at 2^27, readable by every decoder; `zstd --patch-from`). Measured (debug build, 64-byte edit, after `gc --prune-now`): 4 MiB 8.39 → 4.21 MB, 16 MiB 33.6 → 16.8 MB, 48 MiB 100.7 → 50.4 MB; peak RSS 55 → 48, 142 → 113, 371 → 280 MB (the pack buffer shrinks; the long-distance table adds a few MiB per delta). `gc`'s delta compression stops working above a few MiB: two versions of a 4 MiB file that differ in 64 bytes take 8.4 MB after `vl gc` (two full copies); at 1 MiB the second version is stored as a delta. Every version of a large asset is stored whole, against the guide's "with deltas between versions of a file". | `pack.rs:625`: the delta is `zstd::bulk::Compressor::with_dictionary(level, base)` at the pack level (default 3); zstd's match window at that level is far smaller than a multi-MiB dictionary, so matches against the start of the base are out of reach and the delta is no smaller than plain compression. A window/long-distance setting sized to the base (or a real binary delta) is needed. | `large::gc_stores_a_small_edit_of_a_four_mib_file_as_a_delta` (control: `large::gc_stores_a_small_edit_of_a_one_mib_file_as_a_delta`) |
| BUG-293 | low (**fixed**, sync wave) | messages / SFTP | **Fixed:** `OpStore::get` maps only a missing file to `NotFound`; other I/O errors propagate. An I/O error while reading an operation is reported as missing data. An SFTP server that stops answering mid-push ends the command with `error: not found: operation 3fd44253` instead of the timeout (`SFTP connection lost: no reply from the SFTP server for …`); the same happens for EIO or a permission error on a local `.veil/ops/` file. The user is sent looking for a corrupted remote rather than at the network. | `oplog.rs:105`–`110` (`OpStore::get`): `.map_err(\|_\| VeilError::NotFound(…))` replaces every error, including `ConnectionAborted` from `sftp_client.rs:451`–`460`. | `sftp::a_server_that_stops_answering_is_reported_as_a_lost_connection` |

### Conformance notes: remotes and storage

* **Deliberate differences** (documented; tested as such, not reported):
  no atomic push (other tags still go, exit 1); `vl push --force` has no
  lease; a clone of `x.veil` keeps the
  name `x.veil` (Git strips `.git`); tag names are ASCII (Git allows
  UTF-8 refs); `file://`, `http(s)://` and `git://` Veil remotes are
  refused (Veil remotes are paths or SSH); pull creates local tags for new
  remote tags (Git's fetch does not create branches); pushes into a
  repository with a working copy move tags only (Git refuses
  `receive.denyCurrentBranch`).
* **Known, not re-reported:** concurrent first pushes to an empty remote
  (`docs/BACKLOG.md`); the conformance test retries the loser and passes.
  Tilde paths over SSH (BUG-118).
* **Limitations seen:** a push never deltas against what the remote
  already holds (a 64-byte edit of a 4 MiB file re-sends 4 MiB);
  `docs/BACKLOG.md` (chunking) covers it. The stand-in `ssh` cannot
  simulate a dropped TCP connection exactly: cutting the server's output
  leaves the server process alive, which `vl` sees as a server that
  stopped answering (the documented 300 s timeout, shortened in the test).

## Findings from the local-tooling round

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-360 | low (**fixed**, BUG-128 fix) | identity | **Fixed:** `load_identity` stores a first identity with `ctx::create_identity`: the synced `0600` temp file is hard-linked into place, which fails if another process got there first (on a filesystem without hard links, an exclusive create claims the name and the content follows by rename; readers wait out the empty file). The losers load the winner's identity, and only the winner prints "Created new identity". `vl identity new` (without `--force`) uses the same, so it never replaces an identity created in the meantime. Originally: several `vl` processes starting at once on a fresh `VEIL_HOME` each generated and stored their own identity (rename over), and only the last survived; 12 concurrent runs gave 8–16 distinct identities, so the others had signed and been granted under keys that were gone. Found by the serve agent. | `ctx.rs` (`load_identity`): check `exists()`, then generate and `write_identity` (rename over the file). | `identity_cli`: `concurrent_first_runs_create_one_identity`; `serve_cli`: the original reproduction (un-ignored) |
| BUG-400 | low (**fixed**, feature 9) | scanner | **Fixed** (scanner v6): every chunk's window starts at the start of a line. Originally: where a 256 KiB chunk of the assignment pass started inside a line of code, the rest of that line counted as a configuration line, so `    client = Client(…, auth_token=access_token)` cut after `a` reported `access_token)` as a credential (the file was quarantined). Depends only on where the chunk boundary falls, so it came and went as a file grew. | `secrets.rs` (`scan`): the window began at the chunk start, and `config_line` takes the start of the window for the start of a line (`None if from == 0`). | `scanner`: `a_chunk_boundary_inside_a_code_line_is_not_a_configuration_line` |
| BUG-361 | medium (**fixed**, `vl serve` round) | working copy / concurrency | **Fixed:** `Ctx::record_once` first asks whether the directory already holds every change from the snapshot it was recorded at to the new `@` (`Ctx::directory_holds`, a three-way check of the directory's tree); if so it adopts `@` and records the edits into it. A real move (undo, rebase, another workspace) fails the check and is caught up with as before. A `vl` command that runs while another one is finishing `vl commit` or `vl new` in the same directory (after the transaction moved `@`, before its checkout rewrote `checkout.json`) declares the working copy stale, saves the user's edits made since as a side change "recovered working-copy edits", and **removes them from disk** (checkout to the new `@`). Nothing was stale: the new `@` has the very tree the directory holds. Found by `serve_cli`'s concurrent test (an editor's server and a terminal `vl commit`), but two terminals hit it too. No content is lost (it is in the side change, with a warning), but the files vanish from the working copy. | `ctx.rs` `record_once`: `stale` compares snapshot ids only (`checkout.snapshot != view_wc`), and the recovery path saves any difference from the old snapshot's tree as a side change before checking out the target. | `serve_cli::bug_361_edits_survive_a_concurrent_commit` |
| BUG-362 | medium (**fixed**, `vl serve` round) | working copy / concurrency | **Fixed:** `Ctx::record` re-reads the checkout record with the view (`Ctx::refresh`, then finishes an interrupted move) before it tries again, and the BUG-361 check also covers a directory another command has just *recorded* (the tree changed, and every change is already on disk). When another process records the working copy (or commits) while a command is snapshotting, the command's snapshot is superseded and it tries again, but against the view only: it still believes the directory holds the snapshot it saw at start-up. The new `@` then looks like a move it has to catch up with, so it saves the directory's state as a "recovered working-copy edits" side change and checks out `@`, **removing from disk** files written after the other process's snapshot. In `serve_cli`'s concurrent test (4 server clients and a CLI committing, 30 commits) 10 of 30 new files ended up only in side changes, nearly every run. No content is lost (it is in the side changes), but files vanish from the working copy, and `vl watch` next to a terminal hits the same window. | `ctx.rs` `record`: the retry after `out.superseded` called `self.repo.reload()` (view) but kept the `CheckoutState` loaded by `Ctx::open`, so `record_once`'s `stale` test compared the new `@` with an outdated `checkout.snapshot`. | `serve_cli::bug_362_edits_survive_a_concurrent_snapshot` (with a control: a real move is still recovered), `serve_cli::concurrent_clients_and_cli_writes_stay_correct` (failed before the fix in most runs) |
| BUG-300 | low (**fixed**, sparse sandboxes) | stat cache / performance | **Fixed:** the builder's "unchanged cloaked content keeps its sealing" path now learns the file it verified (content id, and the scope and sealed object it keeps), so the next snapshot takes it from the stat cache. Originally: once a checkout has written a cloaked file (a clone, `vl ws switch`, every `ws exec` sandbox sync), every later snapshot reads and decrypts it again: the stat cache never learns it back. A working copy with many cloaked files pays for all of them on every command, and `ws exec` cannot vouch for them either (it must leave cloaked files to the builder's sealing check, BUG-103). | `builder.rs:1390`–`1401` ("Unchanged cloaked content keeps its sealing"): the early `return Ok(Some(pe.clone()))` after `matches_encoding` succeeds records no `learn(…, sealed: Some((scope, pe.id)))`, unlike the unchanged-public path at `builder.rs:1377`. | `veil-core/tests/core_engine/sandbox.rs::unchanged_cloaked_files_are_cached_after_a_checkout` |
| BUG-680 | low (**fixed**, Git-parity stream R) | CLI / terminal output | **Fixed:** `vl merge` prints its warnings through `warn_untrusted` (escaped as a whole). Originally `cmd_merge` printed `MergeResult::warnings` with `warn`, unescaped, and those warnings quote paths from the merged trees (a refused `.veilpolicy` re-key lists the files it would move, `kept_sealed` names files whose audience the merge kept), so a teammate's file named with a terminal escape (`secrets/x\x1b[31m…`, an OSC 52 clipboard write) reached the terminal raw when the merge refused to move it. Found while adding the rename-aware merge's own warnings, which quote paths too. | `main.rs` `cmd_merge`: `for w in &res.warnings { warn(w) }`; `render::warn` does not escape. | `duplicate_cli::merge_warnings_quoting_hostile_paths_are_escaped` (failed before the fix), `duplicate_cli::hostile_names_in_rename_reports_are_escaped` |
| BUG-681 | medium (**fixed**, Git-parity fix wave 1) | embargo / declassification | **Fixed:** one rule for every route that copies content from one revision into another (`veil_core::confinement`): a snapshot is *confined* to an unreleased embargo when it is its withheld history, builds on it (what a push refuses), or sits under its wrapper (the embargoed line); content of a confined snapshot is copied only into a change confined to the same embargo. `vl restore --from` (with and without paths) checks it against `@`; `vl squash` (`--into`, partial or whole, and into the fix's own public parent) against the destination; `vl duplicate`/`cherry-pick` now also refuse work *built on* the fix, whose edits of the fix's files were copied out as conflicts (destinations must still be on the fix line); `Transaction::commit` refuses any rewrite that moves a confined change onto parents outside its embargo (`vl rebase -r/-s` of work on the fix, `vl abandon` of the fix under such work; moving the fix itself is fine, the embargoed merge follows it); `vl resolve` only picks versions already in the change and `vl cat` writes nothing; `vl split` keeps both halves where the change was. Refusals name the change and the embargo, never a file or description. Found, beyond the reported route, by this fix: `restore --from` the embargoed merge (published the fix's names), `rebase -r <work on the fix> -d <public>` (names and plaintext content as a conflict, then `vl resolve` made it an edit), `duplicate` of such work, `squash --into`. `vl restore --from <fix> PATH` on a line outside an unreleased embargo copies a file of the withheld fix as an ordinary edit of that line: public plaintext when the fix was not cloaked (sealed for its scope when it was, which still publishes its name and size). The next push to a public remote publishes the fix's file name and content before the release, although the fix itself is withheld and the threat model promises the embargoed tree is sealed whole. `vl duplicate`/`cherry-pick` refuse the same copy (design 1, threat review 11, whose adversarial note flagged this route). | `main.rs` `cmd_restore` / `checkout::restore_paths`: the source revision is not checked against `remote::embargo_withheld`; the restored entry is recorded by the builder under the destination line's policy. Suggested fix: refuse a `--from` revision an unreleased embargo withholds unless `@` is inside that embargo (the rule `Repo::duplicate` applies), or keep restored entries sealed under the embargo key. | `duplicate_cli::restoring_from_an_embargoed_fix_onto_another_line_publishes_nothing`; `embargo_copy_cli` (one test per route) |
| BUG-682 | high (**fixed**, Git-parity fix wave 2; feature **removed**, format 8) | squash / cloaked lines / push | **Fixed:** the leak was the sealed version's *fallback* (what readers without the key see), not its content. Squashing the draft into the cloaked change merged it into a version of `a.txt` that change already sealed; the merge (`merged_visibility`) gives a sealed result the other side's public version as fallback ("the newest public version"), here the draft's plaintext blob, and `embargo::seal_introduced` kept already-sealed entries as they were, fallback included, so the push of the cloaked change sent the draft. `seal_introduced` now lets a version sealed under the line's scope fall back only to what the line's base already shows readers without the key at that path (`publicly_seen`); any other fallback becomes the base's public version (`public_fallback`). The same rule covers every route that folds content into a cloaked change (squash, rebase onto it, `vl duplicate` and `vl cherry-pick`), so it also fixes **BUG-707** (the same fallback through `vl duplicate`, found by adversarial pass A in round 9). Pinned by `adversarial7::rewrites::a_draft_squashed_into_a_sealed_edit_publishes_nothing_sealed`, `adversarial7::fallbacks` (squash, rebase then squash, duplicate, cherry-pick, and a split of what the cloaked change took in; each fails without the fix) and `split_squash::a_draft_squashed_into_a_sealed_edit_never_becomes_its_fallback` (decoded packs searched with `backend_contains`). A public edit in `@` that was never pushed, squashed into a cloaked change (`vl squash -r @ --into sec@`), reaches the public mirror as plaintext with the next `vl push` of that cloaked change. Present before the Git-parity round (fails the same at `c935cc9`). Found at random by `adversarial7::rewrites::no_rewrite_publishes_sealed_content` during Git-parity fix wave 1. | unknown: the sealing of what the cloaked destination takes in on squash, or the objects the push sends. | `adversarial7/rewrites.rs` sequence `[Draft(0,true), Draft(0,false), SquashIntoSealed(false,None), SquashIntoSealed(true,None)]` (to be pinned as an ignored test) |

# Round 7 findings (Opus, local-tooling features)

Found by the seventh adversarial pass against `integration` @ `c5c6fe4`,
on the features merged in the local-tooling round: `--json` and the API
layer, `vl ls` and `vl push --dry-run`, scanner v6, the WASM / no-`os`
build, first-class conflicts (format 4), `vl serve --local`, and `vl split`
/ interactive `vl squash`. Each reproduction is an
`#[ignore = "BUG-NNN: …"]` test in `crates/vl/tests/cli_adversarial/adversarial7/` (the
real binary) or `crates/veil-core/tests/core_security/adversarial7_core/` (the engine);
`cargo test -p vl --test cli_adversarial -- --ignored adversarial7::` and
`cargo test -p veil-core --test core_security -- --ignored adversarial7_core::` run them
(every one fails; the controls next to them pass). Line numbers refer to
`c5c6fe4`. BUG-490 and BUG-491 were found by the round-7 performance fix
wave (BUG-490 is the pack hypothesis below, demonstrated).

## Round 7 findings

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-480 | high (**fixed**, round-7 sealing wave; feature **removed**, format 8) | squash / conflicts / cloaking | **Fixed:** `seal_introduced` now seals an introduced conflict entry's public versions (`embargo::seal_conflict_terms`): each version (add or remove) whose plaintext readers without the key could not already see at that path in the base (a public entry, a sealed entry's fallback, a public version of the base's conflict) is sealed for the line's scope; a forged public label gets its real one; the conflict keeps its shape. Applies to squash into a cloaked change and to rebases of cloaked lines (`Repo::rebase_tree`). `vl squash` into a cloaked change ("seals what it brings for that change's scope", `--help`) leaves a **conflict entry** it brings exactly as it was: its public side, the very work being moved, stays plaintext in the cloaked change. Pushing that change to a public remote sends the conflict object and the public side, and a mirror reader's checkout shows it. The same squash of a plain file seals it (control). Reached by an ordinary flow: a public change rebased onto the cloaked workspace's change (which conflicts), then squashed into it. | `commands.rs:540` seals what the destination receives with `embargo::seal_introduced`, which skips conflict entries (`embargo.rs:953`, `EntryKind::File \| EntryKind::Conflict => {}`, "none is public content this line introduces"). Each *term* of an introduced conflict that is public and not already a term of the destination's base should be sealed (as the builder seals the sides of an edited conflict, `builder.rs:1288`). `Repo::rebase_tree` (`repo.rs:644`) uses the same function for cloaked lines. | `adversarial7`: `conflicts::squash_into_a_cloaked_change_seals_the_public_side_of_a_conflict_it_brings` (control: `squash_into_a_cloaked_change_seals_a_public_edit`); property: `adversarial7`: `rewrites::no_rewrite_publishes_sealed_content` and its fixed flows |

## Round 7 hypotheses (not demonstrated)

* **Idle `vl serve` clients hold every slot forever**: demonstrated and fixed as BUG-495 (round-7 serve wave).
* **Decompression bomb in a pack from a hostile remote** (both decoders, pre-existing). A pack entry declares `raw_len`, and nothing bounds it: a few KB of zstd RLE blocks declaring 8 GiB make `Pack::get` allocate it (`pack.rs:526-531` libzstd streaming, `pack.rs:607-622` ruzstd) before the hash check. Not run: it would exhaust this machine's memory.
* **Idle `vl serve` clients hold every slot forever** (same-user DoS, low). There is a 60 s write timeout but no read or idle timeout (`serve.rs:471`, `serve_client` at `:568`): 32 connections that never send (or send half a line) make every other client, the user's editor, get `-32001 too many clients` until the server restarts. Reproduced by hand (a `python3` client: still refused after any wait); not made a test, as a correct server would only free a slot after an idle timeout of minutes.
* **Decompression bomb in a pack from a hostile remote**: demonstrated safely and fixed as BUG-490.
* **WASM locks on a networked backend with no clock installed.** A no-`os` holder writes `renewed: 0` and has no heartbeat; a native contender takes the lock over at once, and the holder's self-fencing never fires (`clock::monotonic()` stuck at 0). The documented skew analysis predicts a failed write on the WASM side, with the same residual window. Not testable in the native suite (no-`os` is compile-time).
* **Scanner windows inside a > 2 KiB line.** `line_start` (`secrets.rs:2014`, `LINE_LOOKBACK` 2048) gives up after 2 KiB, so a 256 KiB chunk boundary inside a longer line still starts mid-line (the BUG-400 condition); only a v6 key with a 256+ character unquoted value could flip. A shift harness over minified bundles found nothing.
* **Positional hunk numbers in a plan go stale.** A `--plan` or `--hunks` names hunks by number, and `vl split` first snapshots the working copy: an edit made after the script read `vl diff --json` renumbers the hunks and the plan takes different ones, silently. Nothing is lost or re-audienced (both changes keep everything), so it is a correctness note; a plan could carry the snapshot id it was made from.
* **A conflict object with 64 removes** (the maximum) makes every merge or rebase that touches it fail with "more than 64 removes" (`Conflict::validate`), a recoverable denial by a hostile remote until the conflict is resolved.
* **`vl serve` with a closed standard error**: checked, `eprintln!` is redefined so it never panics; requests still succeed.

## Round 7 coverage notes

* **`--json` confidentiality** (`api.rs`): for a non-member on a team clone and a reader of a public mirror, with an embargoed merge (sealed description, new path, fixed line) and a cloaked workspace: `log`, `ls`, `show`, `diff`, `status`, `files`, `resolve --list`, `tag list` print no sealed description, no embargoed path or content. Op-log descriptions name changes and tags but never messages. Text and JSON are built from the same structures; strings go through `to_json`'s escaping.
* **Conflicts (format 4)**: materialisation for a member and an outsider, partly edited markers (sides are sealed as the resolution), a hidden base, two scopes, `resolve --ours/--theirs/--base` (sealing kept), the Git export and a public push of a conflict with a sealed side (redacted): no leak found outside BUG-480/481. Hostile terms of the wrong kind or with forged labels are handled (`verify_sealing`, `Corrupt` → unreadable).
* **`vl split` / `vl squash`**: partial squash into an unrelated change by hunk and `vl undo` restore exactly; audiences of in-between files follow the new version (`selection::classify`); `.veilpolicy`, conflicts and embargoes move whole; stale selections are refused by `check_current`.
* **`vl push --dry-run`** reads the remote through `ReadOnly` and takes no lock; the object list is the real push's (`PlanRecord`). Only the `files` part is computed apart, from labels (BUG-482).
* **`vl serve --local`** (fork): 40 server writes raced against 40 CLI writes, and a 50 ms working-copy `watch` against 60 rounds of CLI `commit`/`new`/`describe`: no edit or operation lost. 200,000 levels of nesting, huge limits and ids, 900 KB revisions and a 1 MiB+ line without a newline: the server stays up. A second server is refused, a stale socket replaced, symlinked `sock` and `serve.lock` refused. Nothing served beyond what the CLI shows the same identity.
* **Scanner v6** (fork): 38 crafted shapes at 1 and 4 MiB all scan in at most 132 ms (release), ratio ≤ 3.5×; 1.2 GB of real code in under 4 s. UTF-16/32 secrets at chunk boundaries found; no boundary-dependent finding on minified bundles. New v6 shapes (camelCase keys, `*_KEY`, XML elements and attributes, connection strings, URL passwords) found apart from BUG-487/488.
* **WASM / no-`os`** (fork): `cargo check -p veil-core --no-default-features --features wasm` passes for the host and for `wasm32-unknown-unknown`; 1.5 million mutated zstd frames through a copy of `pack::pure::decompress` with ruzstd 0.9.1: no panic, no loop, none slower than 500 ms; pack framing reviewed with no panic path found; a clock at 0 makes locks fail safe.

## Round 8 findings (Opus, sandboxes, mount and team vault)

Baseline: `integration` @ `aa78e6d`. Line numbers refer to it. Tests:
`crates/vl/tests/cli_adversarial/adversarial8/` (modules `sandbox`, `mount`, `vault`;
the mount tests skip without `/dev/fuse` and `fusermount3`, mount under
`$TMPDIR` and unmount on drop) and `crates/veil-core/tests/core_security/adversarial8_core/`
(module `vault`). Each `#[ignore = "BUG-NNN: …"]` test asserts the
correct behaviour and fails today:

```sh
cargo test -p vl --test cli_adversarial -- --ignored adversarial8::
cargo test -p veil-core --test core_security -- --ignored adversarial8_core::
```

Summary: 8 high, 11 medium, 1 low. No critical: every leak below needs a
member's own action (a command they run in a sandbox, a mountpoint they
chose, a teammate's vault entry), not only a hostile remote.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-520 | high (**fixed**, round-8 vault wave) | `vl run`, team vault | **Fixed:** (1) `vl run` never sets execution-affecting names from a team vault (`team_vault::is_execution_variable`: `PATH`, `LD_*`, `DYLD_*`, `BASH_ENV`, `ENV`, `PROMPT_COMMAND`, `PS1`–`PS4`, `SHELL`, `IFS`, `HOME`, `TMPDIR`, `NODE_OPTIONS`, `PYTHON*`, `PERL*`, `RUBY*`, `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS`, `GIT_*`, `NPM_CONFIG_*`, `XDG_*`, `VEIL_*`, compiler/build flags, proxies, CA bundles, tool config paths, case-insensitive) and warns; `vl secret set --scope` stores them but warns. (2) Default changed: `vl run` loads no team vault unless `--scope` is given or the device chose its default with `vl run --scope S --remember` (`.veil/local/run-scopes.json`); it says which scopes hold secrets. (3) New writes need shell identifiers; older `.`/`-` names are still read but not set by `vl run`. By default `vl run` loads the team vault of every scope you belong to, and team entries override the environment. Nothing restricts which names a member may set, so any member of any shared scope can store `BASH_ENV`, `PATH`, `LD_PRELOAD`, `NODE_OPTIONS`, `GIT_SSH_COMMAND`, `KUBECONFIG` and the like. Every teammate's next `vl run -- …` then runs code of that member's choosing, or talks to their endpoints. That command's environment also holds the secrets of every other loaded scope. Reproduced: Bob, a member of `team` only, sets `BASH_ENV=scripts/env.sh`. Alice's `vl run -- bash -c …` prints scope `prod`'s `PROD_DB_PASSWORD`, which Bob was never granted. A member of one scope reads another scope's vault and gets code execution on teammates' machines, and only `vl secret list` shows anything. | `team_vault.rs:231` `validate_name` allows any env-like name. `team_secret.rs:164-174` `run_env` takes every live entry. `main.rs:1829-1832` `cmd_run` `.envs(&team)` overrides the inherited environment, with every scope loaded by default (`team_secret.rs:148`). | `adversarial8::vault::a_team_member_cannot_take_over_vl_run_through_loader_variables` (control: `run_injects_an_ordinary_team_secret`); `team_vault_cli::team_secret_names_are_identifiers_and_loader_variables_are_flagged`, `run_loads_team_secrets_then_the_local_vault_overrides`; unit `execution_variables_are_recognised` |
| BUG-521 | medium (**fixed**, round-8 vault wave) | team vault, `--exposed-to`, undo | **Fixed:** each device keeps a keyed-hash history of the values each name held, with their first generation (`.veil/local/vault-history/<scope>.json`, CRYPTO.md C12). A write of a value seen before takes that `since`, so the record carries the exposure to every replica, and `--exposed-to` consults the history too. Limit: a device that never saw the earlier value cannot link it. The rotation report forgets a leaked value that is set again. Bob could read `DB_PASSWORD=p1`. After his revocation Alice sets `p2`, then `p1` again (the rotation broke something, or `vl undo` of the rotation told her to "set it again with `vl secret set --scope …`"). The same happens after a delete followed by re-setting the old value. `--exposed-to bob` (and the guide's "what is still left to rotate") then says nothing is exposed, though the live value is one Bob holds. The guide says "setting the *same* value again doesn't count as rotation". | `team_vault.rs:799-802`: `since` is carried over only when the immediately previous winner holds the same value, otherwise it becomes the current generation. The value's earlier history (still visible in the revoked member's generation) is not consulted. | `adversarial8_core::vault::exposure_survives_going_back_to_a_leaked_value` (control: `exposure_survives_setting_the_current_value_again`); `team_vault::going_back_to_a_leaked_value_is_exposed_on_every_replica` |
| BUG-522 | medium (**fixed**, round-8 vault wave) | team vault sync, clocks | **Fixed:** push reports refused records too ("not copied (timestamp out of range: … ahead …)"). A write deletes its writer's own records of the name stamped more than a day ahead and is stamped by the clock (re-stamp, not refuse); the next push deletes such records of theirs from the remote when it holds the later write. A winning write of someone else's that far ahead makes the write an error naming the clocks to check. Push silently withholds the writer's own records stamped more than a day ahead, and with them every later write of the same name. A record Alice wrote while her clock ran 2 days ahead pins the Lamport floor (`max(now, winner+1)`). After her clock is fixed, her rotation of that secret is stamped "2 days ahead" too. `push` skips it with no warning (warnings only exist when pulling), reports success, and Bob keeps the old (leaked) value. When the records later fall inside the window, they surface and beat writes teammates made in the meantime. | `team_vault.rs:928` refuses on push with the pusher's own clock. `team_vault.rs:950` and `remote.rs:1096` (`warn = pulling`) drop the reason on push. `team_vault.rs:797` floors new writes on the bad winner, unbounded. | `adversarial8_core::vault::a_rotation_after_a_clock_glitch_reaches_teammates_or_push_says_why`; `team_vault::push_reports_the_records_it_does_not_send`, `a_record_stamped_far_ahead_is_replaced_by_the_next_write_everywhere` |
| BUG-523 | medium (**fixed**, round-8 vault wave) | `vl run`, team vault | **Fixed:** a scope's live entries are capped at 256 KiB in all on write (counted as `execve` counts them); `vl run` adds up arguments and environment before running, and over `ARG_MAX` (or on `E2BIG`) names each loaded scope's size and largest entries and suggests `--scope` / `--local-only`. With the BUG-520 default, plain `vl run` loads no team vault. The test now asserts the write cap (the fifth 64 000-byte entry is refused; it stored 40) and runs the size report under a 2 MiB stack limit. One member's large entries break every member's `vl run`. Values are capped at 64 KiB each but there is no cap on their number or total size. A member storing 40 entries of 64 000 bytes (or thousands of small ones) makes every teammate's plain `vl run -- CMD` (all scopes by default) fail with `failed to run …: Argument list too long (os error 7)`. The error names no scope or entry and doesn't hint at `--scope` / `--local-only`. | `team_secret.rs:130-178` `run_env` has no total-size bound. `main.rs:1829-1834` passes the E2BIG error through as is. `team_vault.rs:94` caps only the per-value size. | `adversarial8::vault::many_large_entries_do_not_break_everyones_vl_run` (no longer slow); `team_secret` unit tests `too_big…`, `exec_size…`; `team_vault::a_scope_vault_is_capped_in_total` |
| BUG-524 | high (**fixed**, round-8 vault wave) | team vault, revocation | **Fixed:** `vl secret set` and `vl secret rm --scope` fast-forward the ring from every team remote first (`remote::refresh_ring`) and warns when none could be checked. Push re-seals the pusher's own records sealed under a generation older than the remote ring's current one (local ring fast-forwarded, same name/value/`since`, stamped 1 ms later) and says so; the stale record is not sent. Other records sealed under generation g after g+1 started and readable by a member revoked in between are copied, named in a push/pull warning, and marked `stale_generation` in `vl secret list` (to set again). Limit (documented in C12): timestamps are self-asserted, so a member can backdate to escape the flag, but could hand the value over anyway. Alice revokes Bob and pushes. A teammate who has not pulled since (Carol) stores a *new* secret. Her ring still says generation 0 is current, so the value is sealed under generation 0, which Bob still holds. Carol's push merges the remote's ring (which has the revocation) and still copies the record: it passes every check, since generation 0 is in the ring and Carol is a current member. Alice's pull accepts it. Nobody is warned, so a value first written after the revocation can be read by the revoked member (with read access to the remote). This contradicts "content that changes after a revocation moves to the new generation". `--exposed-to bob` would list it, but only if someone thinks to run it again. | `team_vault.rs:161` `seal` uses the writer's local `sealing_key`. `team_vault.rs:928-931` `sync` checks only that the generation is *in* the ring, not that it is current there. Push never refuses, re-seals or warns (`remote.rs:1096` passes `warn = pulling`). | `adversarial8_core::vault::a_value_written_after_a_revocation_is_not_readable_by_the_revoked_member`; `team_vault::a_stale_writers_push_reseals_under_the_current_generation`, `a_record_sealed_late_under_an_old_generation_is_flagged_on_pull`; `team_vault_cli::a_write_checks_the_key_ring_against_the_team_remote_first` |
| BUG-534 | high (**fixed**, round-8 mount wave) | mount / visibility | **Fixed:** the mount reads its base again with the repository's current keys every time it looks at the workspace (every second and before every recording), so a grant or a release shows in the mount and the recording judges what the mount shows with the builder's keys. Tests: this one, `mount_props::a_deleted_directory_keeps_what_it_never_showed_across_a_crash_and_a_grant`, the property `mount_props::a_mount_under_grants_moves_undo_and_crashes_matches_the_model`. Bob clones without the key, mounts a workspace, then is granted and pulls (the onboarding flow). The mount's next recording, of any unrelated edit, records the cloaked files it never showed as **deleted**; pushing that deletes them for the team. A checkout fills them in instead (control). | The mount keeps the `ReaderView` it built with the keychain it opened at mount time, so newly readable files never appear (`mount.rs:218` `lower_of`; `Recorder::open` `mount.rs:259`). | BUG-547 | high (**fixed**, round-8 sandbox wave) | `ws exec --with-isolated` | **Fixed:** the guard outlives the run: every isolated file ever injected into a sandbox is kept in `.veil/local/sandboxes/<ws>/guard.sealed` (sealed under the `local` scope, like the vault), and every later recording of that sandbox applies it (quarantine) and scrubs for copies (BUG-546), until `vl ws rm`. Chosen over wiping the sandbox after an injected run, which would destroy unrecorded work (quarantined files, kept edits, build caches) and still not stop a background process from writing a copy afterwards. Background processes are deliberately not killed (a dev server is often the point); the persistent guard is the rule that makes that safe (ARCHITECTURE, sandboxes). The injected-secret guard covers only the `--with-isolated` run itself: a copy of the secret that is still (or newly) in the sandbox afterwards (a background dev server logging its environment, a BUG-546 leftover, an orphan of a killed run) is recorded in plaintext by the next plain `vl ws exec`. The scanner misses arbitrary values; only the guard knows them, and the vault is at hand in every run. | `crates/vl/src/sandbox.rs:296-299,333`: `InjectedSecrets` is built only `if a.with_isolated` and passed only to that run's `snapshot_workspace_with` (`:340`); nothing in the manifest remembers an injection, and later runs are unguarded. | `adversarial8`: `sandbox::a_secret_that_lands_after_an_injected_run_is_never_recorded` |
| BUG-548 | medium (**fixed**, round-8 sandbox wave) | `ws exec` rebuild / data loss | **Fixed:** a rebuild never deletes unrecorded work (`sandbox::set_aside`): quarantined files and files outside a sparse set that the tree does not have as they are are copied back into the rebuilt sandbox (`kept N file(s) of unrecorded work across the rebuild`, quarantined ones stay in the manifest for `vl ws rm`); if other files differ from both the old tree and the new one (hand edits, or files a power loss tore), or the manifest cannot be read, or the sandbox holds an unrecorded run that cannot be recorded (another boot), the previous files are moved whole to `unrecorded-<time>/` with a warning; `vl doctor` lists those and `vl ws rm` refuses without `--force`. Every rebuild silently deletes all of `files/`: quarantined work (which `vl ws rm` protects behind `--force`) and hand edits kept outside a narrower `--only` set included. Triggers: a reboot, an interrupted run, a pruned tree, and on Linux another pid namespace (the boot identity includes `/proc/self/ns/pid`, so `vl` from a container or Flatpak terminal counts as another boot). | `crates/vl/src/sandbox.rs:259-263` calls `core::wipe` for every `rebuild_reason` (`veil-core/src/sandbox.rs:261-291`, boot check at `:277`) without looking at `Manifest::quarantined` or the kept files, and without a warning about them. | `adversarial8`: `sandbox::a_rebuild_keeps_quarantined_work` |
| BUG-549 | medium (**fixed**, round-8 sandbox wave) | sparse `ws exec` / data loss | **Fixed:** a file outside the set only counts as the command's output if neither the policy it started with (the synced tree's) nor the one it left ignores or isolates it (`sandbox::strays` takes both). `vl` deletes, as "written outside --only", files the command never wrote: when the command's `.veilpolicy` edit makes an ignored or isolated path no longer so, everything already there (build caches, local configs, isolated credentials; none in any snapshot) is removed. | `crates/vl/src/sandbox.rs:292-295` lists the files present before the command (`kept`) under the pre-run policy, which skips ignored and isolated files; `:413-417` lists strays under the post-run policy and `:422` removes every stray not in `kept`. | `adversarial8`: `sandbox::a_policy_edit_in_a_sparse_run_never_deletes_files_the_command_did_not_write` (control: `sandbox::ignored_output_outside_a_sparse_set_stays`) |
| BUG-550 | medium (**fixed**, round-8 sandbox wave) | `ws exec` signals / data loss | **Fixed:** `vl` forwards SIGTERM and SIGHUP to the command (as it lets Ctrl-C through), waits for it and records what it left. The reaper of a persistent run never wipes: it only takes out injected files that still hold exactly what was injected, putting back what they replaced; the command's output stays and is recorded by the next run. SIGTERM or SIGHUP to `vl ws exec` (an IDE stopping a task, `kill`, a closed terminal) kills `vl` at once; the reaper then wipes the sandbox and the command's unrecorded output while the command keeps running in a deleted directory. Nothing was injected, so the wipe protects no secret. | `crates/vl/src/sandbox.rs:533-574` ignores only SIGINT and SIGQUIT; the reaper (`:792`, `reap_persistent` at `:825`) wipes whenever the run marker is left, injected or not. | `adversarial8`: `sandbox::sigterm_to_vl_keeps_the_commands_work` |
| BUG-551 | medium (**fixed**, round-8 sandbox wave) | `ws exec` errors / data loss | **Fixed:** no error wipes a sandbox. Just before the command starts the manifest says, durably, that the files hold unrecorded output (`pending`); a recording that fails leaves it so, `vl` says the output stays, and the next `vl ws exec` records it first (`Recording the output of an earlier run…`), or `vl ws exec NAME --record-only` does. Once recorded, the manifest says so, so a cleanup that fails afterwards is resumed without recording twice. If another process holds the repository lock for more than 15 s when the command finishes, recording fails ("repository is locked") and the command's whole output is wiped: lock contention destroys work nothing else threatened. | `PersistentRun::drop` (`crates/vl/src/sandbox.rs:179-197`) wipes on every error after step 3, reached by `?` on `snapshot_workspace_with` (`:340`) after the 15 s lock wait (`veil-core/src/lock.rs:195`). | `adversarial8`: `sandbox::a_busy_repository_lock_at_record_time_keeps_the_run` |

With `--only src/lib.rs`, a command doing `rm src/lib.rs && rmdir src` (or `rm -rf src`) records every file under `src/` as deleted, `src/other.rs` and `src/deep/d.rs` included, though they were never in the set or the sandbox. Full, sparse and `--ephemeral` runs alike. A command that deletes `.veilpolicy` in a sandbox (full or sparse) gets every cloaked file recorded as **public plaintext**, without a warning; in a sparse run, cloaked files outside the set too. | BUG-560 | high (**fixed**, round-8 closing wave) | `ws exec` sandboxes / policy | **Fixed:** recordings of files a command wrote (`ws exec` sandboxes, full, sparse and ephemeral, and mounts: `SnapshotOptions::policy_from_command`) are pinned by the recorded `.veilpolicy` of the tree they were synced from (`Repo::command_policy`, `policy::command_edit`). An edit that cannot weaken it (same rules, appended `isolate`/`ignore`, dropped `public!`, or proved never weaker by the fold's exhaustive check) is recorded; one that only inserts `cloak`/`isolate`/`ignore` rules is recorded unless it weakens a path of the snapshot; anything else (truncate, a rule deleted, `public!`, `** public`, a scope retargeted; in a mount also a deleted, symlinked or unreadable file) is held back: it applies where stronger, the recorded classification where weaker (`P` paths, `BuildReport::policy_held_back`), and the tree keeps the recorded `.veilpolicy` (`BuildReport::policy_edit_held`), so later runs stay pinned without device state. The edit stays in the sandbox or mount overlay until `vl policy accept --workspace NAME` (`Repo::accept_workspace_policy`) records it and lists what it weakens; an ephemeral run drops it, with a warning. The mount is treated as a command (tools and agents write there). `vl serve` needs nothing: no method writes files, and its `snapshot` records the project directory under the accepted policy. Tests: `adversarial8` `policy_edits::*` (every shape through full, sparse, ephemeral sandboxes and the mount; accept; strengthening), `veil-core` `policy::tests::command_edits_are_judged_against_the_recorded_policy`. A command in a `vl ws exec` sandbox (typically an AI agent) can weaken `.veilpolicy` (truncate it, or add `secret/ public`), and the recording takes it as the member's own edit, so it applies at once and declassifies cloaked files in that workspace's next snapshot. In the project directory the member is the editor; in a sandbox the editor is the command. A sandbox's policy weakening must be held back like an incoming one (`policy_incoming`: the pin guards it until `vl policy accept`). Found by the round-8 sandbox wave (reported as low for the truncate case; upgraded). | sandbox recording builds with the member-edit policy rule. | `adversarial8` `policy_edits::*` |
| BUG-561 | medium (**fixed**, round-8 closing wave) | FUSE mount / memory | **Fixed:** open files live on disk: the first write or `truncate` of a lower-only file copies it up (atomically, durably), then reads and writes go straight to the upper file opened in place (`mount::fs::UpperFiles`/`DiskFiles`: `pread`/`pwrite`/`ftruncate`, synced on `flush`/`fsync`/`release`), so a truncate is a hole and memory does not grow with a file's size; renaming an upper file moves it (O(1), holes kept) instead of copying it through memory. Only lower-only files are read into memory while open (one decryption per open, bounded by the object). Tests: `adversarial8` `mount::a_sparse_file_in_the_mount_costs_no_memory` (peak RSS of the serving process across a 2 GiB truncate plus writes, and an existing file truncated to 2 GiB), `vl` unit test `mount::fs::tests::open_files_are_their_upper_file`. Remaining part of BUG-536: a `truncate` (or sparse write) up to the 4 GiB object cap fills the file into memory; on a small machine the kernel OOM killer can take the mount process down (other open files lose unsaved writes). Open files should live on disk (the overlay's upper file), not in memory. Found by the round-8 mount wave. | `mount.rs` open-file buffers are in memory. | `adversarial8` `mount::a_sparse_file_in_the_mount_costs_no_memory` |

## Round 8 hypotheses (not demonstrated)

* **`vl restore --from <embargoed fix> PATH` on a public line** probably publishes the names and sizes of the files the fix touches (inferred from the code by the Git-parity threat review; untested). For the next adversarial pass.
* **Mount, gc'd `base`.** `Recorder::open` falls back to the current snapshot but `state.base` keeps the id gc removed; the next `follow` then has `old_tree = None` and skips the overlap check, so upper edits silently shadow the workspace's change. Needs gc to prune a snapshot the op log still references (`--keep-ops-days`).
* **Mount, unlink race.** A file deleted between `with_view` and `reconcile` of a recording comes back. (**Addressed** in the round-8 mount wave: the recording and its `reconcile` run under one lock, `MountFs::record_with`.)
* **Mount, whole-file buffers.** A large file is decrypted into memory under the global lock on open, stalling every other request meanwhile (a same-user slowdown; see also BUG-536).
* **Sandbox, `vouch_written`** re-stats by path right after the write, so a concurrent writer's edit (a watcher left running by an earlier command) between the write and the stat could be vouched for as checkout content.
* **Sandbox, `sweep_stale`** judges an ephemeral sandbox stale by its owner's pid, so a `vl` in another pid namespace could have its live sandbox removed; `unshare -U` is not permitted on this machine.
* **Sandbox, `FICLONE`.** The clone path falls back on ext4 and tmpfs, so the fstat-before-and-after guard of `try_clone` was only read, not run.
* **Sandbox, deleting `../lock` during a run** could admit a concurrent run (tampering by the command only).
* **Team vault, revocation by an admin who has not pulled the member's last write:** the entry falls back to the older value for everyone and the newer record is "kept but ignored" with a pull warning. Follows from self-asserted timestamps (documented); not filed.
* **Team vault, a revoked member's writes made while revoked** count again after a re-grant. Documented ("they would count again after a re-grant"), but a re-grant silently brings in writes made while the member was out, stamped up to a day ahead.

Leads that held up (no bug): no team-vault name or value reached a public remote, `push --dry-run` (text or JSON), `vl serve`, warnings or error text (op-log descriptions name secrets, but `.veil/ops` never syncs); compaction cannot change the visible value (grouping by signer id and key matches `check_record`'s verdict, and keys come only from the verified ring); replayed or compacted records are outranked again; `=`/NUL names and NUL values are refused at write and when a record is opened; `i64::MAX` and negative timestamps are refused on sync, and the floor saturates; the two-scope refusal, its local override and an explicit `--scope` that fails to load all behave as documented. In the sandbox: same-size rewrites with `touch -r`, inode reuse and `touch -d` into the past or future are caught (ctime is in the stamp; future stamps are never trusted); FIFOs, `chmod 000` and symlinks over tracked files are kept with a warning, nothing hangs or writes through; a command rewriting `../manifest.json` or `../statcache` is overwritten from memory at the end of the run; unreadable files and conflict entries outside an `--only` set are carried; ephemeral `--with-isolated` refuses symlinked parents. In the mount: a non-member's view of a sealed conflict side is the redacted rendering and `stat` matches `read`; `.veil` in any case, symlinks and hard links are refused; no `allow_other`, `default_permissions`, `nosuid`, `nodev`, `0600` files; renames of open files follow POSIX; the record-before-command hook records in-process (no deadlock); torn whiteout lines can only resurrect a file, never delete one; non-member deletes and renames keep the hidden sealed entries.

## Round 8 coverage notes

* **Sandboxes** (`adversarial8::sandbox`): injection cleanup, rebuild triggers, the reaper and signals, error paths, special files and odd permissions, stat-cache games, and for `--only`: spine directories, policy edits, kept hand edits, unreadable entries, conflicts. Not covered: concurrency beyond the existing `ws_sandbox_cli.rs` tests; gc does not consult sandbox manifests, so `--keep-ops-days` pruning is one more trigger of BUG-548's rebuild.
* **Mount** (`adversarial8::mount`): real kernel mounts through `fusermount3`. Not tested: another OS user (needs root; `default_permissions` and the absence of `allow_other` were checked in the mount options instead), macFUSE, and a `vl serve` client over a mounted cloaked workspace (`serve` does not call `record_live`, so a served command does not see the mount's unrecorded edits). BUG-553 and BUG-554 probably apply to mount recordings too: they use the same `snapshot_workspace_with` over a backend that is not the project directory. Trees deeper than `MAX_TREE_DEPTH` make the recording fail and `vl ws unmount` refuse (not mount-specific). `readdir` re-lists the whole directory per request; acceptable at 3,000 entries.
* **Team vault** (`adversarial8::vault`, `adversarial8_core::vault`): core attacks use in-memory peers and forged or planted records (`VaultRecord::seal`), never the global test clock; CLI attacks use the real binary with a path team remote and two identities (`BASH_ENV` exfiltration shown with `/bin/bash`; `LD_PRELOAD` and `PATH` by reflecting them). Not attacked: the SFTP transport of records (covered by `team_vault.rs`), legacy rings, two concurrent `vl secret set` (serialised by `lock_meta`). Design gaps noted, not filed: `ws exec`'s injected-secret guard and the builder never consider team-vault (or local-vault) *values*, so `vl run -- vl ws exec agent -- …`, or a `vl run` build writing `$DB_PASSWORD` into a tracked file, records the value unless a scanner pattern matches; `vl undo` of a team-vault overwrite or `rm` says "set it again", but the superseded value has been compacted away (own writes) or is unreachable by any command.

## Round 9 findings (Opus, Git-parity features)

Baseline: `integration` @ `4a62259`. Line numbers refer to it. Tests:
`crates/vl/tests/cli_adversarial/adversarial9/` (modules `hooks`, `tags`, `history`,
`attributes`, `embargo`, `symlinks`; the hook tests need `python3` and
`make`, the `/proc` test is Linux-only). Each `#[ignore = "BUG-NNN: …"]`
test asserts the correct behaviour and fails today:

```sh
cargo test -p vl --test cli_adversarial -- --ignored adversarial9::
```

Summary: 4 high, 3 medium (a fourth, BUG-706, was about Git-format patches,
since removed), 2 low, plus BUG-682 pinned (deterministic, root
cause below). No critical: the code-execution findings need the victim to
trust a teammate's `.veilhooks` once (after that, nothing more), and the
sandbox escapes need a teammate's tree plus an agent run in `ws exec`.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-700 | high (**fixed**, Git-parity fix wave 3) | hooks / code execution | **Fixed:** a directory named in `files`, or given to a command whose program may run it (`python*`, `pypy*`, `node`, `bun`, as the program or behind a launcher), is pinned as `tree:<blake3>` of a manifest of every file below it (kind, path, content digest; at most 1024 entries, 64 MiB, no links, else trust refuses), and the command gets a private copy of the checked bytes (`hooks::read_tree`, `tree_digest`, `trusted_tree_copy`). Other directories stay the command's data (`ruff check src`), pinned as `directory` only, so editing them does not untrust a linter; `python3 .` is refused. Old `directory` records for such paths no longer match: re-trust. Property test `hooks::tests::every_repository_file_a_hook_runs_is_pinned_or_refused`. A `.veilhooks` command argument that names a **directory** is pinned as the word `directory`, never by content (`vl hooks trust` lists `tools  directory` under "Pinned files", which reads as pinned). `run = ["python3", "tools"]` runs `tools/__main__.py` (as `node tools` runs `tools/index.js`); once the victim trusts it, a teammate's later change to `tools/__main__.py` runs on their device at every commit, with no new trust and no warning. Design 5a threat 1 ("the digests of `.veilhooks` and of every file it names"). The same holds for `files = ["dir"]`. | `crates/vl/src/hooks.rs:349` (`path_state`: a directory is `PathState::Dir`, content not hashed) and `:1024` (`run_one` passes a `Dir`-pinned argument through as the live path). Suggested fix: refuse directories as arguments and in `files` (or pin a manifest of every file below, and copy the tree). | `adversarial9::hooks::a_changed_file_inside_a_trusted_directory_argument_does_not_run` |
| BUG-701 | high (**fixed**, Git-parity fix wave 3) | hooks / code execution | **Fixed:** `hooks::classify` normalises every argument against the project (`spelled`): `./a`, `a//b`, `a/./b`, `a/` are `a`, and the value of `--opt=a` and `VAR=a` is a path too; each is pinned and replaced by its trusted copy after its prefix. What cannot be pinned is refused by `vl hooks trust` (and never runs on an older record): a path through `..` that lands in the project, an absolute path into the project or through `/proc`/`/dev/fd`, a path under `.veil`/`.git`, a path glued to a short option (`-Iscripts/x`), a `PATH`-like list (`PYTHONPATH=lib:tools`), and a `VAR=x` whose whole spelling is also a project file. An argument that names a repository file in any spelling but the canonical one is a literal: neither pinned nor replaced by its trusted copy. `run = ["sh", "./scripts/lint.sh"]` (also `scripts//lint.sh`, `scripts/./lint.sh`, `--rcfile=scripts/x`, `env LD_PRELOAD=./lib/x.so true`) is trusted with **no** pinned file, and `sh` reads whatever the script holds at commit time: a teammate's later edit runs. The copy-then-exec guarantee (threat 2) never applies to it. | `hooks.rs:469-484` (`classify`: `repo_path` refuses `.` and empty components and anything starting with `-` is a literal, so the argument becomes `Arg::Literal`; `VAR=./lib/x.so` is pinned as an absent file of that literal name, which never changes). Suggested fix: refuse any argument that resolves (after normalisation) to a repository path unless it is written canonically, and treat `--opt=path`/`VAR=path` forms the same way. | `adversarial9::hooks::a_changed_script_named_with_a_dot_slash_does_not_run` |
| BUG-702 | medium (**fixed**, Git-parity fix wave 3) | hooks / code execution | **Fixed:** `hooks::programs` counts every argument after a known launcher (`hooks::LAUNCHERS`: `env`, `nice`, `nohup`, `timeout`, `sudo`, `stdbuf`, `flock`, `vl`, …) as a program that may run, so `nice make`, `timeout 60 make`, `sudo -u x make` pin the `Makefile` and need `--i-understand-indirect`. `hooks::refusal` refuses, flag or not, code given as text (`sh -c`/`bash -ec`/`-lc` anywhere after a shell, `python3 -c`, `node -e`/`-p`, `perl -e`, `ruby -e`, `php -r`, `awk` without `-f`, `env -S`, `deno eval`) and programs that run their arguments through a shell or take them from their input (`xargs`, `watch`, `parallel`, `hyperfine`, `entr`). Build-tool indirection (threat 3) is detected only when the build tool is argv[0] or follows `env`. `["nice", "make"]`, `["timeout", "60", "make"]`, `["nohup", "make"]`, `["xargs", "make"]`, `["sh", "-c", "make lint"]` are trusted without `--i-understand-indirect` and without pinning the `Makefile`; a teammate's later `Makefile` edit runs. (Related, documented-limit territory: `python3 -c "import x"` reads `x.py` from the project root unpinned.) | `hooks.rs:426-446` (`effective_program` skips only `env`; `indirect` matches the first other word). Suggested fix: treat every argument that is a known build tool (or any command whose argv[0] is a launcher: `nice`, `timeout`, `nohup`, `xargs`, `sudo`, `exec`, shells with `-c`) as indirect, or refuse launchers. | `adversarial9::hooks::a_build_tool_behind_a_launcher_still_pins_its_files` |
| BUG-703 | medium (**fixed**, Git-parity fix wave 4) | release tags / supply chain | **Fixed:** `remote::pull_with` never moves (or re-annotates) a local release tag: the remote's target is recorded as `<remote>/<name>` and reported at every pull (`SyncReport::release_tags_kept`, "origin moved release tag v1.0 from X to Y; your release tag v1.0 stays at X"); only `vl tag set v1.0 -r origin/v1.0 --force` takes it. `vl clone` creates tags only (no local ones exist). Tests: `adversarial9::tags::a_moved_release_tag_is_reported_recorded_and_taken_only_with_force`. `vl pull` from a Veil remote moves a local **annotated release tag** when the remote's target descends from it, and replaces its annotation with the remote's (another member's atag, or none).
