A teammate (or a hostile remote) re-points everyone's `v1.0` to new code at their next pull, silently ("updated v1.0"). Annotated tags are immutable here (`tag set` needs `--force`, design 5c), and design 8 threat 7 says a remote that moves one never moves the local tag. `vl tag verify` afterwards fails (unknown signer) only if someone thinks to run it. | `crates/veil-core/src/remote.rs:2051-2060` (`pull`: the fast-forward branch applies to every tag, `annotation: tag.annotation`). Suggested fix: never move a local tag that has an annotation; record the remote's target in `remote_tags` and warn "remote moved release tag v1.0 from X to Y", as `vl git fetch` does. | `adversarial9::tags::pull_never_moves_a_local_release_tag` |
| BUG-704 | low (**fixed**, Git-parity fix wave 4; feature **removed**, format 8) | `vl blame --public` / descriptions | **Fixed:** with `--public`, `api::blame` renders `rev` and `changes` through `api::snapshot_ref_as(…, public)`: the description as a public mirror reads it (`git::public_description`: plaintext and released embargoes only, keys held for cloak scopes never used), else `description_hidden` with the new reason `not_public` (documented in `docs/reference/json.md`). `--public` is the only public view among blame, grep and log; readers without the key already got `no_key`. Test: `adversarial9::history::blame_public_hides_sealed_descriptions_and_keeps_public_ones`. `vl blame --public` ("as a public mirror sees the history", design 1: for screenshots and issues) attributes lines correctly, but its `--json` document's `rev` (and `changes`) carry descriptions as *this reader* reads them: the sealed description of a cloaked change and of an unreleased embargoed merge, in plain text, where the mirror shows `(description withheld)` / `[cloaked:embargo/…]`. The text output prints no descriptions. | `crates/vl/src/api.rs:2723` and `:2734` (`blame`: `snapshot_ref(repo, …)` reads the description with the member's keys whatever `req.public` says). Suggested fix: with `public`, render refs through the public projection (`git::project::public_text`). | `adversarial9::history::blame_public_never_prints_a_sealed_description` |
| BUG-705 | low (**fixed**, Git-parity fix wave 4) | attributes / cloaked content | **Fixed:** the merge picks a file's strategy after deciding its merged audience, from the attributes of ours as *that audience* reads them (`merge::MergeAttrs`, `attributes::AudienceTreeSource`): a `.veilattributes` that is public, under a released embargo, or sealed exactly for the result's audience is read as it is; one sealed for anyone else gives its fallback (last public version) or nothing. A public result is therefore shaped by public attributes only; a result cloaked for `team` still follows `team`'s sealed file (test `a_sealed_gitattributes_still_decides_a_merge_sealed_for_its_scope`). A **sealed** `.veilattributes` decides the content of a **public** merge result (the W2 agent's suspicion, proven): with `log.txt merge=union` in a cloaked `.veilattributes`, a member's merge of two public edits is a clean union, recorded and published as public content; with any other rule (same size) the same merge is a conflict. The public result tells every mirror reader one bit only `team` may read, and a non-member merging the same history gets another tree. | `crates/veil-core/src/merge.rs:177` (`Attrs::for_tree(store, plan.ours)` reads every `.veilattributes` this reader can open, sealed ones included, and applies them to every path). Suggested fix: for a path whose merged audience is public, use only public `.veilattributes` files (`Readable::Plain` of a public entry). | `adversarial9::attributes::a_sealed_gitattributes_does_not_decide_a_public_merge` (control: `control_a_public_union_attribute_merges_by_union`) |
| BUG-707 | medium (**fixed** with BUG-682, Git-parity fix wave 2; feature **removed**, format 8) | duplicate / cloaked lines | BUG-682's root cause through `vl duplicate`: duplicating a public change no remote has seen onto a cloaked line seals what the line introduces (design 1, threat 13), but the sealed entry's **fallback** (what readers without the key get) is the duplicated content itself. Pushing the cloaked line publishes the draft in plaintext as that fallback (`vl cat` of the duplicate on a mirror clone prints it). | `crates/veil-core/src/merge.rs:824-827` (`merged_visibility` / `public_other`: when the sealed side has a fallback, the public side becomes the fallback as "the newest public version", although that side is content the cloaked line is taking in and has never been published). | `adversarial9::embargo::a_draft_duplicated_onto_a_cloaked_line_stays_off_the_mirror` |
| BUG-708 | high (**fixed**, Git-parity fix wave 3) | `ws exec` sandboxes / symlinks / isolated files | **Fixed:** `SandboxFs::guard_link` uses `vfs::sandbox_link_check`: a link is materialised only when its target is relative, all its `..` come first and stay beneath the root, and names follow. Since every link sits in a real directory at its own depth, that holds whatever other links the tree has (a `..` after a name, which a chain needs, is refused), with no knowledge of the tree. Same for full, `--only`, `--ephemeral` sandboxes and `vl bisect run`. Property test `symlinks::a_sandbox_never_resolves_outside_itself_through_any_links` (1000 cases; it finds the chain against the old rule). The FUSE mount was checked: it serves links as they are, like the project directory, with no isolation promise. `vl status`'s lexical warning (`vfs::link_resolves_to`) is unchanged and still misses chains (a warning only, outside this wave's area). Threat 7's invariant ("the sandbox checkout refuses relative targets that resolve outside the sandbox root") is checked per link and lexically. A **chain** defeats it: `s1/s2/s3/s4/u -> ../../../..` (the sandbox root: inside) and `s1/s2/s3/s4/x -> u/../../../../../.env` (lexically the root's `.env`: inside). The kernel resolves `u` first and then climbs five levels above the root, to the project's isolated `.env`: `vl ws exec agent -- cat s1/s2/s3/s4/x` prints it without `--with-isolated`. Both links come from a teammate's tree. Checked with `--only s1` too; `--ephemeral` sandboxes live in another directory, so the depth differs (the test's chain missed there; other depths were not tried), and `vl bisect run` (sandboxes) was not run. (`vl status`'s "points to an isolated file" warning uses the same lexical resolution, `vfs::link_resolves_to`, and misses these too.) | `crates/veil-core/src/vfs/sandbox.rs:128-137` (`guard_link`) with `crates/veil-core/src/vfs/mod.rs:106-124` (`link_escapes` treats every component before `..` as a directory). Suggested fix: resolve relative targets against the tree being materialised, following links already in it (bounded), or check each link after the checkout with `openat`-based resolution beneath the sandbox root. | `adversarial9::symlinks::a_chain_of_links_never_reaches_the_projects_isolated_files` (control: `control_a_single_escaping_link_is_blocked`) |
| BUG-709 | high (**fixed**, Git-parity fix wave 3) | `ws exec` sandboxes / symlinks / isolated files | **Fixed:** a sandbox never materialises an absolute link target (`vfs::sandbox_link_check`; `with_forbidden_targets` now only names the project or `$VEIL_HOME` in the message). Documented in the guide and ARCHITECTURE; `/usr/share/…` links are refused too (a command that needs one can create it). Absolute link targets are materialised unless they lie, lexically, inside the project or `$VEIL_HOME`. A `/proc` magic link names the sandbox itself: `x -> /proc/self/cwd/../../../../../.env` is lexically `/.env` (allowed); for the reading command (cwd = the sandbox root under `ws exec`) it is the project's isolated `.env`. `/proc/self/root/…` and `/proc/<pid>/cwd/…` work too. One link in a teammate's tree is enough (Linux; macOS has no `/proc`, but `/dev/fd/N` is the analogue for open directories). | `crates/veil-core/src/vfs/sandbox.rs:138-151` (`guard_link`, absolute branch: lexical normalisation, then a prefix test against `forbidden`). Suggested fix: refuse absolute targets under `/proc` and `/dev/fd` (and any target whose resolution depends on the reader), or apply the post-checkout resolution check of BUG-708 to absolute targets too. | `adversarial9::symlinks::a_proc_self_link_never_reaches_the_projects_isolated_files` |
| BUG-710 | low (**fixed**, final fix wave) | Veil push / release tags | **Fixed:** `remote::accepted_tags` rejects any change of a release tag (annotated on either side) on a Veil remote unless `vl push --force-tags` (`SyncOptions::force_tags`, also for `--dry-run` and the `pre-push` plan); with it the release is replaced. The rejection says so, and the other tags are pushed. Originally: `vl push` of a release tag the user moved locally with `vl tag set --force` moved it on a Veil remote with no extra confirmation. Found by Git-parity fix wave 4. | `remote.rs` push of tags does not distinguish release tags. | `cli_adversarial::adversarial9::tags::a_veil_push_never_moves_a_release_tag_without_force_tags` |
| BUG-682 | high (**fixed**, Git-parity fix wave 2; pinned here by pass A; feature **removed**, format 8) | squash / cloaked lines / push | **Pinned** as a deterministic ignored test (the row above in "Findings from the local-tooling round" has the report). **Root cause:** the sealed `a.txt` the squash leaves in `sec@` has the never-published draft as its *fallback*, and the fallback is what a public push sends: a mirror clone's `vl cat -r s a.txt` prints `DRAFT-0002`. `commands.rs:654-656` (`squash` into a non-ancestor: `merge_into` → `merge::merge_trees`) yields a sealed entry whose fallback comes from `merge.rs:824-827` (`merged_visibility`/`public_other`: "the other side's public version" is the squashed-in draft); `embargo::seal_introduced` then keeps it ("Sealed content keeps its sealing"). BUG-707 is the same rule reached by `vl duplicate`. | (see the earlier row) | `adversarial9::embargo::a_draft_squashed_into_a_cloaked_change_stays_off_the_mirror` |

## Round 9 hypotheses (not demonstrated)

* **Merge-driver `%P` substitution.** `LocalDrivers::merge` and `textconv` (`attr_cmd.rs:487-508`) substitute the path raw into the device's argv. A driver defined Git-style as `["sh", "-c", "tool %A %O %B %P"]` (Git sq-quotes `%P`) runs a teammate's file name `x;touch pwned` as shell; a name starting with `-` reaches tools that permute options. Needs the user's own `sh -c` definition; not filed.
* **Recording under a sealed `.veilattributes`.** The BUG-705 class for recording: a member snapshotting a public CRLF file under a sealed `* text` records LF, a non-member CRLF. Design 7 threat 11 accepts conversion differences on checkout; the recorded-bytes case is the same one-bit leak and was not tested separately.
* **Rename detection cost per command.** Each detection is bounded (limit², 1 GiB hashed, 2^28 comparisons), but auto-rebase of many descendants, `log --follow` and `blame --follow` run it once per step, so a hostile change set multiplies the bound. Not timed.
* **`vl hooks` `files` and TOCTOU.** Files a command only reads (`files = […]`) are checked by digest before the hook starts, then read by the hook from the live tree (only the program and argv files are copied). A local race only.
* **Mount and relative links.** `MountFs` serves links as leaves and the kernel follows them from the mountpoint; a mountpoint inside the project lets `x -> ../.env` reach it. The mount promises nothing about escapes (design 6 threat 8 is about `MountFs` itself), so not filed.

Leads that held up (no bug): the no-follow writer (`vfs/beneath.rs`) for checkout, restore and sandbox writes; reserved names (`.veil` in any case or with HFS-ignorable characters) in checkout; `.veilpolicy` deletion followed by a file under its cloak (held back, `P`); cloaked annotated tags (kept cloaked over `--force`, never pushed public); release tags never auto-advance; escapes in symlink targets (every display path); bidi controls in `.veilhooks` as shown by `vl hooks trust`; CRLF conflicts resolved in a CRLF editor.

## Round 9 coverage notes

* **Hooks** (`adversarial9::hooks`): argument classification, directories, launchers; the trust record writers, environment, timeouts and `serve` output were left to `hooks_cli.rs`, which covers them.
* **Tags** (`adversarial9::tags`): Veil-remote pulls of annotated tags. Forged and transplanted atags are refused by `remote::fetch_annotation` and `annotation_problem` (read, and covered by `tag_object.rs` unit tests); not re-tested.
* **History tools** (`adversarial9::history`): `--public`; the member/outsider/mirror leak sweep of `history_cli.rs` already covers text, JSON and `serve` for blame, grep and log filters.
* **Embargo** (`adversarial9::embargo`): duplicate, squash (BUG-682).
* **Symlinks** (`adversarial9::symlinks`): sandbox materialisation. Case-folding (`VEIL_FS_FOLDING`) only changes Veil's name comparison on Linux, so the CVE-2021-21300 shape cannot be run for real here; macOS CI only.
* **Suite note (not filed):** `adversarial7::serve::silent_connections_never_lock_other_clients_out` failed four runs in a row at `4a62259` on this machine (`serve.rs:206`, "the 33rd client was served at once"), then passed in the next full run. It is timing-dependent; `serve.rs` last changed in the colocated merge (`217f997`).
* **Not covered:** format 4/5 mixing beyond `format_compat.rs`, the FUSE mount with links, `vl renormalize`, rename-aware merge property tests (stream R's).

## Findings from the local-tooling round

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## Findings from the colocated and final fix waves (native code)

The colocated Git mode and its findings (BUG-740 to BUG-756, BUG-759,
BUG-760) were removed with Git interop. Three findings of those waves are
about native code:

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-757 | low (**fixed**, final fix wave) | `vl restore` | **Fixed:** only a *cloaked* (sealed) file restored into a deleted directory failed: the restored entry is carried into the snapshot with `conflict::set_entry`, which refused a path whose directory the working copy's tree no longer had. It now creates missing directories (and removing under one is a no-op). `vl restore <path>` of a file whose directory was deleted (`rm -r`) puts the file back, then fails with `error: not found: <path>` (exit 1). | Not investigated; the restore itself succeeds, a later step reports the path missing. | `cli_basic::working_copy_cli::restoring_a_cloaked_file_into_a_deleted_directory_works` |
| BUG-758 | low (**fixed**, final fix wave) | tests | **Fixed:** the bound is 2× + 0.1 s over the best of five runs each (the quadratic regression it guards against is far slower than 2×). `core_engine::attributes::a_megabyte_of_patterns_slows_a_snapshot_little` asserts a 1.2× timing ratio and fails under full-suite load (206 ms → 399 ms seen twice); passes alone. Needs a looser bound, or a work counter instead of wall time. | timing assertion | the test itself |
| BUG-761 | critical (**fixed**, final fix wave; feature **removed**, format 8) | embargo / publication | **Fixed:** a line under an unreleased embargo stays under it. The builder keeps the wrapper even when the tree holds nothing to hide (`embargo::wrap_line`), and a working-copy tree that is not wrapped while an ancestor is an unreleased embargoed merge is wrapped at its next change, with its parent's public view as the fallback (`Repo::keep_line_embargo`, in every recording path: the snapshot, `build_amending_carried`, `commit_fs_as_child_with`); without the embargo's key, the edits are held back as before. Found in the final fix wave. Plain Veil: `vl merge fix@- --into main --embargo`, `vl new main`, delete the fix's file (`auth.rs`), any `vl` command, put the file back, `vl commit`, `vl tag set main -r @-`, `vl push` to a public remote: a clone of the remote has the fix in plaintext before the release. | `embargo::wrap` returns the bare fallback when the tree equals it, so a snapshot that deleted the fix's files took the working copy off the embargo; the builder judged "under embargo" by the parent *tree* only (`keep_embargo`), so a later re-add was a public edit. | `core_engine::embargo_release::deleting_and_putting_back_the_fix_never_publishes_it` |

## Findings from the scale round (benchmark baseline)

Baseline: `integration` @ `b549e1e`, measured by `crates/veil-bench` (`scripts/bench-scale.sh 1g`). Numbers and causes: `docs/design/scale/BASELINE.md`.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-800 | medium (**fixed**, BUG-800 streaming wave) | store / memory | **Fixed:** the write and transfer side now streams (`docs/design/scale/4-background-cleanup-streaming.md`, G1). `StorageBackend::create_stream` writes a file in pieces and names it only at `finish` (disk: `O_EXCL` temp, fsync, rename, directory fsync; SFTP: pipelined writes, `fsync@openssh.com`, `posix-rename`; others buffer). `pack::PackWriter` writes `PackBuilder`'s exact bytes as objects come, keeping only the entry table and delta depths, and computes the trailer's BLAKE3 while streaming. gc and `PackSink` (pull and push) use it; sync's last versions per path are a 64 MiB LRU. A pull spools the remote's packs to `.veil/objects/incoming/pull` instead of memory, after checking free space (the error names the space needed). Publish order (`.pack`, then `.idx`, then the op head), the received objects' checks and the format are unchanged. Measured on the 1 GB benchmark: clone 367 MiB (SFTP 424), pull of one snapshot 171 MiB, pull with nothing new 139 MiB, gc 297 MiB first and 467 MiB again, initial push 220 MiB (SFTP 341): every case under the 512 MiB gate (`docs/design/scale/BASELINE.md`). Tests: `scale_findings::a_clone_of_a_large_store_does_not_hold_it_in_memory` (peak +33 MiB for a 195 MiB store; +402 MiB before), `durability::an_interrupted_pack_write_leaves_no_visible_pack`, `durability::pulled_packs_are_published_before_their_index_and_the_operation`, `sftp_backend::streams_are_published_whole_and_only_on_finish`, `sync_incremental::a_pull_that_would_fill_the_disk_says_how_much_it_needs`. Earlier, **partly fixed (orchestrator review):** the 1 GB benchmark after this wave measured `vl clone` at 12.1 s and **1,205 MiB** (was 54.5 s, 1,975 MiB), still over the 512 MiB gate. `pull` and `gc` were not measured: the run stopped early. What remains is the write and transfer side: `PullSource::prefetch` downloads remote packs into memory, and `PackBuilder` holds each pack whole before writing (`docs/design/scale/4-background-cleanup-streaming.md`, G1). It is next, as its own stream. What this wave did: packs held whole share one process-wide budget (`pack::SLURP_BUDGET`, 64 MiB, least recently used out; each store holds its own copy, so `--full` walks still read the remote's bytes), and only packs of at most 16 MiB are held whole (several hot packs fit; larger ones are always read by range, so they never thrash). `gc` closes a pack every 128 MiB of kept objects (`gc::GC_PACK_LIMIT`; `GcReport::packs`), so its builder never holds the whole store; pull and clone already split at 128 MiB. Measured with `scripts/bench-scale.sh 1g`: see `docs/design/scale/BASELINE.md`. **Memory grows with the store.** At 1 GB, `vl clone` peaks at 1.98 GB RSS, `vl gc` at 1.0 GB, `vl pull` of one snapshot at 950 MB, and `vl pull` with nothing new at 560 MB. Each is over the scale gate's 512 MiB, and they grow linearly with the store. Reading every object of a 117 MiB store in 4 packs grows RSS by 117 MiB. | `crates/veil-core/src/pack.rs:415` (`Pack::whole`) keeps a whole pack in memory once it has served 64 reads (`SLURP_AFTER`), for any pack of at most 256 MiB (`SLURP_LIMIT`). The limit is per pack, and nothing bounds the total, so any history walk (pull, clone, gc, log) ends up holding every pack. Suggested fix: one store-wide budget for slurped packs (LRU), or ranged reads only. | `crates/veil-core/tests/scale_findings.rs::reading_every_pack_does_not_hold_the_whole_store_in_memory` |
| BUG-801 | medium (**fixed**, final fix wave) | locking / concurrency | **Fixed:** `Repo::snapshot_workspace_with` scans the working copy before taking `.veil/lock`, with every new object staged in memory (`ObjectStore::begin_staging`, at most 32 MiB; an object already on disk is not adopted either, since another transaction may not have made it durable yet: stream O's threat 5). A clean working copy is then recorded without the lock at all. A changed one takes the lock, checks that the workspace, the accepted policy and the scanner's rules are what the scan saw, and publishes the staged objects (under the lock, unsynced, made durable by the transaction's barrier) with the tree; if anything moved, or the content was too large to stage, it builds again under the lock as before. Not stream O: the lock, the wait and the op log are unchanged; O replaces the in-memory staging with on-disk staging and the lock with a compare-and-swap of the op heads. Tests: the reproduction (64 concurrent `vl status` on 20,000 files) passes in about 4 s; `core_durability::adversarial_lock::racing_snapshots_of_one_directory_lose_nothing` (8 handles editing and snapshotting one directory: no fork, no lost edit) and `…::a_clean_snapshot_needs_no_lock_and_large_content_still_records`. **Concurrent commands fail instead of queueing.** Every `vl` command holds `.veil/lock` while it records the project directory. With enough commands at once, contenders give up after 15 s with `repository is locked: another process holds the lock`. The 64-agent profile at 1 GB failed 194 of 640 iterations (`ws exec`, `tag set` and `push` alike), and 64 concurrent `vl status` on 20,000 files fail 15 times. Nothing is lost, but agents and editors see random failures. | `crates/veil-core/src/workspace.rs:280` (`Repo::snapshot_workspace_with` opens its transaction, which takes the lock, before `build_tree_cached` scans the working copy at `:312`), together with `crates/veil-core/src/lock.rs:197` (`LockOptions::default().wait` = 15 s; polling with back-off, no fairness). The scale round's stream O (lock-free op log, compare-and-swap snapshot) removes the cause; a longer or fair wait would only hide it. | `crates/vl/tests/scale_findings_cli.rs::many_concurrent_commands_all_succeed`, `core_durability::adversarial_lock::racing_snapshots_of_one_directory_lose_nothing` |

## Scale round: adversarial pass on stream O

Branch `scale-o-oplog` @ `0c8fce4` merged into `integration` @ `58197ca`. Every finding below is a race between the member's own legitimate commands (an agent's `ws exec`, a long pull, `vl gc`, the human's commands): no attacker outside the trust boundary is needed, and none is involved. Reproductions: `core_durability::oplog_adversarial`.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-802 | high (**fixed**, stream O adversarial fixes) | op log / undo | **Fixed:** `Repo::merge_plan` skips a side's rewrite whose old snapshot that side still shows, as a ref or as an ancestor of one (one walk from the side's refs down to the oldest rewritten snapshot, `GraphIndex::down_to`). **A merge re-applies a rewrite that its side undid.** `vl abandon X` (or `vl describe X`, any amend of a parent of `@`) then `vl undo` in one workspace, while any other command lands concurrently (an agent's commit, a `vl tag set`): the merge of the two heads applies the undone abandon again, so `@` loses X again (or moves onto the amended X the user undid). Recoverable from the op log, but the user's undo silently disappears. | `crates/veil-core/src/repo.rs` `Repo::merge_plan`: a side's rewrite is skipped as "undone since" only when its old snapshot is a *direct* ref of the side's view (`refs.contains(&old)`); an undone rewrite of a parent of `@` leaves the old snapshot visible only as an ancestor. | `undone_abandon_is_not_redone_by_a_concurrent_merge`, `undone_amend_is_not_redone_by_a_concurrent_merge` |
| BUG-803 | high (**fixed**, stream O adversarial fixes) | op log / merge | **Fixed:** `OpStore::split` has no time floor: it walks common operations too (the classic merge-base walk, ancestors of a common operation marked stale and never taken as the base) and stops once everything still queued is common, so it finds the fork point however old, and stays cheap (a timestamp out of order, a merge's, no longer sends one side to the root). The floor stays in `drop_ancestors`, where a miss only costs an extra merge. **The merge base is lost when one side's history spans more than an hour.** A command that runs over an hour (a long pull, a suspended `vl`, a clock step) lands beside a line of commands that started right after it: `OpStore::split` stops an hour below the newest heads, finds no common operation, and the merge runs against an empty view. Tags and workspaces deleted on one side come back (and the next `vl push` publishes a withdrawn tag), concurrent moves of every tag and workspace become conflicts decided by operation id (the stale side's values win about half of the time), and snapshots abandoned on one side become visible again. | `crates/veil-core/src/oplog.rs` `OpStore::split`: the walk stops expanding at `ts < floor` (oldest start minus `ANCESTRY_SLACK_MS`), so the operation the long command forked from is reached by one side only; `merge_plan` then uses `View::default()` as the base. | `merge_base_is_found_below_the_ancestry_slack` |
| BUG-804 | medium (**fixed**, stream O adversarial fixes) | op log / gc | **Fixed:** `Transaction::recheck` fails the commit ("… was pruned by `vl gc --keep-ops-days` while it ran; run the command again") when an operation of its base is gone. **An operation whose base was pruned meanwhile is merged against an empty view.** A long command starts from operation H; two commands land, and `vl gc --keep-ops-days 0` (which keeps only the heads and their parents) prunes H; the long command then publishes an operation whose parent is gone. Its merge with the heads has no common operation: same harm as BUG-803 (here, a deleted tag returns). Threat 13 assumed unknown ancestry only costs "a harmless extra merge". | `crates/veil-core/src/transaction.rs` `Transaction::recheck` does not check that the base still exists; `merge_plan` falls back to `View::default()`. | `transaction_whose_base_was_pruned_does_not_merge_against_nothing` |
| BUG-805 | medium (**fixed**, stream O adversarial fixes) | op log / embargo | **Fixed:** in a merge transaction, a policy refusal of `Repo::rebase_tree` leaves that snapshot (and what is built on it) on its old parents, and the merge warns "change … was left on its old parents: moving it onto a concurrent rewrite was refused (…)"; the confinement check still runs on the result. **A merge whose rebase is refused makes every later command fail.** One side moves change Y onto an embargoed merge (Y joins embargo E1); the other merges a second fix into Y's line under its own embargo E2. Each is allowed on its own view. The merge's rebase of E2's merge onto the moved Y combines two unreleased embargoes, which `merge_trees` refuses with a policy error; every command merges the heads first (at `Repo::open`), so every command fails from then on, `vl embargo release` (which the error suggests) and `vl op restore` included. Threat 18 covered only confinement failures. | `crates/veil-core/src/transaction.rs` `Transaction::rebase_descendants` propagates the policy error of `Repo::rebase_tree` out of `merge_rebase`, `Transaction::commit` and `Repo::settle`. | `merge_combining_two_embargoes_degrades_instead_of_failing_forever` |
| BUG-806 | medium (**fixed**, stream O adversarial fixes) | op log / compare-and-swap | **Fixed:** for a transaction that recorded a working copy (`expect_wc`), `recheck` and `OpStore::publish` run under one process-wide mutex, so a second recorder in the process checks only after the first published, and is superseded (and builds again). Across processes the CLI's recorders already hold the workspace's `WcLock` for the whole recording. **Two recorders of one workspace in one process both publish.** The compare-and-swap is a check (`Transaction::recheck`) followed by a separate publication: A passes the check; B scans (seeing A's edit and its own), checks and publishes; A then publishes over the workspace B moved. The merge keeps one working copy (by operation id) and warns; the other's edit is only in a visible change. Threads of one process (an embedder, `vl serve` paths that do not serialise, a mount's threads) are not kept apart by `WcLock`, which is re-entrant within a process. BUG-801's `adversarial_lock::racing_snapshots_of_one_directory_lose_nothing` failed this way 2 times in 120 runs under load (0 in 150 after the fix). | `crates/veil-core/src/transaction.rs` `Transaction::commit`: `recheck()` and `ops.publish()` are not atomic, and nothing serialises them between threads. | `two_recorders_of_one_workspace_never_both_publish` |

## Scale round: stream P (two-phase pushes)

Found while building stream P, from the hypothesis "push racing an undo of a staged embargo release" (stream-O adversarial pass). Like stream O's findings, a race between the member's own commands: no attacker is needed. Reproductions: `core_sync::push_two_phase`.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-807 | high (**fixed**, stream P) | push / embargo | **Fixed:** phase B of the two-phase push, under the remote's lock, first makes the staged releases permanent (`publish_staged_releases`), then checks that every embargo the plan treated as released (recorded with the staged packs) still is. If one is not, the staging is removed under the lock and the push plans again, withholding the fix. A push journal staged under a release that was taken back since is discarded with its staging. `vl push --full` makes the staged releases permanent before it decides what to withhold. Residual: bytes uploaded to the push's staging before the undo reached the remote's disk (removed under the lock; unreadable through vl), as an aborted upload always did. **A push racing an undo of a staged release publishes the embargoed fix.** `vl embargo release E`, then `vl push` to a public remote, and while it uploads, `vl undo` of the release in another terminal: the undo succeeds (the release was only staged), the push still sends the fix's history (plaintext, here), and the remote's record of E stays unreleased. Readers of the public mirror get the fix before its release. Predates stream O: push never held the local lock. | `crates/veil-core/src/remote.rs` `push_with`: `sendable_tags` computes the withheld history from the release state at the start (`Keychain::embargo_released` counts a staged release); the objects are sent; only then `publish_staged_releases` makes the releases permanent, skipping one taken back meanwhile. Nothing checks that what was treated as released still is. | `push_racing_an_undo_of_a_staged_release_never_publishes_the_fix`, `a_published_fix_always_comes_with_its_released_record`, `nothing_withheld_is_ever_staged` (property: releases and take-backs between the phases) |

## Scale round: adversarial pass on stream P

Adversarial pass on the two-phase push (`t3/a6c6d6aa` @ `167ffc1` merged onto `integration` @ `beeff23`). Attackers: readers of a public mirror (they read whatever a push publishes there, and can never be made to forget it); the trigger for BUG-808 and BUG-809 is a sequence of the member's own commands. Reproductions: `core_sync::push_two_phase`.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-808 | high (**fixed**, stream P adversarial fixes) | push / resume / embargo | **Fixed:** the walk takes each reused object out of the set of staged ones as it meets it (`ResumeSource`); if anything is left and the plan sends something, the staging is removed and the attempt plans again with a new journal. A plan that sends nothing publishes nothing, so a team push rejected meanwhile still keeps its staging for the merge. **A resumed push publishes packs that an earlier attempt staged and that its own plan no longer sends.** A push to a public mirror (tag `wip` at a change) is interrupted after staging. The member then finds the change is a security fix: they delete `wip` and merge the change under an embargo instead. The next `vl push` resumes the journal (same remote and tags) and renames every staged pack into `objects/pack/`, so the embargoed fix's plaintext is published on the mirror, beside a plan that withholds it. The same happens for a change withdrawn or cloaked between attempts, and on team remotes. | `crates/veil-core/src/remote.rs` `push_with`: `Journal::reusable` counts every journalled pack whose files are whole, and `publish` renames all of them. The journal is discarded only when `public` or the released embargoes changed, not when the plan changed (the design sketch: "The staged packs are still reused as known objects if they are present"). | `a_resumed_push_never_publishes_what_its_plan_no_longer_sends`; `a_rejected_team_push_reuses_its_staging_after_the_merge` (reuse kept) |
| BUG-809 | high (**fixed**, stream P adversarial fixes) | push / embargo | **Fixed:** `sendable_tags` reads the release state once (`released_embargoes`) and decides the withheld history from that reading (`embargo_withheld_given`). It returns the reading, which becomes the journal's `released`, the list phase B checks after `publish_staged_releases`. **BUG-807's fix checked a different reading of the release state from the one the plan was made from.** `push_with` decided the withheld history from live reads of the keychain inside `sendable_tags`, then read the released embargoes again for the journal. A `vl undo` of a staged release in another terminal, landing between the two reads, left the fix unwithheld and unchecked, so it was published on the public mirror (the third read of the release state in the sweep). The same happened for a release and its undo, both during the push. | `crates/veil-core/src/remote.rs` `push_with` (before the fix): `sendable_tags` → `embargo_withheld` called `Keychain::embargo_released` live; `released_embargoes` ran afterwards. | `an_undo_landing_anywhere_in_phase_a_never_publishes_the_fix`, `a_release_and_its_undo_during_phase_a_never_publish_the_fix` (sweeps over every read of the release state) |
| BUG-810 | low (**fixed**, scale stream G2) | push / staging expiry | **Fixed:** expiry no longer compares another machine's clock with ours: each host keeps, on the remote, when it first saw each staging directory with its current `touched` stamp (`.veil/local/push-expiry/<host>.json`, by its own clock), and removes one only after it has seen it unchanged for a week of that clock. A live push rewrites `touched` whenever it writes, so its week starts again; hostile or skewed stamps are only compared for equality. **A pusher whose clock is more than a week fast removes other pushes' live staging.** Expiry compares the `touched` stamp, written with the staging pusher's clock, with the clock of whoever cleans up (every push, without the lock, and gc). The fast-clock member's push removes the staging of every push in phase A at the time. Each of those pushes then plans and uploads again, and fails after three attempts ("what was uploaded is kept for it", although it was removed). No data is lost. A clock that slow on the staging side has the same effect. | `crates/veil-core/src/remote.rs` `expire_push_staging`: `now.saturating_sub(t) > PUSH_STAGING_EXPIRY_MS` with `t` from another machine's clock. SFTP metadata carries no server-side time (`Metadata::unstamped`) to compare with instead. | `a_teammate_with_a_fast_clock_never_removes_a_live_push_staging` (un-ignored), `only_week_old_staging_is_removed` (changed to the new rule) |
| BUG-811 | critical (**fixed**, scale stream G2; **pre-existing**: reproduces on `integration` @ `beeff23`) | local gc / lock-free op log | **Fixed:** on a lock-free repository gc deletes only under a fence (`crates/veil-core/src/maintenance.rs`). Every lock-free transaction registers itself (`objects/incoming/live-<owner>`) before it reads the heads and until it has published its operation; gc raises `.veil/local/gc/fence` only once none is registered (waiting up to 5 s for a quiet moment, then up to 60 s with the fence raised; otherwise it deletes nothing that run and says so), and transactions that meet a raised fence wait for it. Under the fence gc reads its roots again (the head files, every operation, keep markers, the checkout pointer) and marks what they reach; only the first listing's operations are pruned, and an object that became reachable survives (with a loose copy if its old pack goes). A commit also checks that every object its view gained and its new objects name is still present ("… was removed by `vl gc` while the command ran; run the command again"), for ids carried from an older view. gc takes the gc lease (`.veil/gc.lock`, an `flock`) instead of `.veil/lock` there, so a recording that writes metadata under `.veil/lock` never waits for gc. `--prune-now` is safe beside running commands (help and guide changed). Load run: 12 copies × 8 rounds, 96/96 pass (63/96 on `2a337ee`, the same binary and load). **`vl gc --keep-ops-days 0 --prune-now` racing `vl ws exec` loses objects under load.** Found while verifying this pass; not in stream P's code. `cli_conformance::conformance_sync::gc::gc_concurrent_with_commits_loses_nothing` passes alone (0/30) but fails when 12 copies run at once: 31/96 on `beeff23`, 44/96 with stream P. Seen: `vl fsck` reports "directory snapshot …:/: tree … is missing" (a recorded snapshot's tree pruned); and `ws exec` / `ws exec --record-only` failing with "object … is not present in this store". Also (found by the deterministic reproduction): the operation itself pruned, written but not yet named by its head file, so the head names a missing operation. | `crates/veil-core/src/gc.rs` `gc`, against `Transaction::commit` (`transaction.rs`): a transaction renames its staged objects into the store (`ObjectStore::publish_tx_staging`) and only then writes its operation and head file. A gc whose inventory came after the renames and whose head read came before the head file saw those objects (and the operation file) as unreachable and deleted them at once with grace 0. The same for an object the transaction found already present and reused, and for an operation older than the window that is about to become a head. Marking what live staging directories reference covered only objects still staged. | `core_durability::gc_concurrency::{prune_now_never_deletes_what_a_transaction_published_but_not_named, prune_now_never_deletes_an_object_a_transaction_reused, prune_now_beside_many_writers_loses_nothing, a_tag_carried_from_an_older_view_is_checked_at_commit}` (fail on `2a337ee`), `…::{prune_now_never_deletes_what_a_transaction_is_renaming, a_transaction_whose_base_gc_prunes_records_whole_or_refuses, gc_on_a_thread_holding_a_transaction_defers_deletion}`; `gc_concurrent_with_commits_loses_nothing` (un-ignored; 12 × 8 under load) |

## Scale round: stream G2 (incremental maintenance)

Found while building G2 (scale stream G2). Reproductions: `cli_adversarial::adversarial_cli_crash`.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-812 | low (**fixed**, scale stream G2; **pre-existing**: fails 66/96 on `2a337ee` under 12 × 8 load, about half the runs alone) | push / crash debris | **Fixed:** the journal is written as a stream, whose temp file names its process (`.stream-<owner>.vltmp…`); a push that publishes removes the temp files in `.veil/local/push/` whose writer is provably gone, and every gc removes those (and owner-less ones a day old), never a live push's. **A push killed while writing its journal leaves a temp file in `.veil/local/push/` for ever.** `kill_9_mid_push_never_publishes_part_of_it_and_the_next_push_resumes` asserts the directory is empty after the resumed push, and failed whenever a kill landed in a journal write (`.<hash>.vltmp<8 hex>` left). No data is lost; the journal is read by its exact name. Under load the same test also fails 8–10/96 on `only N pushes were actually killed` (its kill timing, before and after): a test-timing limit, not a product bug. | `crates/veil-core/src/remote.rs` `Journal::save` wrote with `StorageBackend::write` (an atomic temp file that names no owner), and nothing ever removed such leftovers. | `adversarial_cli_crash::kill_9_mid_push_never_publishes_part_of_it_and_the_next_push_resumes` (86/96 under load, the 10 failures all the kill-timing check; was 30/96), `maintenance::stale_temp_files_go_but_never_a_live_writers` |
| BUG-813 | medium (**removed**, SFTP remotes removed) | clone / memory | **Removed:** SFTP and path remotes, and the clones over them, were removed in the simplification pass, part 1; the scale benchmark no longer has SFTP or path-remote cases. Clones go through the Veil server, whose writer (`remote::Incoming`) is checked for bounded memory by `scale_findings::a_clone_of_a_large_store_does_not_hold_it_in_memory`. **`vl clone` of the 1 GB benchmark store over SFTP peaks at 573 MiB, over the scale gate's 512 MiB** (by path 452 MiB). The G1 wave measured 424 and 367 MiB (`BASELINE.md`, BUG-800); streams O and P merged since, and no 1 GB run was recorded after them. Not in G2: the G2 branch measures 565 and 449 MiB on the same profile. | Not yet located: clone after the lock-free op log (stream O) and the two-phase push (stream P). | `scripts/bench-scale.sh 1g` (`clone, full`); a test-sized reproduction is still owed |

## Scale round: adversarial pass on stream G2

Adversarial pass on incremental maintenance and the BUG-811 deletion fence (`t3/2b0ff3fc` @ `529a22c` merged onto `integration` @ `2a337ee`). gc is the one component that deletes, so the attacker is the member's own concurrent commands (agents run many at once) and crashes: the trigger is always a sequence of the member's own commands, a `kill -9` or an I/O error. Reproductions: `core_durability::gc_concurrency`.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-814 | critical (**fixed**, stream G2 adversarial fixes) | gc / remotes / deletion fence | **Fixed:** with its fence raised and no transaction registered, gc also takes `.veil/lock` before it marks again and deletes (`maintenance::raise_fence`); if it cannot within the lock's usual wait, it deletes nothing that run and says so. Writers that take `.veil/lock` instead of registering are then finished (their operation is a head, read by the second marking) or not started. **`vl -R ../hub gc --prune-now` deletes what a push over SSH is publishing.** The guide's way to collect a remote opens the bare hub as a lock-free repository, so G2's gc takes the gc lease and the fence, not `.veil/lock` (which it held for the whole run before). A push over SSH (a network backend: no staging, so no registration; it holds only `.veil/lock`) that has renamed its packs into `pack/` and not yet written its operation is invisible to the fence: gc drops those packs as garbage and the push then records a tag whose history is gone on the hub (`Missing` reading the pushed file, fsck: missing snapshot). The same for every transaction over a network backend and every writer that holds `.veil/lock` without registering. A push over a path registers and was safe (the control). | `crates/veil-core/src/maintenance.rs` `raise_fence` excluded only registered transactions; `GcHold::take` decides by `Repo::lock_free()`, which is true for a bare repository opened as a project, while its pushers use the remote protocol (`.veil/lock`). | `gc_concurrency::hub_gc_never_deletes_what_a_push_over_ssh_is_publishing` (fails on `529a22c`; 96/96 under 12 × 8 load with the fix) |
| BUG-815 | high (**fixed**, stream G2 adversarial fixes) | gc / prune epoch / crash | **Fixed:** the prune epoch moves under the fence before anything is deleted, not after; a spurious move only makes commands check what they carried. **A gc that dies after deleting (`kill -9`, EIO, a full disk) leaves the prune epoch unchanged, so a command carrying an id from its older view records it unchecked.** BUG-811's fix checks carried ids (a revision `vl tag set` resolved before gc ran, a long command's base) only when the epoch changed since the command read its view, and gc wrote the epoch as its last step. A gc killed or failing anywhere between its first deletion and that write (removing pruned operations, loose objects, packs, keep markers, writing the pending list) made the next such commit record a tag (or a parent) that names a deleted snapshot. | `crates/veil-core/src/gc.rs` `prune_store`: `bump_prune_epoch` after step 7, conditioned on what was deleted. | `gc_concurrency::a_gc_that_dies_after_deleting_still_makes_carried_ids_checked` (fails on `529a22c`; 96/96 under 12 × 8 load with the fix) |
| BUG-816 | low (**removed**, SFTP remotes removed) | push staging / hub gc | **Removed:** the two-phase push to plain-file remotes, its staging and its expiry were removed with SFTP and path remotes in the simplification pass, part 1. Seen in the stream G2 adversarial pass, not filed there. Hub gc no longer holds `.veil/lock` for its whole run, so it can remove a push's staging directory (untouched for a week) while that push is publishing. The push fails and uploads again; nothing is lost. | Expiry of `push-*` staging runs during hub gc outside the lock that phase B holds. Suggested fix: skip staging whose journal shows a phase-B publish in progress, or expire staging only under `.veil/lock`. | none yet |

## Hub milestone S1

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-817 | low (**fixed**, Hub S1; **pre-existing**: `integration` @ `cbfe9ee`) | docs / guide | **Fixed:** `docs/guide/embargoes.md` line 10 showed the planned `vl merge … --embargo-for … --until …` as a runnable command, so `guide_commands_parse` failed on `integration`. It is now marked as planned, with placeholders the check skips. | The docs rewrite (`8a585eb`) added a command of a later milestone without the placeholder syntax that marks it | `vl` `docgen::guide_commands_parse` |
| BUG-818 | medium (**fixed**, BUG-818 fix; **pre-existing**: `integration` @ `cbfe9ee`) | checkout / speed | **Fixed:** checkout stages every file beside its path (`StorageBackend::write_staged`: a temp file, unsynced, with the mode set on it), makes them all durable with one barrier (the object store's parallel `fsync`s), renames them into place, and makes the names and its removals durable with a second barrier; only then does it return and the caller save `checkout.json`. A power loss leaves each path old or new, never torn; an EIO barrier fails the checkout with the move still pending, and finishing it rewrites every file already holding the target; resuming removes the staged debris. The 10k clone went from 10,044 to 1,014 ms through the server (10,363 to 1,178 ms from a path remote), with 10,155 `fsync`s instead of 20,154 (`docs/design/scale/OPENSCION.md`). `syncfs` was 0.2 s faster on a quiet disk but was not used: before Linux 5.8 it does not report writeback errors, and it waits for every other process's dirty data. **Checkout makes every file durable on its own**: a temporary file, `fsync`, rename, `fsync` of the directory, so a 10,001-file clone makes 20,154 `fsync`s, 7.3 s of a 9.4 s clone on an ext4 NVMe disk (`strace -f -w -c`). Cloning through the server and from a path remote take the same time (`docs/design/scale/OPENSCION.md`); the OpenScion "clone everything" gate (544 ms) is out of reach until checkout batches its durability. `OVERVIEW.md`'s 483 ms does not reproduce on this machine. | `checkout` writes through the durable `StorageBackend::write` per file instead of `write_unsynced` plus one barrier | `veil-bench openscion` (the "Veil, path remote" column); `core_engine` `checkout_speed::checkout_of_many_files_is_one_barrier_not_one_fsync_per_file`; `core_durability` `crash::crash_during_workspace_switch` (power loss tears staged copies), `crash::eio_during_workspace_switch` |
| BUG-830 | medium (**fixed**, post-S1 fix round; **pre-existing**: `integration` @ `cbfe9ee`; found by the BUG-818 fix) | clone / crash | **Fixed:** both clones check out through `Repo::check_out_new`, which journals the checkout like every other move (`move_working_copy` from the root snapshot: `pending` before the first file), so the next command finishes it. A `--only` clone journals from the target tree without its directory (`Materialised::shown_as`): finishing it writes only that directory, whose objects are the only ones fetched. The reproduction now kills a real `vl clone` (path and `--only` through the server) as soon as its checkout starts. **A `vl clone` killed during its checkout is recorded as deleting the files it had not written.** Clone writes `.veil/` and the new change, checks the files out, and saves `checkout.json` only afterwards. Killed in between (`kill -9`, power loss), the next command finds no record, takes the directory for one holding its change, and records every file not yet written as deleted (`vl status` shows them as `D`). Both clones (`main.rs` from a path, `hub_cmd.rs` from the server). | The clone's checkout is not journalled (`working_copy` does it for every other move: `pending` before the first file). A plain journal does not fit `--only`, whose tree is a restriction with no snapshot to resume to. | `cli_sync` `clone_crash_cli::a_clone_killed_during_its_checkout_never_records_deletions`; `server_hub` `sync::a_one_directory_clone_killed_during_its_checkout_is_finished` |
| BUG-831 | low (**fixed**, post-S1 fix round; **pre-existing**: `integration` @ `cbfe9ee`; found by the BUG-818 fix) | tests / flaky | **Fixed:** the liveness half no longer depends on the scheduler: once the swapper stops, a second checkout must complete the whole tree; the safety half (nothing outside, no abort) is checked on every attempt as before. 96 of 96 runs pass, 32 at a time beside 16 busy loops. **`symlinks::a_directory_swapped_for_a_link_mid_checkout_receives_nothing_outside` fails under load on its liveness half** ("no attempt wrote anything"): once in a full `verify-fast` run; 22 of 96 on `ab4304a` and 7 of 96 with the BUG-818 fix, run 32 at a time beside 16 busy loops. Its safety half (nothing written outside) held in every run. | A swapper thread that wins every race, on a loaded machine, makes every one of the 400 writes refused in all 5 attempts: a liveness assertion that depends on scheduling. | `core_working_copy` `symlinks::a_directory_swapped_for_a_link_mid_checkout_receives_nothing_outside` (under load) |
| BUG-832 | high (**fixed**, adversarial pass on the post-S1 fixes; **pre-existing**: `integration` @ `b78e52d`; found by the post-S1 fix round) | clone `--only` / secrets | **Fixed:** a sealed entry is kept as recorded against a rule that isolates or ignores its path, unless this snapshot's own edit of `.veilpolicy` reclassifies it (`Builder::keeps_sealing`); nothing of the local file is recorded (under such a rule it may be the member's own: sealing it would hand it to the scope, `adversarial::a_view_without_the_policy_never_seals_a_local_only_file`), so an edit there stays local and `vl status` says so. A `.veilpolicy` the working copy never showed (`--only`) is not a policy change. **A clone without the project's `.veilpolicy` on disk records the folder's secret as deleted, and its next push deletes it for everyone.** Not only `vl clone --only DIR`: a whole clone by anyone who cannot read the root `.veilpolicy` (the project root is private to admins by default) too. `vl status` shows `D DIR/.env [cloaked:team]` and `L DIR/.env [isolated]`. | The working copy's policy is what classifies files; without the project's `.veilpolicy` it is the built-in rules, which keep `.env` local-only (`isolated`): the builder returned no entry for an isolated path (`plain_file_entry`), so the sealed entry counted as deleted. "Sealed content keeps its audience" (`Builder::audience`) covered public classifications, not isolated or ignored ones. An `--only` clone also took its never-materialised `.veilpolicy` for a deleted one. | `server_hub` `sync::bug_832_a_one_directory_clone_keeps_the_folders_secrets` (fin `--only`, fin whole, admin `--only`), `adversarial::a_view_without_the_policy_never_seals_a_local_only_file` |

## Server S1: adversarial pass

Independent adversarial pass on milestone S1 (`t3/7a33597b` @ `ab4304a` merged onto `integration` @ `c3e425a`): the server is the one thing between private code and the internet, so attackers are anyone on Earth, a signed-up stranger, a participant without a folder, the operator of another Veil server, and (for secrets only, HUB.md §13.6) a compromised server. Reproductions: `veil-server` `server_hub::adversarial` (Docker: `scripts/verify-server.sh`), `vl` `cli_security::policy_cli`. Fixes: "server S1 adversarial fixes".

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-819 | critical (**fixed**, server S1 adversarial fixes) | sign-in / relay | **Fixed:** sign-in also requires the server name the device signed for to be one this server's TLS certificate is valid for (`tls::names`, rustls-webpki: the check the client made before it signed). **Another Veil server's operator signs in as anyone who signs in to theirs.** Attacker: the operator of server E. When a victim's `vl` signs in to E (`vl login`, or silently after any 401 E answers), E asks this server for a challenge for the victim's key and hands it to the victim, whose `vl` signs `E-name ‖ challenge`; E sends that login here with `Host: E-name`. The only check was `hub == Host`, and the relaying server writes both. Harm: a session as the victim: every private folder, push, group changes. | `crates/veil-server/src/auth.rs` `login` compared the signed name with the request's `Host` header, which the caller controls | `server_hub::adversarial::bug_819_a_login_relayed_by_another_server_is_refused`, `veil-server` `tls::tests::names_are_the_certificates_own` |
| BUG-820 | high (**fixed**, server S1 adversarial fixes) | push / write access / merges | **Fixed:** a path the pusher may not write is accepted from a merge's other parent only where that parent's side changed it since a common ancestor of the two (`sync::common_ancestor`, walked only when needed); taking the version the first parent replaced is a write. **Anyone in the project reverts any file they can read but not write.** Attacker: a participant who reads a public folder (`src/  public devs`) but is not in `devs`. They push a merge of the tip and an older snapshot whose tree takes the older version of `src/lib.rs` (a security fix undone); "taken unchanged from another parent" let it through, and `.veilpolicy`'s non-access lines the same way. | `crates/veil-server/src/sync.rs` `check_changes`: `from_other` accepted any path equal to another parent's, and the pusher chooses the parents | `server_hub::adversarial::bug_820_a_merge_cannot_revert_a_file_the_pusher_may_not_write` (also checks that merging a dev's branch still lands) |
| BUG-821 | high (**fixed**, server S1 adversarial fixes) | permissions / names | **Fixed:** creating a group rebinds an accepted access line that names it to the new group, in the same transaction ("a group always wins", now also for groups created after the line). **A stranger who signed up under a group's name keeps that group's folder.** Attacker: anyone on Earth who registers likely group names (`ops`, `finance`). An admin writes `ops-area/  ops`, pushes (the push says "→ person ops"), then runs `vl group create ops` and `vl group add ops …`, the order a team often uses. The binding stayed with the person until the access lines changed again: the stranger read the folder and the group's members did not. Remaining (documented): between that push and the group's creation the person named has the folder, as the push listed. | `crates/veil-server/src/perm.rs` `bind_names` ran only when an admin's push changed the access lines; `api.rs` `create_group` left the stored bindings alone | `server_hub::adversarial::bug_821_a_group_created_after_its_access_line_takes_the_name` |
| BUG-822 | high (**fixed**, server S1 adversarial fixes) | secrets / key rings | **Fixed:** only an admin of the project may post the first ring of a scope; later changes still verify against the ring's own signed log. **Any participant starts a secret scope's key ring and is its only ring admin.** Attacker: a group member. For a scope the server has no ring for yet (`team` when an existing project turns on `vl upgrade --sync-secrets` after people joined; any later scope) they post their own ring. The admins' ring for that scope is then refused for ever ("unrelated history"), and a new member's device adopts the attacker's ring from its first pull and seals under the attacker's key: the admin's signature is no longer what grants the key. | `crates/veil-server/src/api.rs` `post_ring` accepted any participant's ring, and `merge_ring` stores an incoming ring when none exists | `server_hub::adversarial::bug_822_only_an_admin_starts_a_key_ring` |
| BUG-823 | medium (**fixed**, server S1 adversarial fixes) | push / projections | **Fixed:** the branch a push moves wins over the default branch when both look the same to the pusher. **A push to a branch whose only change since `main` is in a folder the pusher cannot see is refused at random** ("not a fast-forward", whatever they pull): its projected parent was resolved to `main`'s tip or the branch's, in `HashMap` order. | `sync.rs` `push`: `tip_projection` filled from a `HashMap` of all candidates | `server_hub::adversarial::bug_823_a_push_builds_on_the_tip_of_the_branch_it_moves` (8 attempts) |
| BUG-824 | medium (**fixed**, post-S1 fix round) | push / existence oracle | **Fixed:** every object a push introduces must carry the pusher's **upload receipt** (a server MAC over project ‖ person ‖ object id, returned by `POST …/objects`), unless it is the entry at the same path in one of the snapshot's parents (or a pushed directory the pusher sees there). Anything else gets the answer a missing object gets, stored or not, for files and for whole directories (`Graft::proving`, `sync::push`). Uploading a guess lands it either way, so the push says nothing. **A push confirms guesses of whole files it cannot see.** Attacker: a participant who may write somewhere. A tree they push may name an object id without uploading it: the push lands if the project stores that object (in any folder) and is refused "missing object" if not. A right guess of a short hidden file (a password file, a salary) gets a different answer from a wrong one. Folders under access lines are plain text, so their ids follow from their content; secrets are sealed and not exposed this way. Suggested fix: the server records who uploaded each object (or the push carries what it names that the pusher cannot read), and treats a named object that neither the pusher uploaded nor can read like a missing one. | `sync.rs` `check_changes` / `Graft::diff` read any object of the project by id | `server_hub::adversarial::bug_824_a_push_does_not_confirm_guesses_of_hidden_content` |
| BUG-825 | medium (**fixed**, server S1 adversarial fixes) | server / denial of service | **Fixed:** the TLS handshake and each request's head must arrive within 20 s (`IDLE_LIMIT`), and a failing `accept` backs off. **Anyone on Earth holds idle connections open for ever**: a TCP connection that never starts TLS kept a task and a file descriptor; enough of them exhaust the descriptors, and then `accept` failed in a busy loop. HTTP/2 connections idle between requests are not bounded yet. | `crates/veil-server/src/lib.rs` `serve_tls`: no timeout on `acceptor.accept`, no hyper timer; `Err(_) => continue` | `server_hub::adversarial::bug_825_a_silent_connection_is_closed` |
| BUG-826 | low (**fixed**, post-S1 fix round) | projections / ids | **Fixed:** a projection's predecessors are projected too (`sync::projected_predecessors`): a stored predecessor becomes the viewer's version of that change, or is dropped if the viewer's history has no version of it; one the server never stored is kept. This also covers a version served as is whose predecessor touched a hidden folder. **A projected snapshot keeps its real `predecessors`**, so a viewer learns the real id of an earlier version of a change that touched a folder they cannot see (an id, never its content or names: fetching it is checked like any other). Suggested fix: leave `predecessors` out where the server signs a projection. | `sync.rs` `projected` rewrites only `tree`, `parents` and `conflicts` | `server_hub::adversarial::bug_826_a_projection_names_no_hidden_predecessor` |
| BUG-827 | high (**fixed**, post-S1 fix round) | secrets / compromised server | **Fixed** (design in HUB.md §13.11): `vl` wraps a secret key only for devices vouched for outside the server: a person's first device confirmed by the admin by its fingerprint (`vl group add … --confirm 4F2A-…`), later devices signed by one of the person's own (`vl identity confirm`, signature context `hub-device`), or devices a verified key ring already records under the person. A device signing in under a taken name is pending (no session) until vouched for. Removal also revokes every ring member recorded under the person. **A compromised server obtains the project's secret key at the next admin's `vl group add`.** Attacker: whoever controls the server (outside the boundary for secrets, HUB.md §13.6). `GET /v1/people/{name}/devices` is the only source of a person's devices, and the admin's device wraps the key for every identity it returns (each is only checked to be self-consistent, never to be the person's). The server lists its own device under the person being added and receives the key; every `.env` it stores then opens. HUB.md §13.6's "a compromised server's group change gives no key" holds only for changes without an admin. Needs what S2 plans (§13.6 step 3): a person's later devices vouched for by their existing devices, and the first one confirmed outside the server. | `crates/vl/src/hub_cmd.rs` `sync_secret_keys` trusts `client.devices(person)` | `server_hub::adversarial::bug_827_a_server_cannot_slip_its_own_device_into_a_key_grant`, `adversarial::a_later_device_is_confirmed_by_its_person` |
| BUG-828 | medium (**fixed**, server S1 adversarial fixes) | secrets / removal | **Fixed:** a removal by someone who is no ring admin says the key was not rotated and asks an admin to run the removal too. **A manager's removal of someone from their last group silently leaves them the secret key** (only a ring admin's device rotates it; HUB.md said removal rotates). The folder closes at once; the key opens later secrets only if their ciphertext reaches the removed person. | `hub_cmd.rs` `sync_secret_keys` skips scopes this device is not a ring admin of, and said nothing | `server_hub::adversarial::bug_828_a_managers_removal_says_the_key_was_not_rotated` |
| BUG-829 | medium (**fixed**, server S1 adversarial fixes) | `vl access` / policy | **Fixed:** `vl access PATH WHO` edits the last line for PATH, the one that decides. **With two lines for one path (a hand edit, a merge), `vl access billing/ finance` rewrote the first and said "Updated"**, while the last (`billing/ public`) still decided: the folder stayed public. | `crates/vl/src/hub_cmd.rs` `access` replaced the first matching line; the server applies the last | `cli_security::policy_cli::access_edits_the_line_that_decides` |

What held up in this pass (tested, no finding): replayed, forged and cross-key logins; tokens after expiry and after device removal; anonymous and stranger access to private projects; every route crawled for whole markers by each viewer (the builder's `leaks` plus this pass's walks); writes into hidden folders through partial views and grafts; non-admin access-line edits on any branch; a pulled `.veilpolicy` that would expose `.env` (the member's device holds the weakening back and keeps sealing: `adversarial::a_pulled_policy_never_unseals_a_members_secret`); `kill -9` of the server mid-push (6 rounds, 2 killed mid-push: branches always name recorded snapshots, clones and re-pushes work); 300 concurrent requests on a one-connection pool (all answered, none hung).

## Adversarial pass: post-S1 fixes + perf round 1

Branches `t3/87cfa3a3` @ `ad37828` (post-S1 fix round) and `t3/4fc6d273` @ `8f315d0` (performance round 1), merged onto `integration` @ `b78e52d`.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-833 | medium (**fixed**; **pre-existing**: `integration` @ `b78e52d`, a regression from the BUG-818 fix `713e1b3`) | checkout / symlinks | **Fixed:** the barrier opens a recorded directory with `O_DIRECTORY \| O_NOFOLLOW` and takes one replaced since by a link or a file (`ELOOP`, `ENOTDIR`) as removed: the replacement's directory is synced instead, and a link there is never followed. **Checking out `[a → a]` over `[a/a]` failed the whole checkout** with `ELOOP` (`Too many levels of symbolic links`), the proptest `checkouts_over_hostile_states_never_write_outside_or_through_a_link` with random seeds. Attacker: anyone whose tree a member checks out (a self-referencing link replacing a directory). Harm: the checkout cannot complete; a link to a FIFO would also have made the barrier's `open` block. | Checkout records the parent of each removal for the end-of-checkout barrier (`sync_parent_later`); when the removed directory is then replaced by a link, `barrier_sync_dir` opened the path, following the link. | `core_working_copy` `symlinks::a_directory_replaced_by_a_link_to_its_own_name_is_checked_out`; the seed is pinned in `symlinks.proptest-regressions` |
| BUG-834 | medium (**fixed**; BUG-824's class, routes its fix missed) | push / existence oracle | **Fixed:** every object a pushed entry names (its object, its `fallback`, a conflict's terms and their fallbacks: `sync::named_ids`) needs the pusher's upload receipt, unless the entry at that path in a parent names it. **A push lands a guess of a hidden file inside a conflict or as a secret's fallback, and `fetch` then serves it back.** Attacker: a participant who may write somewhere (dev writes `src/`). Controls: a conflict object at `src/c.txt` whose term names an object id, or a sealed `src/.env` whose `fallback` does. Receipts were checked for the entry's own object only, and `fetch` serves any id a conflict or fallback at a readable path names: a right guess of `billing/…` comes back, a wrong one is not found. | `sync::push` checked receipts for `Change::new.id` only; `fetch` accepts `e.fallback` and `Conflict::referenced_ids` | `server_hub` `adversarial::bug_834_a_conflict_names_no_hidden_object`, `adversarial::bug_834_a_fallback_names_no_hidden_object` |
| BUG-835 | medium (**fixed**) | secrets / compromised server | **Fixed:** `--confirm` takes one person (`vl group add finance alice --confirm …`). **A device confirmed in a `vl group add` of several people is recorded in the key ring under whichever person the server lists it for,** so removing its owner later does not rotate the key away from it. Attacker: a compromised server helping someone keep the key after their removal. Controls: the device lists: when an admin runs `vl group add finance one two --confirm <two's fingerprint>`, it lists two's device under one. Harm: after `vl group remove finance two` (the server hides two's device), two's device still holds the secret key. | `confirmed_devices` matches the `--confirm` fingerprints against every person of the command | `server_hub` `adversarial::bug_835_a_confirmed_device_is_recorded_under_its_persons_name` |
| BUG-836 | medium (**fixed**) | secrets / removal | **Fixed:** a removal that leaves someone in another group says so: `keys: fin is still in security: the secret key was not rotated away from them`. **Whether a removal rotates the key is decided by the server's group list, silently.** Attacker: a compromised server keeping a removed person the key. Controls: the group list `vl group remove` reads; it lists the person in another group, so no rotation happens and nothing is shown. (The admin can see and check it now; signed membership statements are S2's, `HUB.md` §13.6.) | `hub_cmd::group` `Remove` rotated only for people the server lists in no group, and said nothing about the others (BUG-828's class) | `server_hub` `adversarial::bug_836_a_removal_that_does_not_rotate_says_so` |
| BUG-837 | medium (**fixed**; **pre-existing**: `integration` @ `b78e52d`) | clone / crash | **Fixed:** `vl clone` writes `.veil/local/clone-incomplete` before its repository exists and removes it after its checkout; a command in a directory holding it refuses ("a clone that was interrupted before it fetched the project: remove it and clone again"), unless the checkout's journal exists (everything was fetched, and the journal finishes the checkout, BUG-830). **A clone killed during its fetch looks like a clean clone of an empty project:** `vl status` says "no changes", `vl log` shows only the root. Found by `kill -9` at random points (60 path clones: 43 such). | Clone creates the repository, then fetches, then checks out; nothing recorded that the fetch had not finished | `cli_sync` `clone_crash_cli::a_clone_killed_during_its_fetch_is_refused_not_shown_empty`; `server_hub` `sync::a_clone_killed_at_any_point_is_refused_or_finished` |
| BUG-838 | medium (**fixed**, performance round 2) | server / migrations / YugabyteDB | **Fixed:** migrations run once, by the one process holding a database advisory lock (`veil-server migrate`, or the first server to start; the others wait for the lock, then find the schema current); a server that finds the schema current runs **no DDL**; every request that fails on a retryable database error (`40001`, a restarted read, a catalog or schema version changed by DDL) before it committed anything runs again, with backoff, until the 45 s deadline (`db::retry_requests`), and sign-in writes its session in the same transaction as the device so that it never commits half way; `verify-server.sh` migrates each database once before any server starts. Tests: `server_hub` `startup::servers_starting_together_migrate_once`, `a_server_on_a_current_schema_runs_no_ddl` (a role without DDL rights), `requests_survive_a_schema_change` (failed on YugabyteDB before: sign-ins answered `busy`). **Servers starting together make requests fail with `busy` on YugabyteDB.** Each `veil-server` runs the schema migrations at start-up (`db::migrate`), and the DDL races with the other servers' (`40001`, `Restart read required`, `type schema_migrations already exists`). YugabyteDB aborts concurrent transactions while the catalog version changes, so `vl` requests to the servers already running fail after the 45 s deadline (`the database is busy`). It was seen as 7 of 36 failures in `verify-server.sh`'s YugabyteDB suite when merging `t3/4bf2a483` (PostgreSQL and both load steps were green), and once before in that branch's own run. In production this is a rolling restart of several servers. | Migrations run in every server process with no coordination, and requests outside `db::serializable` aren't retried across a schema change. Suggested fix: apply migrations once (a `veil-server migrate` step, or one holder of an advisory lock, with others waiting for the schema version); a server that finds the schema current runs no DDL; retry `40001` and catalog-version errors on every request path. | `verify-server.sh` YugabyteDB suite (intermittent) |

**Held up** (no finding): pending devices (no session, no signature accepted, no key; vouching by another person refused); vouches moved to another device or signed by another key; `--confirm` with a fingerprint the server chose (matches nothing the person reads); renamed people (ring roots are the names the admin typed); a forged X25519 key under a ring member's signing key (the binding is verified); upload receipts replayed across people or projects (the MAC binds both); the receipt key file (0600, created once); the lazily compiled scanner (a `OnceLock`: a scan waits for a compilation in progress, a failed one panics loudly, nothing catches it into "no secret"); the pack path under `kill -9` (60 of 60 kills of a 2,000-file `vl commit`: fsck clean, the retry records everything); packing writers beside `gc --prune-now` on a disk (96 of 96 under load); racy stat-cache records (stamps include ctime, a racy record never vouches).

**Hypothesis (seen once, not reproduced, not filed):** on YugabyteDB, one `verify-server.sh` run failed `adversarial::bug_824_a_push_does_not_confirm_guesses_of_hidden_content` (35 of 36 passed; the next run was green, 36 of 36): its server's request got `40001 schema version mismatch for table …` while other test servers, starting on the fresh database at the same moment, were still applying migrations (the log shows the `schema_migrations` race and a `40001` retry of migration 1). A migration whose losing copy re-runs DDL (`IF NOT EXISTS`) may bump YugabyteDB's catalog version under a server already serving, and a query outside `db::serializable` is not retried. In production this would be a rolling start with a new migration; a fix would retry `40001` on every query, or start serving only once `schema_migrations` holds every version.

**Out of scope (trusted):** an admin who confirms a fingerprint without comparing it; a person who vouches for a device that is not theirs.

**Residual (design, not filed):** a device fingerprint is 80 bits: a server that wants its device to match one person's needs a second preimage, about 2^80 key generations, between the person's sign-in and the admin's check.

## Confinement verification review

Independent verification of `vl ws exec` confinement (`t3/267b9531` @ `c2fe202`, merged onto `integration` @ `bfa691a`), against `docs/design/scale/9-confinement.md`. The attacker is the confined command (an agent, a test suite, a dependency's build script): outside the trust boundary for files (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, "Threat model"). Owner decisions, not findings: the environment passes through, the network is on by default, the allow-list is read-only. Reproductions: `crates/vl/tests/cli_workspaces/confine_cli.rs`, after "Confinement verification review".

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-848 | high (**fixed**, confinement review fixes) | confinement / allow-list | **Fixed:** the Veil home, the real project and cargo's credentials are hidden under every name an allowed path gives them (`base_args` maps each canonical target through each allowed bind), cargo's credentials in `$CARGO_HOME` too, and an allowed path inside the Veil home or the project is refused. **An allowed link to a folder that holds the Veil home or the project showed the command the person's identity with its secret keys, the real `.veil` (the store, other workspaces' sandboxes) and the real working copy**; an allowed link to `~/.cargo`, or an allowed `$CARGO_HOME`, showed the crates.io token. Attacker: the confined command. Controls: what it reads. Harm: personal private keys and every workspace's files reach a command that the design says cannot read them, for an ordinary setup (`--allow ~/src` where `~/src` links to a data disk). | the masks were applied only at each target's own path, while an allowed link binds the target's canonical folder under the link's name | `an_allowed_link_to_a_folder_holding_keys_or_the_project_still_hides_them` |
| BUG-849 | medium (**fixed**, confinement review fixes) | confinement / limits | **Fixed:** `systemd-run` gets `--expand-environment=no` where it knows it (systemd 254 and later; probed). **With a limit that uses a systemd scope, the command was rewritten:** `systemd-run` expands `$VAR`, `${VAR}` and `$$` in everything after `--` (the `bwrap` arguments and the command), so `sh -c 'echo $$ > f'` wrote `$`, and a project path with a `$` in it was bound under another name. | systemd 254 made expansion the default for `--scope` | `a_command_with_dollar_signs_runs_as_given_under_a_systemd_scope` |
| BUG-850 | medium (**fixed**, confinement review fixes) | confinement / limits | **Fixed:** every `systemd-run` and `systemctl` call has a 5-second deadline (`output_within`); a user manager that does not answer means the polling fallback before the run, and never delays recording after it. **A slow or hung systemd user manager hung `vl ws exec`:** with a limit, before anything ran (the probe), and after a scoped run that was killed (`systemctl show`, `reset-failed`), before the run was recorded. | the probe and the post-run queries waited without a limit | `a_slow_systemd_user_manager_does_not_hang_vl`, `a_hung_systemctl_after_a_scoped_run_does_not_keep_vl_from_recording` |
| BUG-851 | medium (**fixed**, confinement review fixes) | confinement / clean-up | **Fixed:** `vl` arms `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` (SIGKILL) in the child before it execs `systemd-run` or `bwrap` (it survives both execs) and refuses to start if `vl` is already gone. **`kill -9` of `vl` while `systemd-run` or `bwrap` was starting left the command running**, orphaned, in the sandbox, after the reaper had taken the injected files out and without the sandbox's lock (the next run could start beside it). | `bwrap`'s `--die-with-parent` only holds once `bwrap` has armed it | `killing_vl_while_bwrap_or_systemd_run_starts_leaves_nothing_running` |
| BUG-852 | medium (**fixed**, small fixes 1) | confinement / clean-up | **Fixed:** what a dead `vl` left of a confined run is stopped: the processes still running a `bwrap` (or `systemd-run`) that binds the sandbox, found by their command line in `/proc` and killed through a pidfd (killing the sandbox's init ends its pid namespace), and the `veil-exec-<pid>-*` scopes of `vl` processes that are gone (killed, stopped, and their failed state cleared). The reaper does it as soon as `vl` dies; the next `vl ws exec` in the workspace, `vl ws rm`, and the sweep of throwaway sandboxes do it for a run with no reaper (or whose reaper died too), always under the sandbox's lock or for a dead owner's throwaway directory, so no live run is touched. Under load (16 busy CPUs, 8 copies at once), the reproduction passed 32/32 and failed 23/24 with the stopping turned off. **`kill -9` of `vl` in the moment between `bwrap` creating the sandbox's first process and that process arming its own parent-death signal still leaves the command running** (seen for kills 50–100 ms after the start, also in a plain `verify-fast.sh` run; under load, 4 of 416 kills with 8 copies of the test at once; with 8 copies of a 64-kill loop, every loop hit it at least once, 5 leaving the command running and the others an empty or failed scope), and `systemd-run` killed while systemd starts its scope can leave the empty scope active, or failed (the scope is not `--collect`ed, so that `vl` can read why it failed; the design's sketch said it was).
