Smallest fix: the next run of the workspace stops what an earlier, dead `vl` left (its `veil-exec-<pid>-*` scope, or the processes whose root is the sandbox), or `bwrap` checks its parent after arming the signal (upstream). | inside `bwrap`'s start-up, and systemd missing the empty cgroup | `killing_vl_at_any_moment_leaves_nothing_and_the_next_run_recovers`, `the_next_run_and_ws_rm_stop_what_a_dead_run_left_running` |
| BUG-853 | medium (**fixed**, confinement review fixes) | confinement / doctor | **Fixed:** `vl doctor` looks for `bwrap`, `systemd-run` and `systemctl` outside the project, as `vl ws exec` does. **`vl doctor` ran a `bwrap` or `systemd-run` from the project** when `PATH` names a folder of it (a direnv `PATH_add bin`). Attacker: whoever adds `bin/bwrap` to the repository (a hostile pull). Harm: their program runs as the person. | `confine::doctor` searched `PATH` without the project root | `doctor_never_runs_a_bwrap_from_inside_the_project` |
| BUG-854 | low (**fixed**, confinement review fixes) | confinement / allow-list | **Fixed:** without a usable `HOME` there is no default allow-list. **With `HOME` unset, relative or `/`, the default allow-list was read from `/tmp/home`**, which any user of the machine can create: another OS user's `/tmp/home/.claude` (an agent's settings and hooks) reached the confined agent. | the stand-in home was used for the defaults too | `confine::tests::without_a_home_there_is_no_default_allow_list` |
| BUG-855 | low (**fixed**, small fixes 1) | `vl serve` / tests | **Fixed (the test's timing; the cap is right):** `Server::start` learns that the server listens by connecting, and that probe is a client too. The server may accept it only after the test's connections have started arriving, and it holds a slot until the server's thread for it has seen it leave, so under load the 32nd connection was refused. `connections_are_capped` now waits for the server's `Serving` line instead (`Server::start_unprobed`). Under load (32 busy CPUs, 24 copies at once): 50 of 96 runs failed before; 144/144 passed after, and 144/144 with no probe and nothing else changed. **`cli_sync::serve_cli::connections_are_capped` fails intermittently in the full parallel suite:** one of the first 32 connections is refused. It has been seen in three separate full runs (perf round 1, the BUG-818 fix, the confinement merge). It passes in isolation (150/150) and under targeted load (48/48), and none of those changes touched `vl serve`. | The test harness's own readiness probe connection, accepted late and released late, held one of the 32 slots. | `cli_sync::serve_cli::connections_are_capped` |

What held up (tested, no finding): writes through `..`, absolute paths, absolute and relative symlinks, `/proc/self/root`, `/proc/1/root`, `/proc/self/cwd`, renames and hard links across the boundary, `mknod` and `mkfifo` into an allowed folder, and the allow-list itself (outside files hash the same before and after); links, FIFOs and sockets the command plants in its sandbox (replacing an injected file or its folder) never make `vl` read, write or delete outside it, on the next sync, injection, take-out or sparse clean-up, nor hang it; keys and other home files, the Veil identity, the real `.veil` and other workspaces' sandboxes stay unreadable; the command inherits no descriptor of `vl`; project paths with spaces, newlines, leading dashes and `$`; hostile `--allow` values, `HOME` and configuration; a `bwrap` inside the project; 6 and 2 workspaces run at once stay separate (96 of 96 runs of each under load, 12 copies at a time); signals (32 of 32 under load); limits under both mechanisms.

**Out of scope (trusted):** an explicit `--allow` of a folder that holds the home (it shows `~/.ssh`; the person's deliberate command), or of a folder that holds `$TMPDIR` (other `--ephemeral` runs' sandboxes); a hostile `PATH` that names a hostile `bwrap` outside the project.

## Server benchmarks (2026-11-06)

Found by the 1 GB profile through the server (`scripts/bench-server.sh 1g`, `integration` @ `bfa691a`; `docs/design/scale/BASELINE.md`, "Through the Veil server"). The reproductions need the server, PostgreSQL and MinIO, so they are the benchmark's cases for now; the stream that fixes each adds an ignored-then-passing test to `server_hub` (the container suite).

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-857 | medium (**fixed**, server bench fixes; **pre-existing**: S1) | client / push to the server | **Fixed:** a push sends what each new snapshot introduces at each path, walking a tree once for each set of parent trees it is compared with (`hub::Introduced`); it sends exactly what check 7 asks a receipt for. The 1 GB initial push now succeeds in one push (`BASELINE.md`, "After the server bench fixes"). **A push of a history of more than a few snapshots is refused:** `missing object 7a8b7c15 (server: bad_request)`, after uploading everything (the initial push of the 1 GB profile: 121 s, 2.4 GB uploaded; of the `tiny` profile, 200 snapshots, the same). Pushes of a few snapshots at a time succeed. This blocks moving a repository with history onto the server (`HUB.md` §5.7, §13.8). Nothing is lost: the server refuses the whole push. | `hub.rs` `collect_tree` skips an entry equal to the same path's entry in one of the snapshot's parents, and stops at any tree already in `seen`, whatever parents that tree was walked against. A tree first walked under a later snapshot (against a parent that already had a file) is not walked again under the snapshot that introduced the file, so the file is never sent; the server requires every object a snapshot introduces at a path to carry the pusher's receipt (check 7, BUG-824). Suggested fix: keep `seen` for what is sent, not for where the walk stops (each snapshot's walk is bounded by the parent comparison already), or walk each changed subtree once per parent context. | `server_hub` `sync::bug_857_a_push_of_a_history_that_reuses_a_tree_is_accepted`, `sync::random_histories_pushed_in_one_push_are_accepted`; `vl` `hub::tests::a_push_sends_exactly_what_the_server_lacks` (proptest: sent = what the server lacks, over random histories); `scripts/bench-server.sh tiny` and `1g` |
| BUG-858 | medium (**fixed**, server bench fixes; **pre-existing**: S1) | client / push memory | **Fixed:** the push uploads each object as the walk finds it, about 8 MiB per request (`hub::Uploads`). **`vl push` to the server holds every new object in memory:** the initial push of the 1 GB profile peaked at **2,410 MiB**; one push of its import (11 snapshots, 20,883 files) at 867 MiB. The memory gate is 512 MiB per command. | `hub.rs` `push` collects the raw bytes of every object it introduces (`objects: Vec<(ObjectId, Vec<u8>)>`) before uploading them in 8 MiB frames. Suggested fix: upload each 8 MiB batch as the walk fills it (receipts are per object, so nothing else changes). | `scripts/bench-server.sh 1g` (`push, initial (all tags)`) |
| BUG-859 | medium (**fixed**, server bench fixes; **pre-existing**: S1) | client / clone and pull memory | **Fixed:** a level is fetched in parts of at most 2,500 objects and about 8 MiB (by the sizes tree entries carry), at most 8 in flight, and each part's objects are written as it arrives (`Client::fetch_each`). **`vl clone` from the server peaks at 979 MiB** on the 1 GB profile (792 MiB with `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2`), over the 512 MiB gate. Path clones measured 503 MiB before path remotes went away. Pull of one snapshot, and a clone of one directory, stay under 110 MiB. | `pull` fetches every new snapshot's trees and files a level at a time; a level is up to 20,000 objects (many versions of many files), fetched by `Client::fetch_all` as 8 parts whose response bodies are all kept until the last arrives, then copied object by object (`raw.to_vec()`) before any is written; checkout's staging threads then add theirs on top. RSS climbs level by level to ~600 MiB during the fetch, then to 974 MiB during checkout. Suggested fix: hand each part's objects to `Incoming` as it arrives, without copying, and size a level's requests by bytes (tree entries carry sizes), not by count. | `scripts/bench-server.sh 1g` (`clone, full`) |
| BUG-860 | medium (**fixed**, server bench fixes; **pre-existing**: S1) | server / memory | **Fixed:** a fetch checks its wants a snapshot at a time (dropping that snapshot's projected trees), then streams its answer with at most 64 objects or about 8 MiB read ahead; uploaded objects are copied out of the request (a slice kept the whole 8 MiB body alive in the object cache); decoded trees are weighed by the memory they hold. The projection is unchanged (every `server_hub` leak and adversarial test passes). **The server's memory grows to several GB serving one project:** a fresh server serving one full clone of the 1 GB profile peaked at **2.5 GB** (1.95 GB with `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2`), growing steadily through the clone; it reached 3.1 GB over the benchmark and kept it; a push of the whole history peaked at 1.5 GB. Its caches are bounded at 512 MiB by weight. Thousands of agents cloning at once would multiply the per-request part. | `sync.rs` `fetch` builds each response's whole body in memory (8 concurrent requests per level, up to 2,500 objects each), and keeps the projected root and directory trees (`roots`, `Dirs`: decoded `Arc<Tree>`s) of every snapshot its wants name (with revision 1, most of the history in one request), outside the bounded tree cache; decoded trees are weighed at twice their encoded size, which is likely below their real size. The push path was not profiled. Suggested fix: stream a response's frames; drop a snapshot's directory trees once its wants are served; weigh decoded trees by their real size. | `scripts/bench-server.sh 1g` (server RSS column) |
| BUG-861 | low (**fixed**, server bench fixes; **pre-existing**: S1) | server / S3 | **Fixed:** every S3 request (all idempotent) is tried again for up to 10 s (jittered waits doubling from 25 ms to 1 s; a request's deadline is 45 s) when its connection closes or resets, before or during the answer, or the store answers 5xx; idle connections are dropped after 15 s. A first try with 6 tries in about 1.5 s was not enough: under the load profile's 100 projects the local MinIO stayed unreachable for longer. **A reset S3 connection fails the request with HTTP 500:** under the load profile with MinIO, 1,239 uploads at 100 projects answered `HTTP 500 internal error` (`scripts/bench-server.sh load`, first run); a second run's server log shows the cause, five S3 requests failing with `connection closed before message completed` and `Connection reset by peer`. S3 services close idle keep-alive connections (AWS after about 20 s), so production sees the same. | `store.rs` `S3Store` sends each request once on a pooled `reqwest` connection (`pool_max_idle_per_host(64)`), with no retry; a request on a connection the store has closed fails, and the error becomes a 500. Suggested fix: retry idempotent S3 requests (GET, HEAD, PUT of a content-addressed key) on connection errors, with backoff, and drop idle connections before the store does (`pool_idle_timeout`). | `server_hub` `sync::bug_861_a_closed_object_store_connection_is_tried_again` (a proxy in front of MinIO drops connections without answering); `scripts/bench-server.sh load` |
| BUG-864 | medium (**fixed**, mem864; found by the server bench fixes) | server / memory | **Fixed:** `veil-server`'s global allocator is jemalloc (`tikv-jemallocator` 0.7.0), with two arenas, background purging and a 1 s decay (`src/main.rs`). Of glibc, glibc capped at 1 or 2 arenas, mimalloc and jemalloc, it kept the least on the 1 GB profile, with no throughput loss under `bench-server.sh load`: the push peaks at 984 MiB (was 2,367), the full clone at 1,579 MiB (was 2,877), and the server keeps 948 MiB at the end of the run (was 2,880). `veil-bench run` gates the server's peak under 1 GB; the clone's remaining excess is BUG-1000 (`BASELINE.md`, "The server's allocator"). **The server's resident memory is two to three times what it holds:** after the server bench fixes its data is bounded (two 256 MiB caches, a request's tables), yet a push of the 1 GB history leaves it at 1.5–2.6 GB (`BASELINE.md`, "After the server bench fixes"). Measured by hand on a fresh server, the same push peaks at 0.84 GB with `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=1` and 1.1 GB with `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2`; pinning glibc's mmap threshold changes nothing. Thousands of agents on one server would multiply it by its threads. | glibc gives each thread its own arena (up to 8 per core) and never returns an arena's memory below its high-water mark: the server runs 32 runtime workers and up to 512 blocking threads (each snapshot of a push is checked on one; `FsStore` writes on them), 34–146 threads in these runs, and `/proc/PID/smaps` shows the memory in their 64 MiB arena heaps. Suggested fix (a dependency or deployment choice for the owner): an allocator with bounded per-thread caches (jemalloc or mimalloc as the global allocator), or a cap on arenas (`mallopt(M_ARENA_MAX)` at start, or `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2` in the deployment), measured against the load profile. | `scripts/bench-server.sh 1g` (server RSS column) |
| BUG-865 | low (open; **pre-existing**: S1; found by the server bench fixes) | server / push speed | **An initial push of a long history takes minutes on the server:** the 1 GB profile's 10,022 snapshots and 2.4 GB take 337 s to push in one push (before the fixes, the refused upload and the 54 fast-forward steps of the workaround took 316 s together). Moving a repository with history onto the server is slow, never wrong. | `sync::push` completes and checks the new snapshots strictly one after another, each in its own blocking task: its graft and change checks read every changed tree and the parents' entries at each changed path (`entry_at` from the root, per path and parent), and the object store is S3. Suggested fix: check independent snapshots in parallel (a snapshot needs only its parents' real trees), and reuse the parents' decoded paths across a snapshot's changes. | `scripts/bench-server.sh 1g` (`push, initial (all tags)`) |
| BUG-890 | low (**fixed**, fix1; **pre-existing**: `integration` @ `1f9f880`; found by the server bench fixes; not reproduced since) | server / YugabyteDB | **Fixed:** `Error::is_retryable` counts YugabyteDB's `Rpc timeout` (`error::is_rpc_timeout`), so `db::transaction` and `db::retry_requests` run the work again within `TRANSACTION_DEADLINE` (45 s, three 15 s RPC timeouts at most), as for a serialisation failure. A commit that timed out may have committed, so `db::transaction` never runs `f` again after one (it answers 500, as before) and `retry_requests` already stops once a commit was tried. The stall itself cannot be caused on demand; `error::tests::bug_890_a_yugabytedb_rpc_timeout_is_retryable` checks the classification. **A YugabyteDB RPC timeout fails the request with HTTP 500:** in one run of the `server_hub` suite on a freshly started YugabyteDB (base `1f9f880`, 2026-11-07), 27 tests failed on `internal error`; the server's log shows `database error [XX000]: … Rpc timeout, passed: 15.0s, timeout: 15.000s`. (That run's and the others' `vl group create` "the database is busy" failures were BUG-847, fixed on `integration` @ `1e2c933`: `verify-server.sh` is green on this branch after merging it, YugabyteDB 68/68.) | `Error::is_retryable` does not count YugabyteDB's `XX000 … Rpc timeout` (a stalled tablet RPC, safe to run again before anything committed), so `db::retry_requests` never runs the request again. Suggested fix: treat `Rpc timeout` as retryable, like the other YugabyteDB messages there. | none yet (seen once in the `server_hub` suite on a fresh YugabyteDB container) |
| BUG-1000 | medium (open; found by mem864) | server / clone memory | **A full clone of the 1 GB profile takes the server past 1 GB, whatever the allocator:** with jemalloc (BUG-864's fix) the server is at 851 MiB after the push and peaks at 1,579 MiB during the clone; the clone of one directory after it peaks at 1,069 MiB. So these two cases are over `veil-bench run`'s server memory gate (under 1 GB, `storage-and-cost.md` §9); while this bug is open they show "⛔ (known: BUG-1000)" and do not fail the run (`report::SERVER_GATE_KNOWN`: remove both entries with the fix). With a single glibc arena the clone still adds 857 MiB (928 → 1,785), so this is the fetch path's memory, not arenas (`BASELINE.md`, "The server's allocator"). | Not profiled yet. The server's two caches are bounded at 256 MiB each by weight, and a fetch streams its answer with about 8 MiB read ahead (BUG-860), so 0.7 GB more held during a clone points to memory outside those bounds: the fetch's per-request state across a clone's 8 concurrent requests per level, or cache weights below the real size. A heap profile (a jemalloc build with `profiling`, `_RJEM_MALLOC_CONF=prof:true`) during `clone, full` would name it. | `scripts/bench-server.sh 1g` (the server RSS column: `clone, full`) |
| BUG-1001 | low (**fixed**, fix1; found by mem864; seen once in 12 load runs) | benchmark / load | **Fixed:** `server::Server::start` passes `--listen 127.0.0.1:0` and reads the address the server prints once it has bound (`veil-server listening on https://…`), draining the rest of its stdout; it no longer binds a port of its own. The message was most likely that bind: with no context, `?` printed only the OS error, and a bind to port 0 fails with `EADDRINUSE` when the previous curve's closed connections (TIME_WAIT) hold the ephemeral range. Checked with three `veil-bench load` runs in a row against PostgreSQL (each server on the port it chose, 4,013 operations, 0 errors); the exhaustion itself was not reproduced. **`veil-bench load` stopped before its third curve with `Error: Address already in use (os error 98)`:** `bench-server.sh load` with the jemalloc server (`f7e9aac`), after the pull and land curves finished; the 11 other load runs that day started all three curves. | Not found. The message is `veil-bench`'s own, not the server's ("veil-server did not start"); `server::Server::start` picks the server's port by binding port 0 and releasing it (a race with any other process taking the port meanwhile), and the load tool opens thousands of connections per curve, so the machine's ephemeral ports may have run short. | `scripts/bench-server.sh load` (not reproduced) |

## Performance round 3 (2026-11-07)

Found while changing the order of a transaction's durable writes (`docs/design/scale/OPENSCION.md`, round 3), and by its verification. All three are gaps in the tests, not in the product.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-905 | medium (**fixed**, performance round 3; **pre-existing**: the crash harness since format 6) | tests / crash ordering | **Fixed:** `FaultyBackend` keeps the unsynced renames of every other directory at a power loss (by first appearance), keeps the renames into one directory together, and drops staged names. **The crash harness could not catch a name made durable before what it depends on.** At a simulated power loss it undid every unsynced rename together, as if they all reached the disk or none did; a real disk may keep a rename into one directory and not into another. A transaction that renamed its operation and its head without a barrier between them passed every sweep (mutation check: removing that barrier now fails `crash_during_snapshot` and `crash_during_one_operation_commit`). | `crash.rs` `FaultyBackend::tear_volatile` restored every entry of `renamed`. | `core_durability` `crash::crash_during_one_operation_commit` (and every sweep) |
| BUG-906 | low (**fixed**, performance round 3; **pre-existing**: since `vl commit` became one operation, round 2) | tests / crash coverage | **Fixed:** a sweep of `snapshot_workspace_with(… commit …)`. **`vl commit`'s one-operation path was never crash-swept:** `crash_during_commit` sweeps `commit_working_copy`, the old two-step commit; the transaction `vl commit` makes (the directory recorded and committed together, `SnapshotOptions::commit`) had no sweep. | Round 2 added the path without a crash sweep. | `core_durability` `crash::crash_during_one_operation_commit` |
| BUG-907 | low (**fixed**, fix1: obsolete; **pre-existing**: `integration` @ `1b39297`) | tests / flaky | **Fixed (obsolete):** the test went with path remotes in the simplification pass, part 1 (`4e27eeb`); no remaining test counts kills it needs to have landed (the server's `a_clone_killed_at_any_point_is_refused_or_finished` asserts safety on every kill, and nothing about how many). **`kill_9_mid_push_never_publishes_part_of_it_and_the_next_push_resumes` fails under load:** "only 2 pushes were actually killed" (it needs 3 of its 6 pushes killed mid-push). 8 of 32 runs failed on the base and 2 of 32 on this round's code, 8 at a time; 0 of 10 alone. What it checks for safety (the remote consistent after every kill, the resumed push) held in every run. | A liveness assertion on timing: the test polls the push journal every millisecond and kills the push once it has staged one to three more packs; a descheduled poller lets a push stage everything and finish, so later rounds have nothing left to kill. Suggested fix: a hook that stops the push at its N-th staged pack (as the gc races do), instead of polling. | `cli_adversarial` `adversarial_cli_crash::kill_9_mid_push_…`, 32 runs 8 at a time |

## Adversarial pass: performance round 3 (2026-11-07)

Security and correctness review (tier A, Deep) of performance round 3 (`t3/fa4aac19` @ `d7edb20`): the scanner's literal check, stats beside the listing, `prefetch.rs`, the staged operation and head, the stat cache's moved records, and the policy and stat cache read on other threads. Nothing this round introduced was found. The scanner's literal check finds what the full search finds, by a new adversarial differential test (`secrets::tests::token_literal_check_adversarial_windows`: case variants, split, escaped and percent-encoded prefixes, Unicode look-alikes, NUL padding, random `from..limit` windows, NUL-stripped searches, and UTF-16 and UTF-32 files). `core_durability` (crash sweeps, gc races, the op-log races) passed every crash, gc and op-log test in 24 runs, 8 at a time. One pre-existing gap:

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-970 | low (**fixed**, fix1; **pre-existing**: `integration` @ `0fbe207`) | tests / flaky | **Fixed, for the class (BUG-1007, 1010, 1085 and `bug_1053` too):** a claim about work is counted, not timed. In `core_durability`, "taken over at once" is a lock acquisition on a simulated clock that only its own back-off moves (`acquire_counting_wait`: waited zero), the memory peak is measured in a process of the test's own (`in_own_process`), a writer that must not wait for a slow snapshot commits while the scan is held until it has (no bound), and gc's "did not wait" is the reason it gives (refused at once, not "kept running for 65 s"). Every `elapsed() <` bound in the binary is gone; what is left times only a hang guard or waits that load can only lengthen (`>=`). The whole binary, 8 copies at a time beside a build, 3 rounds: see the report. **Timing and memory tests in `core_durability` fail under load.** In 24 runs of the whole binary, 8 at a time, `maintenance::pruning_memory_is_the_inventory_bits_not_the_objects` ("pruning grew the peak by 5 MiB, 88 bytes per object") and `lock::dead_holder_is_stolen_immediately` ("did not wait for the lease (156 ms)") failed 4 times on this round's code. On the base, they and `adversarial_lock::a_live_lock_holder_is_never_robbed` and two `metadata_lock` tests failed 5 times. Alone, every run passes. The merge check (`verify-fast.sh`) runs nextest, so these can turn a merge red that broke nothing. | Liveness and resource assertions on wall-clock time (a lease wait, a lock steal) and on the process's peak memory, which other tests' threads in the same process share. A loaded machine stretches the first and grows the second. Suggested fix: measure what the test's own work allocates (a counting allocator scoped to the thread, or a child process), and wait on events, not elapsed time. | `core_durability`, the whole binary run 8 at a time, 3 rounds (`for r in 1 2 3; do for j in $(seq 8); do $BIN -q & done; wait; done`) |

| BUG-839 | low (**fixed**, fix1; **pre-existing**: `integration` @ `a9f4fa8`; found by the S2 secrets stream) | policy / messages | **Fixed:** an access line that names only groups and people (`Rule::grantees`) never sets `blocked_exposure`, and settles an earlier ignored `public` rule like any later rule; one that also names `public` publishes the folder and still warns (`policy_cli::bug_839_…` checks both, `a_later_rule_settles_an_ignored_public_rule` the classification). **Every snapshot under an access line that covers a shared secret warns that the policy "tries to make built-in secret path `billing/.env` public; ignored".** An access line (`billing/  finance`) publishes nothing: on the device it is a plain-text rule, and the secret stays sealed (`cloak rule`/`cloak team`). The warning appears on every commit an admin makes after `vl access billing/ finance` while `billing/.env` exists, and teaches people to ignore it. ## Adversarial pass: secrets S2

Branch `t3/8c680abf` @ `1a81c2c` (S2: invites, keys per access rule, background key sharing, device removal, ring format 4, repository format 7) merged onto `integration` @ `a9f4fa8`. Attackers: a compromised server (it writes its own rows, key rings included), a removed person, an outsider, a member reaching beyond their rules. Tests: `server_hub` `adversarial_s2`. Fixed in "secrets S2 adversarial fixes"; BUG-843 and BUG-845, and the residual and nits below, in "secrets S2 owner fixes" (owner-approved designs; tests: `server_hub` `projects`, `core_security` `ring_shares`).

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-840 | critical (**fixed**) | secrets / compromised server | **Fixed:** a ring of a scope a device does not hold yet is taken from the server only if a current member of the device's `team` ring (the admins', on the server) started it (`hub_keys::merge_server_ring`, on every pull, clone, ring change and `vl identity remove`; `team` first). **A key ring the server makes confirms its own device to an admin's `vl group add`.** Attacker: a compromised server. Controls: the rings it serves (a ring of its own, any scope name, genesis signed by its own key, recording its device under the name of a person being added) and that person's device list. Harm: the admin's ordinary `vl pull` stores the ring; `vl group add finance newbie` (no `--confirm`) takes "a verified ring holds it under newbie's name" as a confirmation (`How::Ring`) and wraps `billing/`'s key for the server's device; the output says "granted to newbie". Every secret of the rule. (An `invite` in such a ring confirmed a join the server signed, the same way.) | `confirmed_devices` trusts every ring that verifies (`local_states`), and `merge_ring` stores any ring of a scope the repository does not hold, whoever started it. S1 read only the scopes its policy named, which `vl init` had made | `server_hub` `adversarial_s2::bug_840_a_ring_the_server_made_never_confirms_a_device` |
| BUG-841 | critical (**fixed** with BUG-840) | secrets / compromised server | **A rule's key ring the server puts up first seals that rule's secrets under the server's key.** Attacker: a compromised server. Controls: the ring of `rule-<infra>` (the name is public: a hash of the path) before the admin creates it, started by its own key with a key it made, wrapped for the admin's device and with its own device under the admin's name. Harm: `vl access infra/ ops` fetches the server's ring first and keeps it (the admin is an "admin" of it, and the server's device, named like the admin, is never revoked); the admin's next commit seals `infra/.env` under a key the server holds, and it reads it. | As BUG-840: rings of scopes the device does not hold are taken on first sight, and S2 creates rule rings late (at `vl access`), after the project is on the server | `server_hub` `adversarial_s2::bug_841_a_ring_the_server_made_never_seals_a_secret` |
| BUG-842 | high (**fixed**) | invites / removed person | **Fixed:** removing someone from a group deletes their invites to it that were not used (`change_group`), also for a name nobody has signed up with yet, which `vl group remove` now accepts. **An invite cannot be taken back: a removal before the join does not cancel it.** Attacker: a person an admin invited and then removed (the admin changed their mind; the link was sent). Controls: the link, within its 7 days. Harm: `vl join` still works and the server adds them to the group: they read the group's folders (plain text on the server). For a name with no account yet, `vl group remove` failed ("no person named …"), so there was no way at all to cancel. (The key rings' invites were cancelled; folder access was not.) | The server's invite row outlives the group membership it grants; nothing but its use or the group's deletion ends it | `server_hub` `adversarial_s2::bug_842_removing_an_invited_person_cancels_their_invite` |
| BUG-843 | medium (**fixed**) | devices / outsider | **Fixed:** an admin's `vl identity remove` bars the device from their project only (`device_bars`, checked on every request to the project and at a join; the project's keys rotate away from it, and only that project reports it removed); only the person, from another of their confirmed devices, removes a device everywhere. **An admin of any project removes a person's device everywhere.** Attacker: anyone. Controls: a project of their own; they add the person to one of its groups (no consent is asked), then, as its admin, `POST /v1/devices/remove` with the person's device (any listed one). Harm: the device can no longer sign in anywhere, the person's own company's project included, and that project's devices revoke it from its rings in the background (it gets the keys back only through a new `--confirm`). Denial of service against anyone, by anyone. | `remove_device` allows an admin of any project in which the person takes part, and removes the device globally (`devices.removed_at`, all sessions) | `server_hub` `adversarial_s2::bug_843_an_admin_removes_a_device_only_for_their_own_project`. Suggested fix (a design choice for the owner): an admin's removal bars the device from that project only (a per-project record checked at sign-in to it); only the person removes a device everywhere |
| BUG-844 | high (**fixed**) | migration / crash | **Fixed:** a re-run of `vl upgrade --rule-keys` on a policy already rewritten still creates and grants the rule rings and makes `team` the admins' only. **An interrupted `vl upgrade --rule-keys` is reported done.** It rewrites `.veilpolicy` first and moves the keys after; if it stops in between (server unreachable, a 409 storm, ^C), running it again prints "already seals each folder's secrets with that folder's key" and does nothing. Attacker: a member of S1's `team` (every person an admin added to a group). Harm: `team` keeps them, and after the move it seals the admins' own secrets (paths no access line names anyone for, e.g. a root `.env`): they open the admins' new secrets. | The early return on `per_rule(text)` treats "the policy says `cloak rule`" as "the keys were moved" | `server_hub` `adversarial_s2::bug_844_an_interrupted_rule_keys_move_is_finished_by_a_rerun` |
| BUG-845 | high (**fixed**; the class was **pre-existing**: S1's `team` ring has the same name in every project) | secrets / compromised server / cross-project | **Fixed:** every ring is bound to its project: a project's id is the hash of its `team` ring's genesis, every other ring's signed genesis carries it (ring format 4, format 7, changed in place before release), and every replay that decides about keys refuses a ring naming another project (`Keychain::bound`; the server too, for a new ring). A device trusts a project's rings only once it anchored the project's fingerprint outside the server: the invite link carries it (`vl join` checks it), the device that created the project has it, or the person confirms it after comparing it (`vl identity confirm` in the clone; an admin's new laptop, or a device confirmed with `--confirm`). Until then a clone takes no ring and seals no secret. **A ring of another project with the same rule path seals this project's secrets.** Rule scope names depend on the path only (`rule-<billing>` is the same in every project), and a key ring records nothing of the project it belongs to. Attacker: a compromised server. Controls: the rings it serves to a device's first clone of a project. Harm: an admin runs two projects with a `billing/` line; their new laptop (confirmed, holding project A's keys) clones project B, and the server hands it A's `billing/` ring as B's (started by the same admin, so BUG-840's check passes). The laptop seals B's `billing/.env` under A's key: A's finance, who have nothing in B, open it. The same holds for `team` in S1 and S2 (a first clone takes `team` on first sight). | Rings are not bound to their project; a first clone has no anchor but the server's word | `server_hub` `adversarial_s2::bug_845_a_ring_of_another_project_never_seals_a_secret`. Suggested fix (crypto and format: the owner's approval): bind each ring to its project, e.g. the genesis signs the project's `team` genesis hash (and `team`'s the project id), checked by every replay; and give a first clone an anchor from outside the server (the invite link can carry the hash of `team`'s genesis) |
| BUG-846 | medium (**fixed**) | secrets / access lines | **Fixed:** a ring `admin_sync` creates is sent even when nobody else is granted. **`vl access billing/ finance` while finance has nobody else in it creates no key ring,** so the admin's own `billing/.env` is "kept local-only (no key for scope 'rule-…')" until someone joins finance and an admin's `vl group add` creates the ring. A confusing failure (the secret silently does not sync). | `admin_sync` returned "nothing changed" when it had only created the ring, and `ring_update` then drops the in-memory ring | `server_hub` `adversarial_s2::bug_846_an_access_line_gets_its_ring_with_nobody_else_in_it` |
| BUG-847 | medium (**fixed**; **pre-existing**: `integration` @ `3851159`, found merging it into "secrets S2 owner fixes") | server / sign-in / YugabyteDB | **Fixed:** sign-in, project creation, group creation and group changes run at READ COMMITTED: their races are decided by unique keys (a name raced for: the loser's insert fails on the key, `23505`, and sign-in runs again and finds the person, so it becomes a pending device; a second project or group of a name: "exists") and by row locks (the manager's right, `FOR SHARE`; the project's bindings, `FOR UPDATE`). **Concurrent sign-ups deadlock on YugabyteDB; under load some answer "the database is busy".** Measured: 31 of 192 parallel sign-ups failed on `integration`'s code, 20–64 on this branch's; `verify-server.sh`'s YugabyteDB suite failed 2–21 of 64 tests in most runs (sign-ups, then `vl group create` and `vl group add` timing out), with 1,245–5,000 retried deadlocks per run. Attacker: none (availability under load, and so at agent scale). | On YugabyteDB 2025.2 a SERIALIZABLE read, even a point lookup that finds its row, blocks every insert into the table it read until its transaction ends (shown with two sessions: an insert waits the reader's whole transaction; at REPEATABLE READ or READ COMMITTED it does not wait). A new sign-up reads `devices` by key and `people` by name, then inserts into both: two at once each hold a read the other's insert needs, a deadlock by construction; group creation and changes did the same with `groups`, `group_members` and `people`. Retries only queued them | `server_hub` `concurrency::bug_847_concurrent_sign_ups_all_succeed` (192 parallel sign-ups, and 8 racing for one name: one person, 7 pending devices). Also, same class, in this merge: S2's key-request hint (a table-wide expiry delete and a `groups`/`group_members` read inside SERIALIZABLE transactions: now per project, and after commit) and the pending-device pruning (after sign-in's transaction) |

**Held up** (no finding; by the builder's tests, this pass's tests, or by reading the code), by attack surface:
- **Invites (8):** a join under a key that is not the invite's, under a key the server chose, or moved onto another device (the replay and the server refuse); a second use, in the ring and on the server; use after expiry (the share's signed time against the admin-signed `expires_at`); a join for another person, or by a device signed in as another person; a server-substituted invite key (the ring's admin-signed `invite` names the real one); the link's secret in the server's tables, files, audit log, `vl` errors and the op log (`vl join` runs outside a repository); a name squatted before the invitee joins (refused join, noticed: denial of service only, as any first-come name).
- **Proofs (7):** a vouch by another person's device, by a revoked device, or moved onto another device; a share to a device or signing key the ring ever held; cross-ring and cross-project replay of a change (the signature covers the scope and the previous hash); `hub-invite` and `hub-device` signatures used for each other or for `hub-login` (separate contexts and messages); the server's and the devices' replays agree (same `verify`, the share's own signed time).
- **Per-rule keys (6):** `billing/` cannot open `infra/.env` or a nested rule's secret; last-match ordering and nested lines (the device's `rule_scope` and the server's `deciding` are the same function); a line edited to name another group rotates the key away from the old one; a line made `public` (names nobody) moves new secrets to `team`; a hand-written access line with no ring keeps its secret on the device, with a warning; non-admins cannot write `.veilpolicy` (the server's access check on the root).
- **Background sharing (5):** hostile listings (forged joins and vouches, re-listed removed devices) share nothing the replay refuses; shares racing a removal (the builder's concurrency test, under load: 8 copies on PostgreSQL, 3 on YugabyteDB); `VEIL_KEY_SHARE=0`; the detached child (no stdin, its own process group, a 0600 log, `VEIL_KEY_SHARE=0` in its environment); a pass never shares to a device without a proof.
- **Device removal (3):** a device hidden by the server is found among the rings' members; a removed device re-listed with its old vouch is not shared to again; a member's `revoke_own` reaches only their own devices.
- **Formats (3):** rings that say format 3 but hold shares, format 5, or malformed JSON, are refused or dropped; a format-6 clone keeps its own ring and warns.

**Accepted by design** (`docs/HUB.md` §13.12, rows 15–16; not filed): a server that misreports group members or admins can make an admin's `vl access` or `vl group add` grant a *real teammate* a rule's key (never its own device); a manager's removal does not rotate, and a member's background pass may still share to a person a manager removed before anyone shared to them.

**Out of scope (trusted):** a member who shares their key to a device that is not the person's (they hold the key); a person who vouches for a device that is not theirs; an admin who confirms a fingerprint without comparing it.

**Residual (not filed, no practical test):** the invite link is a command-line argument of `vl join`, which other OS users of the same machine can read (`/proc/<pid>/cmdline`) while it runs. For a name nobody has yet, one who reads it and registers the name before `vl join`'s own sign-in (a window of milliseconds) joins in the invitee's place; the invitee's join then fails ("a name somebody has"), so it is noticed. **Done** (secrets S2 owner fixes): `vl join -` reads the link on standard input, and `vl invite` and the guide say to use it (`server_hub` `projects::an_invite_names_the_projects_fingerprint`).

**Nits** (not filed; **both fixed** in secrets S2 owner fixes): `Keychain::check_with_keys` checks that a `Revoke` or `Rotate` generation unlocks the previous one, but not a `RevokeOwn`'s (`keychain.rs`, the one match the new kind was not added to; now checked, `core_security` `ring_shares::a_revoke_own_rotation_must_unlock_the_previous_generation`); unbounded pending devices (no cap per person since S2) let anyone grow the `devices` table, which only a sign-in under the same name prunes (now at most 32 per person, the oldest pushed out, never one whose fingerprint the person looked for in the last hour: `server_hub` `secrets2::pending_devices_made_by_others_never_hide_the_real_one`, with floods in parallel).

## Adversarial pass: simplification part 2

Security review (layer 3, tier A, Deep) of `t3/b837a04e` @ `dcf9127` (only secret files are encrypted, repository format 8), merged onto `integration` @ `1b39297`. Every finding was reproduced by a failing test, checked a second time against the code, and checked on `1b39297` (a worktree of it, the same test) before being called a regression.

| ID | Severity | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproduction |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-942 | important (regression; **fixed**, part-2 review fixes) | embargo / `vl squash` / push to the server | **Fixed:** `Transaction::commit` refuses any rewrite that drops an unreleased embargoed merge, or replaces it with a snapshot without its embargo, while a change built on it survives (`check_embargoed_merges`, since replaced by BUG-990's invariant, `check_withheld`, which keeps these tests); a merge of concurrent operations drops such a rewrite instead (`merge_rebase`). Tests: `adversarial2_embargo::squashing_the_embargoed_merge_into_its_follow_up_keeps_the_fix_withheld`, `embargo_copy_cli::squashing_the_embargoed_merge_away_publishes_nothing`, `oplog_adversarial::a_concurrent_abandon_of_the_embargoed_merge_never_unwithholds_its_fix`. Originally: **`vl squash` of the embargoed merge into the change on top of it publishes the fix before its release.** Attacker: anyone the embargo keeps the fix from (project members outside its groups on the server; everyone on Earth for a `public` folder). What they get: the fix. Sequence: `vl merge fix --into main --embargo-for security`, a follow-up change on `main`, then `vl squash -r <merge> --into main` (a destination inside the embargo, so the copy check lets it through). The merge is abandoned, the follow-up keeps the fix in its tree, and nothing in its history is an embargoed merge any more, so `remote::embargo_withheld` withholds nothing and the next `vl push` (`hub::push` → `embargo_blocks`: `None`) uploads the fix. `integration` 1b39297 refused this squash ("a change under an embargo can only be squashed whole into its parent"). | The refusal was a check on the embargo's wrapper tree (`commands.rs` on 1b39297, `squash_into`: `wrapper_of(&store, t)`), deleted with the wrapper; nothing keyed on `Snapshot.embargo` replaced it. The class: a rewrite that removes the embargoed merge from history while its tree survives in descendants is treated as withdrawing the embargo (`transaction.rs:709`, `confinement::active_embargoes`). Smallest fix for the class: refuse rewriting an unreleased embargoed merge away (whole squash, abandon) while it has descendants, as `vl split` already refuses (`commands.rs:418`). | `core_security` `adversarial2_embargo::squashing_the_embargoed_merge_into_its_follow_up_keeps_the_fix_withheld`; `cli_security` `embargo_copy_cli::squashing_the_embargoed_merge_away_publishes_nothing` |
| BUG-943 | important (**pre-existing**: the same leak on `integration` 1b39297, to a public remote; **fixed** with BUG-942) | embargo / `vl abandon` / push to the server | **Fixed** with BUG-942 (the same check). Test: `adversarial2_embargo::abandoning_the_embargoed_merge_keeps_the_fix_withheld`. Originally: **`vl abandon` of the embargoed merge publishes the fix before its release.** The follow-up on `main` is rebased onto the merge's parents (`main` and the fix): a plain merge of the fix, with no embargoed ancestor, so the next `vl push` uploads it (same attacker and harm as BUG-942). The transaction's "moved out of an embargo" check skips it because the merge is no longer visible ("abandoning the embargoed merge itself withdraws the embargo"), although its fix stays in `main`. | `transaction.rs:709` (`active_embargoes` judged after the rewrite); `remote.rs:486` (`embargo_withheld` starts from visible embargoed merges only). Fixed with BUG-942's class. | `core_security` `adversarial2_embargo::abandoning_the_embargoed_merge_keeps_the_fix_withheld` |
| BUG-944 | important (**fixed**, part-2 review fixes) | JSON / embargo | **Fixed:** a file of a snapshot an unreleased embargo withholds whose version is not in the history a push already sends has the tier `embargoed` (`api::EmbargoTiers`, `remote::embargo_base`) in `status`, `ls` and `grep`; the 17 schemas that lost fields are `/2`, listed in `docs/reference/json.md` "Versioning". Test: `embargo_copy_cli::json_never_calls_a_file_under_an_unreleased_embargo_public`. Originally: **The JSON documents call an unreleased embargo's files `public`, and format 8 changed `/1` schemas without a version bump.** `vl ls -r main --json` (and `status`, `show`, `diff`) gives `"tier": "public"`, `"visibility": {"kind": "public"}` for the files the embargoed merge brought in; `docs/reference/json.md` defines `public` as "Plaintext, sent to every remote you may push it to", and no push sends them before the release. Format 8 removed the `embargoed` tier and the `embargo`, `cloak`, `fallback` and `description_hidden` fields under the same `veil.*/1` schemas, which json.md "Versioning" says bumps the version (removing a field, or changing what a value means). A consumer (CI, an agent) that publishes what Veil calls public publishes the fix. | `api.rs:161`, `api.rs:508`, `api.rs:513` (tier and visibility from the entry's label only, which is public for an embargoed merge now); the schema constants unchanged. Smallest fix: an `embargoed` tier value for paths an unreleased embargo withholds (an added enumeration value keeps the version), and a version bump for the removed fields. | `cli_security` `embargo_copy_cli::json_never_calls_a_file_under_an_unreleased_embargo_public` |
| BUG-940 | medium (**fixed**, part-2 review fixes) | `vl upgrade` / formats | **Fixed:** the removed-content check follows a sealed entry's lineage: one at a path no secret rule names is a secret's when its object was a secret's before, a parent held a secret at that path, or a parent's secret of the same scope left its path (a rename with an edit). Test: `format_compat::a_renamed_secret_does_not_block_the_upgrade`. Originally: **`vl upgrade` refuses a format-7 repository that holds only what format 8 itself writes: a secret renamed to an ordinary path.** Format 8 keeps a moved secret sealed at its new path (`builder.rs:824` `protect_moves`, "protection follows a secret across a rename"), as format 7 did. The removed-content check names any encrypted file whose path no built-in secret rule names (`upgrade.rs:293`), so `mv .env settings.txt` in a format-7 repository makes it refuse to upgrade, saying to keep using the old vl or start a new repository (its history is lost to this vl). | The check tells removed content from kept content by the path's name alone; a sealed file at an ordinary path is either (cloaked folder, or a moved secret). | `core_format` `format_compat::a_renamed_secret_does_not_block_the_upgrade` |
| BUG-941 | medium (**fixed**, part-2 review fixes) | `vl upgrade` / formats | **Fixed:** conflict sides are inspected like entries, by the same rule, and named (`PATH (a conflict side): encrypted (…)`). Test: `format_compat::an_encrypted_conflict_side_of_an_ordinary_file_is_named`. Originally: **`vl upgrade` misses a conflict side encrypted for a cloaked folder.** For a conflict entry the removed-content check only decodes the conflict object (`upgrade.rs:295`), so a side sealed under `finance` at `billing/report.txt` (a cloaked folder before format 8: encrypted code) passes, and the upgraded format-8 repository keeps encrypted code, which the check exists to refuse (`docs/FORMAT.md` §5 "Format 8": "an encrypted file whose path no built-in secret rule names … refuses"). No plaintext leaks (the side stays ciphertext). | Conflict terms are not walked with the tree entries' rule. | `core_format` `format_compat::an_encrypted_conflict_side_of_an_ordinary_file_is_named` |

**Held up** (tested or traced): the only route that uploads content is `hub::push` (`client.upload` has no other caller), and it withholds every tag whose history reaches an unreleased embargoed merge or the fix it merged, including combined embargoed lines, rebased and described merges, and the fix tag itself; the server still refuses embargoed snapshots, and its upload decodes canonically, so a snapshot with a `cloak` field or a sealed description from an older vl is refused there; the hub never syncs embargo records, so a forged release can only come from the test-only path remotes (debug builds with `VEIL_TEST_PATH_REMOTES`): BUG-108's removed checks guarded a route the product no longer has; decoders refuse embargoed tree entries, fallbacks (trees and conflict terms), sealed directories, sealed descriptions and tag messages, and a snapshot's `cloak` field (non-canonical); `vl upgrade` scans loose and packed objects and every retained operation's view, refuses formats 1 to 5 and removed content before taking any lock, and leaves `.veil/` byte for byte unchanged (`format_compat::every_set_before_format_8_is_refused_and_left_unchanged`); a `cloak` line on a non-secret file isolates it (fails safe); a sealed secret keeps its audience across rebase, merge and moves; personal keys named `id_*` are refused by the server; restricted folders: S1's server suite (`scripts/verify-server.sh`) as recorded in the report.

**Deleted tests that guarded something still needed:** `core_durability` `adversarial_lock::a_live_lock_holder_is_never_robbed` (a holder whose lease looks expired but whose process is alive keeps `.veil/lock`) ran only on a format-5 repository and was deleted; `RepoLock` still serialises `vl upgrade`, gc, the vault and key-ring writes, and no remaining test covers that case (`lock.rs` unit tests cover fresh leases and other hosts). Not filed: a coverage gap, no failure shown.

**Out of scope (trusted):** `vl cloak id_rsa` or `vl cloak deploy.pem` seals a personal key and syncs it as ciphertext (the server refuses only `id_*` names): a deliberate command, unchanged from 1b39297; a device clock set past an embargo's deadline releases it locally (the member's own device).

### Simplification cycle 2

Security review (layer 3, tier A, Deep) of the cycle-1 fixes, `t3/b837a04e` @ `bd42e3a` (new diff `375dc4c..bd42e3a`), merged onto `integration` @ `0fbe207`. Each finding has a failing test, was re-traced in the code, and was run on `0fbe207` (a worktree of it, the same test) before being called a regression.

| ID | Severity | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproduction |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-990 | important (**pre-existing**: the same leak on `integration` 0fbe207; the class of BUG-942/943; **fixed**, simplification cycle 2) | embargo / `vl rebase` / push to the server | **Fixed:** one invariant at transaction commit replaces the abandon and squash special case (`Transaction::check_withheld`): every snapshot an unreleased embargo withheld in the view the transaction started from that is still visible must still be withheld, unless the embargo was released or withdrawn (no visible snapshot carries it and nothing built on its merges survives the rewrites, as when the merge is undone or abandoned bare). A merge of concurrent operations drops the rewrites of that embargo's merges instead (`merge_rebase`). Transactions without rewrites need no check: what a visible embargoed merge withholds depends on its parents only (false, BUG-1016: replaced in simplification cycle 3 by the rule by change, `remote::Withheld`, checked on every view change). BUG-942/943's tests pass unchanged. Originally: **A rebase of the embargoed merge that keeps its embargo but changes its parents publishes the fix before its release.** Attacker: anyone the embargo keeps the fix from (members outside its groups; everyone for a `public` folder). What they get: the fix's files, content and description. Sequence: `vl merge fix --into main --embargo`, then `vl rebase -r main -d fix -d <v1>` (parents swapped), `-d fix` (the fix alone) or `-d <v1>` (the fix dropped). The embargo stays pending and `main` stays withheld, but the `fix` tag is no longer withheld, so the next `vl push` (`hub::push` → `embargo_blocks`) uploads it. | `remote::embargo_withheld` (`remote.rs:486`) takes the fix to be the history behind the merge's *non-first* parents, judged on the rewritten merge; `check_embargoed_merges` (`transaction.rs:650`) accepts any rewrite whose final keeps the `embargo` field ("a rebase of the merge … keeps the embargo"), and `confinement_violations` only judges the rewritten changes, not the fix the merge stops covering. Smallest fix for the class: in the commit check, refuse a transaction after which a snapshot an unreleased embargo withheld before is still visible but no longer withheld (withheld-before ∩ visible-after ⊆ withheld-after), which also covers BUG-942/943. | `cli_security` `embargo_copy_cli::rebasing_the_embargoed_merge_onto_other_parents_publishes_nothing` |
| BUG-991 | important (regression: `integration` 0fbe207 refuses, "cannot be read as the public sees it"; **fixed**, simplification cycle 2) | embargo / `vl blame --public` / JSON | **Fixed:** `blame::blame` with `Reader::Public` refuses a revision a push withholds (`remote::embargo_blocks`): a public mirror does not have it, and a revision it has holds none of the withheld history. The other public-view readers were checked: `Reader::Public` is also used by inexact rename detection (`merge.rs`) and attribute reading (`attributes.rs`), both internal and showing nothing; `--public` exists only on `vl blame`; public remotes (a debug test fixture) push through `push_closure`, which withholds. Originally: **`vl blame --public` shows an unreleased embargo's fix as a public mirror's view.** `vl blame FIX.c -r main --public --json` (`"public": true`, "annotated as a public mirror sees the history") lists the fix's lines, attributed to the fix's change with its description; no push sends any of it before the release. A consumer that publishes the public view (a docs site, CI) publishes the fix (same harm as BUG-944). | `blame::Reader::Public` ("plaintext objects and released embargoes only", `blame.rs:71`) only refuses sealed objects; format 8 made embargoed content plain, and nothing in the walk asks `remote::embargo_withheld`. Smallest fix: with `Reader::Public`, treat a snapshot a push withholds (`embargo_blocks`) as absent, as a public mirror lacks it. | `cli_security` `embargo_copy_cli::blame_public_never_shows_an_unreleased_embargo_as_public` |
| BUG-992 | important (the rest of BUG-944; **fixed**, simplification cycle 2) | JSON / embargo | **Fixed:** a file-diff entry of `vl diff --json` and `vl show --json` (and `diff`/`show` over JSON-RPC) has a `tier`, `embargoed` when an unreleased embargo withholds either version (`api::diff_body_with`, `EmbargoTiers` of each side's snapshot); an added field, so `/2` stays. Audit of every other JSON output: `status` (changes and carried paths), `ls` and `grep` give the tier; the policy tiers of `scan` and the accepted classification describe paths, not versions; log, tag, op and snapshot documents carry no tier or visibility; conflict versions are under their entry's tier. Originally: **`vl diff --json` and `vl show --json` call an unreleased embargo's files public.** `vl diff --from <v1> --to main --json`, `vl diff -r fix --json` and `vl show fix --json` give `"visibility": {"kind": "public"}` for the fix's files, and a file diff has no `tier` to say otherwise; `docs/reference/json.md` defines a visibility as "who can read a file". BUG-944's row named `show` and `diff`. | `api::change`/file-diff entries take the visibility from the entry's label only; `api::EmbargoTiers` is applied in `status`, `ls` and `grep` (`api.rs:746`, `api.rs:1980`, `api.rs:2581`) but not in diff or show. Smallest fix: give a file-diff entry the same `tier` (an added field keeps `/2`) from `EmbargoTiers` of the `--to` snapshot. | `cli_security` `embargo_copy_cli::diff_and_show_json_never_call_a_file_under_an_unreleased_embargo_public` |

**Held up** (tested or traced): `vl undo --op <the merge>` after a follow-up was built on it, and `vl undo`/`vl op restore`, only replace the view: a surviving descendant keeps the merge reachable, so `visible_embargoed` still finds it and the follow-up and fix stay withheld (probe: nothing reached a public remote); `vl edit <merge>` with a follow-up on it and a working-copy edit keeps the embargo (the edit is `[embargoed]` in `vl status`) and publishes nothing; `vl describe` keeps it; `vl split` of the merge is refused; abandon and whole squash of the merge with a follow-up are refused, and a concurrent abandon is dropped by the op-log merge (`embargoed_merge_violations` in `merge_rebase`); the `embargoed` tier in `status`, `ls` and `grep`. `vl upgrade`'s lineage rule (BUG-940) is broader than "a secret's lineage" (a sealed entry is accepted when *any* secret of the same scope left its path in that snapshot, and an object id seen as a secret's in any earlier-visited, not necessarily ancestral, snapshot), so a format-7 history that deletes `.env` while adding `billing/x` under the same scope keeps that file encrypted after the upgrade; not filed: what is accepted is a `cloaked` file entry, which format 8 itself writes and reads (a moved secret), so nothing is mis-read and it fails safe (stays sealed for its scope). Conflict sides are walked by the same rule (BUG-941).

**Not filed (no failure shown):** `api::EmbargoTiers::of` → `remote::embargo_base` re-runs `push_closure` from every first parent while walking down a withheld line, so `vl status`/`ls` on a change k deep above an embargoed merge does O(k × history) snapshot reads while an embargo is pending. **Addressed** (simplification cycle 2): `embargo_base` no longer runs `push_closure`; a snapshot builds on withheld history exactly when one of the lowest withheld snapshots is it or its ancestor, one bounded graph-index query (`reachable_among`) per first parent walked.

**Out of scope (trusted):** none.

### Simplification cycle 3

Security review (layer 3, tier A, Deep) of the cycle-2 fixes, `t3/b837a04e` @ `0e7d859` (new diff `94aba9b..0e7d859` first-parent, plus the hand-resolved merge of `crates/vl/src/hub.rs` with the server bench fixes, `integration` `f7e9aac`). Each finding has a failing test and was run on `94aba9b` (a worktree of it, the same test) before being called a regression or pre-existing. The branch no longer merges cleanly into `integration` @ `b3ac86e` (performance round 3): `transaction.rs` (`Transaction::commit`, where `check_withheld` runs), `lib.rs` and `core_durability/durability.rs` conflict, so the merge with it is a new hand merge of a tier-A file and needs its own review.

| ID | Severity | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproduction |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-1015 | important (regression: `94aba9b` refuses, "cannot drop or replace the embargoed merge … is built on it"; **fixed**, the withholding rule by change) | embargo / `vl squash` / push to the server | **Fixed** by withholding by change rather than by snapshot id (`remote::Withheld`, the one source push, confinement, blame, the JSON tiers and the commit check ask): an embargo withholds every version of every change of its fix line, and everything descending from one. The commit check (`Transaction::check_withheld`) compares versions of withheld changes, not ids: after the squash, the fix's new version is a version of a withheld change that nothing withholds, and the embargo is neither released nor withdrawn (the transaction replaced what it withheld), so the squash is refused. Tests un-ignored: both reproductions; and `embargo_copy_cli::random_rewrites_never_push_what_an_unreleased_embargo_withholds`. Originally: **`vl squash` of the embargoed merge into the fix publishes the fix before its release.** Attacker: anyone the embargo keeps the fix from (today, everyone but the device that made the merge: the server refuses embargoed merges; later, members outside its groups, and everyone for a `public` folder). What they get: the fix's files, content and description. Sequence: `vl merge fix --into main --embargo`, optionally a follow-up committed on `main`, then `vl squash -r <the merge> --into fix`. It is accepted; `vl embargo list` still shows the embargo pending, but `fix` and `main` (the follow-up, now a plain merge of the main line and the fix) are no longer withheld, and the next `vl push` uploads them: shown on the server (`server_hub` `sync::squashing_the_embargoed_merge_into_the_fix_never_reaches_the_server`: `dev` clones the fix) and on a public remote. | `Transaction::withheld_violations` (`transaction.rs:673`, `:677`) compares object ids: it only asks whether a snapshot withheld before the transaction is *still visible* and no longer withheld. A squash into the fix rewrites the fix, the merge and everything built on them, so every withheld id is replaced and none is still visible: no violation is found whatever the new snapshots hold. The class: any rewrite whose replacements of withheld snapshots end up outside the embargo (the old `check_embargoed_merges` caught this one by looking at the rewritten merge's surviving descendants). Smallest fix for the class: judge the replacements as well, i.e. map each withheld snapshot through the transaction's rewrites (`resolve`) and require every visible final of one to be withheld afterwards (unless released or withdrawn). | `cli_security` `embargo_copy_cli::squashing_the_embargoed_merge_into_the_fix_publishes_nothing`; `server_hub` `sync::squashing_the_embargoed_merge_into_the_fix_never_reaches_the_server` |
| BUG-1016 | important (**pre-existing**: the same leak on `94aba9b`; the class of BUG-990; **fixed**, the withholding rule by change) | embargo / `vl undo --op` / `vl restore --from` / push to the server | **Fixed** by the same rule: an earlier version of the fix has the fix's change id, so it is withheld wherever it shows up (`vl undo --op`, `vl tag set -r`, `vl new`), `vl restore --from` it outside the embargo is refused (confinement asks the same rule), and its JSON tier is `embargoed`. Every view change is now checked, rewrites or not. The fix line also includes every earlier version of the merge (`predecessors`), so abandoning the fix (the merge rebased down to one parent) and then bringing back a version of it by `vl undo --op` keeps it withheld (found by the randomized test). Originally: **An earlier version of a change the embargo withholds is not withheld.** Attacker: as BUG-1015. Sequence: commit the fix and tag it `fix`, `vl describe -r fix` (any rewrite of the fix before the merge), `vl merge fix --into main --embargo`, then `vl undo --op <the describe>` (the member wants the old description back): the `fix` tag returns to the earlier version, which holds the fix, and the next `vl push` sends it. `vl restore --from <the earlier version>` onto another line (or `vl new`/`vl tag set -r` on it) copies or exposes it the same way, and `vl show <it> --json` calls its files `public`. The claim "ref-only transactions need no check" (BUG-990's fix) is false for this route. | `remote::embargo_withheld` (`remote.rs:486`) withholds only the snapshots behind a visible embargoed merge, by id; the other versions of the same changes (same change id, hidden by rewrites before the merge) are neither withheld nor known to `check_withheld` (skipped for transactions without rewrites, `transaction.rs:1084`) or to `check_confinement`. Smallest fix for the class: withhold by change, not by snapshot: a snapshot whose change id is that of a withheld snapshot is withheld too (in `embargo_withheld`, so push, confinement, blame and tiers all follow). | `cli_security` `embargo_copy_cli::an_earlier_version_of_the_fix_is_withheld_too` |
| BUG-1017 | medium (**pre-existing** on `94aba9b`; **fixed**) | embargo / `vl embargo list` | **Fixed:** `vl embargo list` shows an unreleased embargo that no visible snapshot carries as `withdrawn` (`remote::withdrawn_embargoes`), and a push (to the server or a path remote) that publishes history a withdrawn embargo used to hold back warns (`remote::withdrawn_warnings`, from its merges among the snapshots this device has indexed). Originally: **A withdrawn embargo is still listed as pending.** After `vl undo` of `vl merge --embargo` (or abandoning the bare merge), nothing withholds the fix any more and the next push publishes the fix line as soon as a tag reaches it (the documented limitation, `docs/guide/embargoes.md` "Undoing the merge undoes the embargo"), but `vl embargo list` still says `manual release` and the push says nothing, so the member is told the fix is still withheld. | `vl embargo list` (`main.rs:1131`) reads the keychain's embargo records; withdrawal is computed from the graph (`remote::embargo_withheld`) and never reflected there. Smallest fix: list such an embargo as withdrawn (no visible merge carries it). | `cli_security` `embargo_copy_cli::a_withdrawn_embargo_is_not_listed_as_pending` |

**Held up** (tested or traced): `vl rebase -r main` onto other parents is refused (BUG-990's test); `vl rebase -r fix -d other` and `-s fix -d other` keep the fix withheld (the merge follows); `vl rebase -r fix -d <v1>`; moving `main` back off the merge (`vl tag set main -r v1 --allow-backwards`) keeps the merge visible as a head and publishes nothing; `vl duplicate` of an earlier version publishes nothing untagged; `vl blame --public` refuses a withheld revision on the command line and over JSON-RPC (`api::blame`); `diff`/`show` tiers over JSON-RPC (`serve.rs` `read_document`), `status` carried paths; JSON-RPC write results (`split`, `squash`, `duplicate`) name paths only. `remote::embargo_base`'s lowest-withheld query is equivalent to `push_closure` (an ancestor in the withheld set leads, down withheld parents, to a lowest one). The `hub.rs` hand merge only drops format-7 `fallback`/`cloak` and keeps `embargo_blocks` before the walk: `Introduced` is the bench fixes' (8 MiB `Uploads` batches, `a_push_sends_exactly_what_the_server_lacks` passes; it lost its fallback cases and gained no secret-file case, which names a blob as any file does), the walk starts only from tags `embargo_blocks` let through, `pull` is the bench fixes' streaming with the fallback child removed, and no format-7 field is left in `hub.rs` or the server.

**Out of scope (trusted):** withdrawal itself. `vl undo` of the embargoed merge, or abandoning it with nothing built on it, makes the fix line ordinary again and the next push publishes it, with its descendants, once a tag reaches it. That is the member's own command, returns the repository to the state before the merge (where the same tag would have been pushed), needs a tag on the fix line (the guide's workflow keeps the fix in an untagged workspace), and is documented (`docs/guide/embargoes.md` "Limitations today", `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` threat model). What is not trusted is the misleading record around it (BUG-1017).


### Simplification cycle 4

Security review (layer 3, tier A, Deep) of the withholding rule by change, `t3/b837a04e` @ `7b3456a` (new diff `82429c6..7b3456a` first-parent: `f86a10b`, and the hand merge `719be5a` of `Transaction::commit` with performance round 3, `b3ac86e`), merged onto `integration` @ `a97af94`. Each finding has a failing test, was re-traced in the code, and was run on `82429c6` (a worktree of it, the same test) or traced through code this diff does not change before being called pre-existing.

| ID | Severity | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproduction |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-1040 | important (**pre-existing**: the same leak on `82429c6`, which lists the embargo as `manual release`; the class of BUG-942/943, by a new route; **fixed**) | embargo / `vl squash` / `vl abandon` / push to the server | **Fixed:** `Transaction::check_withheld` also requires every withheld snapshot the transaction rewrote (not abandoned) to have visible finals that are still withheld, unless the embargo was released, and a transaction that rewrote anything withheld is never a withdrawal; it runs on every transaction with rewrites (not only when the visible merges change), after the confinement check. Both routes are refused. Test un-ignored. Originally: **Squashing the fix into work built on the embargoed merge, then dropping the merge, publishes the fix while its embargo is pending.** `vl new main; …; vl squash -r fix --into fu` is allowed (a copy inside the embargo: `fu` descends from the merge) and abandons the emptied fix, so the merge is rebased down to one parent and the fix's content now lives only in `fu`. `vl abandon main` (or `vl squash -r main --into fu`) then rebases `fu` onto v1, and the next push uploads it, with the fix's files, content and description. Attacker: anyone the embargo keeps the fix from (readers of the public remote or the server's public folders). They control nothing; the member ran two ordinary commands, the first inside the embargo and the second refused in BUG-943's form (abandoning the merge with work on it). Harm: the fix is public before its release. The record also misleads: `vl embargo list` says `withdrawn`, and the push gives no warning (`withdrawn_warnings` asks the old merges' rule, under which the moved `fu` is not withheld either). | `transaction.rs:736` (`withheld_violations`) compares only **versions** of fix-line changes (`Withheld::versions_from`), and none is visible after the squash; a snapshot withheld only by **descent** (`fu`) can lose it unchecked. `replaced` (a withheld snapshot rewritten, not dropped) is computed but only consulted for a version that was lost. `check_confinement` (`transaction.rs:851`) skips any embargo whose merge is no longer visible (`active_embargoes`), so the move of `fu` off the embargo is not judged either. Class: a rewrite that keeps withheld content visible but outside every visible merge's fix line and descent. The fix that closes it compares what the transaction **replaced**: every withheld snapshot rewritten (not abandoned) must have finals that are still withheld, unless the embargo was released; a transaction that replaced one never counts as a withdrawal. | `embargo_copy_cli::the_fix_squashed_into_work_on_the_merge_stays_withheld_when_the_merge_goes` (both routes) |
| BUG-1041 | important (**pre-existing**: `Repo::restore_view_keeping_edits` and `undo_operation_keeping_edits` are unchanged since `82429c6`; **fixed**) | `vl undo --op` / `vl undo` / working copy / embargo | **Fixed:** `vl undo` (with or without `--op`) and `vl redo` carry over a workspace's edits only from a view whose working copy is **the same change** as the current one: the undone operation's (edits recorded since then, which successive undos must keep: `undo_redo_props`), else the last command's (`Repo::last_command_op`, as `vl op restore`), else none. Once the working copy moved to another change (`vl edit`, `vl new`), that change's content is never carried as an edit. (The last command's view alone loses an edit carried by an earlier undo.) Tests un-ignored, with the randomized seeds `0x7777deadbeef0001` and `0xa1b2c3d4e5f60718`. Originally: **`vl undo --op` copies the change being edited into the working copy it restores.** `vl abandon other` (the working copy moves onto v1), `vl edit fixchild` (or `vl new fixchild`), `vl undo --op <the abandon>`: the working copy goes back to its change on `other`, and that change now holds the fix's files (`vl status`: `A FIXNAME-K7.c`, `A FIX2-K7.c`, `A child.c`, with no `[embargoed]` tier). The next `vl commit` and push publish them while the embargo is pending. Plain `vl undo` reaches it too, after an earlier `undo --op` moved the working copy (randomized seed `0xa1b2c3d4e5f60718`, case 4). Attacker and harm as BUG-1040. Without an embargo it is a wrong result: another change's content lands silently in the restored working copy. | `workspace.rs:891` passes the **undone** operation's view as the base of "the edits recorded since" (`before_edits`); `restore_view_keeping_edits` (`workspace.rs:1130`–`1188`) then 3-way merges the difference between that operation's working copy and the current one into the restored working copy. When the working copy moved to another change since (`vl edit`, `vl new`, an undo), the difference is that change's whole content, not edits. The rewrite keeps the change's parents, so `check_confinement` (which judges only rewrites that change parents) never sees the copy. `vl op restore` uses the last command's view (`workspace.rs:1018`), which is right. | `embargo_copy_cli::undo_never_copies_the_change_being_edited_into_another` (both routes); `embargo_copy_cli::random_rewrites_and_working_copy_commands_never_push_what_an_unreleased_embargo_withholds` (seed `0x7777deadbeef0001`, case 6) |

**Held up** (tested or traced): the hand merge of `Transaction::commit` with performance round 3 (`719be5a`) keeps round 3's code byte for byte apart from the check: `check_withheld` (on any view change) and `check_confinement` run before the staging, the first barrier and every publication, so a refusal leaves only staged objects that `Drop` publishes unnamed (as any aborted transaction does) and no operation; the other conflicts (`lib.rs`, `core_durability/durability.rs`) are resolved as both sides meant (`push_plan` and `Transaction::ensure_held` went with part 1's path-remote push). `core_durability` `crash::`, `durability::`, `oplog_adversarial::` and `oplog_concurrency::` (62 tests) passed 64 of 64 runs, eight copies at a time. The commit check itself: with the visible embargoed merges unchanged, a visible version of a fix-line change stays withheld (the rule then depends only on the graph), so the early return is sound; reordering the merge's parents (`vl rebase -r main -d fix -d v1`), moving its first parent onto work built on the fix (`-d fixchild -d fix`, `-s main -d fixchild`) are refused; `vl cherry-pick`/`vl duplicate` of the fix or the merge onto another line, `vl squash -r main --into other`, `vl restore --from fix PATH` on another line are refused; `vl split -r fix` keeps both halves withheld (the merge's new fix line holds the new change). The pinned randomized test, and the builder's command set on 3 fresh seeds × 40 cases (`0xa1b2c3d4e5f60718`, `0x0badc0ffee123457`, `0x7777deadbeef0001`), published nothing while an embargo was pending (11 cases ended withdrawn, each listed and warned); the extended command set found BUG-1041 on two of the three seeds. `vl commit` of one file at 10,000 files (release builds, median of 15, three rounds, a busy machine): round 3 (`b3ac86e`) 25/33/46 ms and this branch 25/32/42 ms without an embargo; with one pending, on its line 31/147/36 ms and 27/34/36 ms, on another line 30/51/26 ms and 32/52/27 ms: no regression beyond the noise.

**Not filed (no failure shown):** `vl status --json` and `vl ls --json` on a change k first parents above an embargoed merge take O(k²) (`api::EmbargoTiers` → `remote::embargo_base` asks `Withheld::of_snapshot` once per first parent walked, each a graph walk down to the merge's first parent): 9 ms at k = 51, 24 ms at 201, 334 ms at 801 (release). `82429c6` also queried once per step (traced, not measured). A `main` that keeps moving while an embargo is pending reaches such depths; one query from the starting snapshot would do.

**Not filed (seen under load, no failure tied to this diff):** `scripts/verify-server.sh` failed its YugabyteDB step in both runs, each time a different test and with a contention error, while the machine's load average was about 50: `startup::a_server_on_a_current_schema_runs_no_ddl` (`40001 Restart read required` in its own setup DDL, `CREATE ROLE`/`GRANT`), then `projects::an_admin_bars_a_device_from_their_project_only` (`vl identity confirm`: "the database is busy … (server: busy)"). The other steps (unit, clippy, PostgreSQL, both load runs) passed. Neither route is changed by `82429c6..7b3456a`. To file if it recurs on a quiet machine (the class of BUG-890).

**Out of scope (trusted):** none new. Withdrawal by `vl undo` of the merge, or abandoning it bare, stays as cycle 3 recorded it.
## Adversarial pass: S12 derived data

Security review (layer 3, tier A) of milestone S12 (`t3/be6d12dc` @ `c2e2c28` on `integration` @ `d9f221b`): `crates/veil-server/src/derive/`, `migrations/0004_derived.sql`, the hook in `sync::push`. All are introduced by S12. Reproductions are in `crates/veil-server/tests/server_hub/derived.rs` (module `s12_adversarial`; the container suite, `scripts/verify-server.sh`). Today only the hook reaches the deriver: `derive::landed` after every push, then `memo_tips`. No route serves `view::Reader` yet (S13), so BUG-869 and BUG-871 are latent until S13 serves it.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-866 | medium (**fixed**, S12 fixes) | server / derived graph | **One far-future timestamp stops, or corrupts, the project's derived history:** a member with write access to one folder pushes a snapshot with `timestamp = i64::MAX` (the push accepts it), then any snapshot on top. In debug builds every derive run panics (`attempt to add with overflow`), so no branch of the project is derived again. Release builds wrap instead: generations no longer decrease along parent edges, and `is_ancestor`, `merge_bases` and the paint-down give wrong answers. This contradicts §10's "a hostile timestamp only lengthens a walk". | `derive/graph.rs:70` `p.generation + 1` on an unbounded client timestamp. Push checks no timestamp. Suggested fix: clamp the timestamp used for the generation (e.g. to `i64::MAX / 2`) and add with `saturating_add`, so the generation stays strictly decreasing. **Fixed:** `GraphRow::compute` clamps the timestamp it takes to `MAX_STAMP` (`i64::MAX / 4`) and adds with `saturating_add` (depth too), so generations strictly decrease along every edge whatever a pusher chose. No other arithmetic in `derive/` takes a client value unbounded (similarity counts are capped by the 1 MiB file limit; depths and row numbers are counts). | `bug_866_a_far_future_timestamp_breaks_the_commit_graph` (through `Craft::push_edited`) |
| BUG-867 | medium (**fixed**, S12 fixes) | server / derive memory | **One push can drive the server out of memory:** a batch holds every queued `file_history` row, list row, changed-paths object and per-path map entry of up to 512 snapshots before writing anything. A member alternating one folder between two trees uploads a few hundred bytes per snapshot, yet each snapshot changes tens of thousands of paths. Memory rose **432 MiB / 1,156 MiB / 2,247 MiB** over 16 / 48 / 96 snapshots of 50,000 paths each, about 22 MiB per snapshot.
