A full batch of 100,000-path changes would need about 23 GiB, and the whole server process (every project) goes down with it. | `derive/mod.rs:536` flushes by snapshot count only (`FLUSH_EVERY`). `Writes` and `Batch::history` grow with the paths each snapshot changed. Suggested fix: also flush when the queued rows or object bytes pass a bound (e.g. 100,000 rows or 64 MiB). **Fixed:** a batch is written every 512 snapshots, 100,000 queued `file_history`/`rule_history` rows or 64 MiB of queued objects, whichever comes first (`Writes::full`), so its memory is bounded whatever the snapshots changed; one snapshot's own rows are queued whole, as its change list is in memory anyway. | `bug_867_a_derive_batch_holds_unbounded_memory` (`VEIL_BUG867_SNAPSHOTS`, `VEIL_BUG867_FILES`) |
| BUG-868 | medium (**fixed**, S12 fixes) | server / derive work | **`many` snapshots slow every later landing, everyone's:** each snapshot of more than 100,000 changed paths is kept in `Batch::many`. For every path of every later change, and per parent, each `many` snapshot on the line is checked against the trees (`at_depth` plus two `entry_at`). They cost a member next to nothing to make (one folder alternating between two trees; up to 100,000 snapshots per push). After 30 of them, an unrelated landing of 5,000 files took **58 s instead of 0.48 s** (the same history with the folder just under the cap). Work grows as paths × `many` snapshots: row 10's "within 2× linear" does not hold. | `derive/mod.rs:639`–`656` (`last_changes`) scans all of `self.many` per (path, parent); `changed_path` (`:734`) reads two trees each time. `Batch::new` also loads every `*` row on each run. Suggested fix: write `file_history` rows for `many` snapshots too (only the object is capped), and drop the per-path tree check. **Fixed:** every changed path of a `many` snapshot gets its `file_history` row like any other change's (only the object is capped), so `last_changes` finds it by path; the `many` list, its per-path tree checks and the `*` rows are gone. | `bug_868_many_snapshots_slow_every_later_landing` (`VEIL_BUG868_MANY`) |
| BUG-869 | medium (latent until S13 serves `Reader`, **fixed**, S12 fixes) | server / derived reads | **`Reader::visible` overflows the stack on a chain of merges, aborting the process:** `visible` of a merge calls `rep` of its parents, which calls `visible` of the next merge down: one frame pair per merge. A member pushes 20,000 merges that each take an old snapshot in again; 100,000 are allowed per push. The first `visible(tip)` then dies with `thread 'tokio-rt-worker' has overflowed its stack` / `fatal runtime error: stack overflow, aborting`. That takes down the whole server, not one request. Any S13 log, `rep` or `merge_bases` on such a branch would trigger it, for any viewer. | `derive/view.rs:94`–`128`: `visible` and `rep` recurse into each other, one level per merge on the first-parent line, with the memo filled only on the way back. Suggested fix: compute visibility iteratively (an explicit stack, children after parents) and memoise as it goes. **Fixed:** `Reader::visible` and `rep` are iterative: a snapshot waiting on an older one's visibility goes on an explicit stack, and representatives are memoised as they are walked. The rest of `derive/` has no recursion over history (`changes::walk` recurses over one tree's depth, capped at 1,024). | `bug_869_a_chain_of_merges_overflows_the_readers_stack` (runs the reader in a child process; `VEIL_BUG869_MERGES`) |
| BUG-870 | medium (**fixed**, S12 fixes) | server / derive work | **Every landing does work in proportion to the project's branch count:** each run upserts one `derive_progress` row per branch, and `memo_tips` projects every branch tip for every view key, whether or not the branch moved. Any participant may create branches with no limit (one push can create thousands, all naming one snapshot). With 3,001 branches, one landing took **2.76 s instead of 15 ms** (two view keys). It costs thousands of statements per view key on every push by anyone, on the database every project shares. | `derive/mod.rs:375`–`391` (every branch, every run) and `:872` (`memo_tips`: every branch × every view key). Suggested fix: write progress only for tips that changed, and memoise only the branches the landing moved (`push` knows them: `moved`). **Fixed:** `derive::landed` takes the branches the push moved (`moved`), and the run (`Scope::Branches`), its `derive_progress` rows (one statement) and `memo_tips` touch only those; `Scope::All` is a full catch-up. | `bug_870_branches_multiply_the_work_of_every_landing` (`VEIL_BUG870_BRANCHES`) |
| BUG-871 | low (latent until S13 serves `Reader`; needs a crafted push from someone who may write the folder, **fixed**, S12 fixes) | server / derived visibility | **A tree change with no leaf change is shown to every viewer:** a change that only adds an empty folder in `fin/` (or changes only a hidden directory entry's metadata) is derived as `none`. `Reader::visible` shows a one-parent `none` change to everyone; `sync::projected` hides it from whoever cannot read `fin/`, where it collapses into its parent. The reader then disagrees with the projection, so S13 would list a change of `fin/` only, with its description and author, to anyone on Earth. `vl` never writes empty folders; a finance member's crafted push does (`Graft` accepts the empty tree). This falsifies §10's equivalence claim, which `projection_equivalence` misses because its trees never hold empty folders. | `derive/mod.rs:577` sets `none` whenever no leaf changed, even when the tree did; `derive/view.rs:106` treats `none` as visible to all. Suggested fix: `none` only when the tree equals the first parent's. Otherwise list the change under the lines deciding the directories whose entries changed (or treat it as unlisted). **Fixed:** `none` is only a change whose tree is its first parent's. `changes::diff` also reports the folders that changed with no leaf changing below them (deepest only; `Changed::dirs`), and they are listed under the lines deciding them (`Lines::dir_key`, as `Projector` shows them). `projection_equivalence`'s generator now makes empty folders in every folder. | `bug_871_a_change_of_an_empty_hidden_folder_is_visible_to_all` |

### S12 cycle 2

Security review (layer 3, tier A, cycle 2) of the S12 fixes (`git diff 30d828e 614576f`, `t3/be6d12dc` @ `614576f`) merged onto `integration` @ `0fbe207`. All three findings are introduced by the fixes. Reproductions: `crates/veil-server/tests/server_hub/derived.rs`, module `s12_adversarial` (`bug_980`–`bug_982`, ignored).

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-980 | medium (latent until S13 serves `Reader`; introduced by BUG-869's fix; **fixed**, S12 cycle 2) | server / derived reads | **The iterative `Reader` is quadratic over a run of changes the viewer cannot see:** a merge on top of `k` one-parent changes of `fin/` only (ordinary work by finance; no crafted push needed) costs anyone outside finance `k²/2` steps for the first `visible`, `rep` or `merge_bases` of it, where the recursive reader took `k`. Measured (one `visible` of the merge, anyone on Earth): 2,000 hidden changes **47 s**, 8,000 **603 s** on PostgreSQL; 500 **4.6 s**, 2,000 **52 s** on YugabyteDB (linear would be 4× per 4× changes; it is 11–13×). At 20,000 that is about an hour. Every S13 log of such a branch for anyone on Earth pays it, on a blocking thread. | `derive/view.rs:178` `known_rep` walks the first-parent line down to the first snapshot whose visibility is undecided, returns `Err(that)` and drops `walked` (memoising only on success); `visible` (`:111`) then decides that one snapshot and calls `step` on the merge again, which walks the whole run again from its top. `rep` (`:203`) loops the same way. Suggested fix: decide one-parent snapshots inline as `known_rep` reaches them (they need nothing else), so that it only stops at an undecided merge, and memoise the walked prefix when it stops. **Fixed:** `known_rep` decides one-parent snapshots as it reaches them (listed, withheld, or an equal tree: `Reader::simple`), stops only at a merge not decided yet, and leaves every snapshot it walked pointing at where the walk ended, so no snapshot is walked twice. Measured with `Reader::work` (steps: snapshots walked and list lookups): 4,004 steps for 2,000 hidden changes, 16,004 for 8,000 (553 ms and 1.10 s on PostgreSQL, under load); the test asserts the steps, which do not depend on the machine's load. | `bug_980_a_run_of_hidden_changes_makes_the_reader_quadratic` (`VEIL_BUG980_HIDDEN`) |
| BUG-981 | medium (introduced by BUG-871's fix; **fixed**, S12 cycle 2) | server / derived storage | **The folders a change lists are not counted against `MAX_PATHS`, so a changed-paths object has no size bound:** a snapshot that adds 100,001 empty folders and changes no file is not `many`; its object lists all 100,001 folders (1.9 MB). A member who may write a folder uploads such a tree once, then alternates it, changing one file each time so that no two objects are equal: each snapshot of a few hundred bytes writes an object of megabytes (a million folders: tens of MB each; 100,000 snapshots a push), and every later line rebuild (`prepare_lists`) and S13 read of the change decodes it whole. | `derive/mod.rs` `derive_one`: `ch.len() > changes::MAX_PATHS` decides `many` on the leaves only; `changes::diff` returns `dirs` unbounded and `Changed { paths, similar, dirs }` is encoded whole. Suggested fix: decide `many` on `ch.len() + dirs.len()`. **Fixed:** `derive_one` decides `many` on the leaves and folders together (`ch.len() + dirs.len() > MAX_PATHS`), so no changed-paths object lists more than `MAX_PATHS` entries. | `bug_981_empty_folders_are_not_capped` |
| BUG-982 | medium (introduced by BUG-870's fix; **fixed**, S12 cycle 2) | server / derive worker | **A landing whose run is skipped, fails or is cut by a restart is never resumed by a later landing:** a run now derives and memoises only the branches its own landing moved, and nothing derives the others: no caller runs `Scope::All`, there is no start-up scan, and `derive_progress` is written but never read. A landing that waits out another server's lock (60 s, e.g. behind a long backfill), meets a busy database, or is lost to a restart leaves its branch's tip underived and its projections unmemoised until the same branch moves again (a release branch may never move). Queries still derive on demand (`ensure`) and pulls project on their own, so nothing wrong is served; but the design (phase-3 §S12, "The worker": "leave it to the holder or the next landing"; "the next landing, or the first query, resumes") no longer tells the truth. | `derive/mod.rs` `run`: `Scope::Branches` reads only the named branches; `landed` drops the branches of a run that returned `skipped` or an error. Before the fix, every run derived every tip. Suggested fix: keep the class bounded per landing and still resume: a run also takes the branches whose `derive_progress` row differs from their target (one indexed statement), or a skipped or failed run puts its branches back for the next one. **Fixed:** every landing's run (`Scope::Branches`) also takes each branch whose `derive_progress` is not its target, in the same statement that reads the moved branches, and memoises what it derived; a server catches up at start (`Config::catch_up_at_start`, `derive::resume`, in the background, one project at a time). The reproduction also covers a landing lost to a restart. | `bug_982_a_skipped_landing_is_resumed_by_the_next` (holds the project's lock for the 60 s wait) |

## S27 compressed objects (2026-11-07)

Found while verifying S27 (`scripts/verify-server.sh` on the shared containers, merged with `integration` @ `7a3a9b0`).

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-1005 | low (**fixed**, S27; **duplicate of BUG-1053**, fixed the same way by S10 at the same time; **pre-existing**: server bench fixes) | server / S3 | **Fixed:** an S3 answer of 429 is tried again like a 5xx (jittered backoff, up to 10 s): one implementation, `store.rs` `s3_send`, as BUG-1053 records. **A store asking the server to slow down fails the request:** under load on the shared MinIO, `derived::derived_graph_matches_direct_walks` failed with `S3 PUT: HTTP 429`, 69 times in one run (YugabyteDB suite). S3 and MinIO answer 429 (or 503) when throttling. | `store.rs` `s3_send` (BUG-861) tries again on connection errors and 5xx only; 429 is a 4xx, so it was returned at once and became an error. | `server_hub` `sync::bug_1005_an_object_store_asking_to_slow_down_is_tried_again` (a proxy answers the first eight connections 429) |
| BUG-1006 | low (open; **pre-existing**: S12, `integration` @ `7a3a9b0`; seen once, S27's `verify-server.sh` on `d58a952`) | server tests / YugabyteDB | **A projection computed outside a request fails on a YugabyteDB catalog change:** `derived::projection_equivalence` failed once in the YugabyteDB suite with `40001 … The catalog snapshot used for this transaction has been invalidated … MISMATCHED_SCHEMA` at `derived.rs:591` (`sync::projected` called directly); it passes alone (3 of 3). Other tests' migrations change the shared database's catalog while it runs. | `sync::projected`'s statements run outside `db::transaction` and outside a request, so neither the transaction retry nor `db::retry_requests` (which retries 40001) runs them again; through the API the middleware would. Suggested fix: the test (and any background caller, such as an eager projection at a branch move) retries 40001 as a request does. | none yet (seen once under the full suite) |
| BUG-1007 | low (**fixed**, fix1; **pre-existing**; BUG-970's class) | tests / flaky | **Fixed:** laziness is the count of snapshots searched (one), with no time bound; linearity keeps the fastest of five interleaved runs of each size and compares 8 MiB with the 1 MiB baseline of the same run, so one pause no longer decides it (the search is `memchr::memmem` or a `regex` scan, linear by construction; no work counter reaches inside them). **A wall-clock ratio fails under load:** `core_engine history_tools::pickaxe_is_lazy_and_linear` failed once in S27's `verify-fast.sh` on `4ed6015` (load about 50): "superlinear: 1 MiB 17.157891ms, 8 MiB 722.365066ms". It passes alone (3 of 3, load 49). | `history_tools.rs:643` compares two timings (`tl < ts * 24 + 300 ms`); a pause of the machine during the 8 MiB search alone breaks it. Suggested fix: count the work (bytes or snapshots searched), as the first half of the test already does with `searched`, instead of timing it. | `core_engine history_tools::pickaxe_is_lazy_and_linear` (under load) |
| BUG-1010 | medium until explained (first test **fixed**, race wave, as BUG-1084; the other two **fixed**, fix1, as BUG-970's class; **pre-existing**: none of S27's code is on their paths; seen once, S27's `verify-fast.sh s27b` on `4e9ab01`, load average 122) | tests / flaky under load (client) | **Fixed (the first test only):** `concurrent_status_loops_lose_nothing`'s "last edit is not recorded" was BUG-1084: a `vl status` checked the directory out to an older `@` and the edit was rewritten on disk with its previous version (it stayed in a "recovered working-copy edits" change, never lost from the store); 1 of 6 base failures in 240 runs under load had this form, 0 of 480 after the fix. The other two are not on that path and stay open. **Fixed (the other two, fix1):** `many_concurrent_pushers_to_one_repository` runs a push again when it found the hub's lock busy for its whole 15 s wait (`Locked`, which leaves the remote untouched), until a 600 s deadline; `a_request_after_a_move_is_refused_at_once` stages the newcomer beforehand and renames it in right after the move (it was made between the move and the request, over a second under load, so the server's own once-a-second check stopped it first: the broken pipe), and tries again, safety checked each time, should that check still win. **Three client tests failed together under heavy load and pass alone (3 of 3 each):** `cli_adversarial oplog_cli::concurrent_status_loops_lose_nothing` ("loop 1's last edit is not recorded": left `2`, right `5`), `core_sync push_releases::many_concurrent_pushers_to_one_repository` (`Locked("another process holds the lock")` at `push_releases.rs:189`), `cli_adversarial adversarial7::serve::a_request_after_a_move_is_refused_at_once` (broken pipe at `serve.rs:158`). The last two are timeouts of a lock wait and a pipe under load; the first says an edit made during concurrent `vl status` loops was not recorded, which needs its owner to tell a timing assumption of the test from a lost snapshot. | not found: S27 changed no op-log, lock, push-release or `vl serve` code (its client change is `vl/src/hub.rs` only) | the three tests, under load (several copies at once) |

## Adversarial pass: S27

Security review (layer 3, tier A, Deep) of S27, compressed objects (`t3/e1a8e8ad` @ `d7d28e3`, on `integration` @ `d58a952`). Anyone who can sign in can create a project, and so is a participant who may upload objects.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-1009 | medium (**fixed**, S27 review cycle 1; introduced by S27) | server / uploads | **Fixed:** a compressed frame a client sends may expand at most 64 times its own size (any frame up to 64 KiB decoded may expand more: `encoding::within_ratio`); the server refuses anything else before decoding it, and `vl` sends such an object plain (`encode_to_send`), so its size is the sender's to pay, as before S27. Decoding in uploads also holds a server-wide budget of 256 MiB of decoded bytes (`sync::DECODING_IN_FLIGHT`, a semaphore by bytes), and a blob is no longer copied for its canonical check. Sixteen uploads at once, each decoding 48 MiB within the ratio, raise the server's memory by 301 MiB (821 MiB without the budget). **Small uploads exhaust the server's memory.** Anyone signed in (in a project of their own) sends a few KiB that decode to an object of up to 1 GiB, which the server holds decoded, about twice, while it checks it. Nothing bounds this across requests: eight 4 KiB requests at once (128 MiB of zeros each, 33 KB sent in all) raised the server's peak memory by 1.33 GiB, one by 262 MiB. 1 GiB objects (32 KiB requests) need only a few dozen at once to kill a shared server. Before S27, each such object cost the sender its size in upload. §13.13's accepted risk ("up to 2 GiB per request") is bounded per request only. | `sync::upload` decodes each frame (`encoding::decode`, bounded only by the object limit and the 2 GiB body limit, per request) and runs `Object::decode` on it (a blob is copied), on a blocking thread per request: tokio's blocking pool allows 512 at once. Suggested fix: refuse a frame that expands more than a fixed ratio (`vl` then sends such an object plain, so its cost is the sender's), or bound decoded bytes in flight server-wide. | `server_hub` `sync::bug_1009_a_small_upload_cannot_make_the_server_decode_a_large_object` |
| BUG-1008 | medium (**fixed**, S27 review cycle 1; introduced by S27) | server / encoding / web client | **Fixed:** `encoding::decode` refuses a frame declaring a window above 2^27 (`MAX_WINDOW`, what every decoder reads) on every path, before decoding; and an upload is stored as the server's own `encode` of what it decoded, never as the client sent it, so no client chooses the stored bytes of an object others share (an S3 PUT replaces what the key held). **A participant can make another folder's object unreadable to the web client.** A zstd frame may declare a window far larger than its content (2 GiB here). libzstd's one-shot decoder, which the server and native `vl` use for objects up to 64 MiB, reads it. The pure decoder of the WASM web client refuses any window above 2^27. `dev` (no billing access) uploads such a frame, as encoded frames, for a file billing also holds (the same object: a licence, a vendored file). The server checks it, gives a receipt, and stores it as sent over the admin's encoding. Every snapshot naming that object, billing's included, now serves a frame the web client can't decode. | `encoding::decode` checks the declared size but not the window, and `sync::upload` keeps a client's encoding (`_ if encoded => enc`) under an id the project may already hold. Suggested fix: `decode` refuses a frame whose window is above `MAX_DELTA_WINDOW_LOG` (2^27, what both decoders accept), or the server stores only its own `encode` of what it decoded. | `veil-core` `hub::encoding::tests::bug_1008_a_frame_every_decoder_reads_or_none` (with `--features ruzstd` it also shows the pure decoder refusing); `server_hub` `sync::bug_1008_an_upload_cannot_store_a_frame_the_web_client_cannot_read` |

## Confinement: the allow-list is writable per workspace (2026-11-07)

Found by the owner. Attacker: none; a confinement that breaks the tools it is for (`docs/design/scale/9-confinement.md`, "Writes to the allow-list").

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-1025 | high (**fixed**) | confinement / allow-list | **Fixed:** each allowed folder is an overlay whose lower layer is the real folder and whose upper layer is the workspace's own (`.veil/local/sandboxes/<ws>/layers`, `0700`); an allowed file is a per-workspace copy (`0600`) bound read-write; `--ephemeral` and `--with-isolated` runs get throwaway layers (`--tmp-overlay`); the masks are mounted on top; a folder that holds the project, or anything hidden below its top level, stays read-only; without overlayfs the run goes on read-only and says what breaks. The real folders are never written. **`vl ws exec` showed the allow-list (`~/.cargo`, `~/.rustup`, `~/.claude`, `~/.claude.json`, `~/.codex`, `~/.nvm`, …) read-only, with no writable layer, and `HOME` was a tmpfs gone after the run:** `cargo build` after an agent added a crate failed (`failed to create directory ~/.cargo/registry/cache/…: Read-only file system`, seen with the real cargo), Claude Code, Codex and Gemini could not save their settings or sessions, npm and pyenv installs failed, and nothing a tool wrote survived to the next run. | the design made the allow-list read-only and left the overlay sandboxes for later | `bug_1025_a_confined_cargo_writes_its_home_and_the_next_run_has_it`, `bug_1025_coding_agents_save_their_settings_and_sessions`, `bug_1025_what_a_run_writes_to_the_allow_list_stays_with_that_workspace_only` |
| BUG-1030 | medium (**fixed**, layers verification) | confinement / layers | **Fixed:** before the layers are mounted, whatever the upper layer holds at a masked path is removed (`remove_tree`, links never followed), so the mask lies on the real entry; the warning names the cause it found (overlayfs, tried on `/usr`, or the workspace's layers). **A run that left folders in its layer at `~/.cargo/credentials` and `credentials.toml`, before the real files existed, made every later run of the workspace read-only** once they did: the masks (`/dev/null` bound on a file) could not be mounted onto folders, and the warning said "overlayfs does not work here". The masks still hid the credentials. Attacker: the confined command. Harm: the allow-list read-only for good (a denial of service), and a false diagnosis. | the masks assumed the layer's entry has the real entry's shape | `bug_1030_masks_hold_and_the_layers_mount_whatever_a_run_left_at_a_masked_path` |
| BUG-1031 | high (**fixed**, layers verification) | confinement / layers / workspaces | **Fixed:** a workspace gets a random `id` in the view when it is made (`WorkspaceState::new`; merges and undo carry it with the working copy that won), and its layers are `layers-<hash of the id>`. **After `vl undo` of `vl ws new agent`, the sandbox folder stayed, and a new workspace named `agent` saw the old one's layers** (`cat ~/.claude/x` printed what the earlier workspace wrote): its agent sessions, settings and anything planted there. | layers keyed by the workspace's name | `bug_1031_a_new_workspace_of_the_same_name_never_sees_the_old_ones_layers`, `oplog_merge::a_workspace_keeps_its_identity_through_merges_and_undo` |
| BUG-1032 | medium (**fixed**, layers verification) | confinement / allow-list | **Fixed:** only a folder or a regular file gets a layer; a socket, FIFO or device is bound as it is. A copy opens its file without blocking and checks it is regular once open, and gives up after 30 s; every `bwrap` probe has the same deadline. **`--allow <socket>` made the whole allow-list read-only** (copying it failed: "No such device or address"), **and `--allow <fifo>` hung `vl` before the command started, holding the workspace's lock.** | every allowed non-folder was copied, through a blocking open | `bug_1032_an_allowed_socket_or_fifo_is_bound_as_it_is` |
| BUG-1033 | medium (**fixed**, layers verification; **pre-existing**: `integration` @ `b3ac86e`, found with BUG-1031) | sandbox / workspaces | **Fixed:** the manifest records the workspace's `id` (`workspace_id`); a sandbox whose id is not the workspace's is rebuilt with its files set aside whole (`unrecorded-…`), never synced into, re-imported or recorded. **A new workspace made under the name of one `vl undo` removed got the old one's sandbox files:** its quarantined work (a file that looked like a secret) was in the new workspace's sandbox, and an unrecorded run would have been recorded into the new workspace. | the sandbox was matched to its workspace by name | `bug_1033_a_new_workspace_of_the_same_name_never_gets_the_old_ones_files` |

## Adversarial pass: secret shares

Security review (layer 3, tier A, Deep) of person-to-person secret shares (`t3/efec787d` @ `1dbb696` on `integration` @ `d58a952`): `veil-core/src/secret_share.rs` (C14), `veil-server/src/shares.rs`, migration `0009_secret_shares`, `vl/src/share_cmd.rs`, `docs/HUB.md` §13.17. Attackers: a compromised server (it writes its own rows), a person the sharer cut off, a member at the limits. All four are introduced by this change, and fixed in "secret shares: review cycle 1" on the same branch (the four reproductions run un-ignored; the review's two nits too: `a_version_seen_but_not_written_still_refuses_older_ones`, `a_received_copy_is_never_recorded_after_the_device_gains_the_key`). Reproductions are in `crates/veil-server/tests/server_hub/shares.rs` (the container suite, `scripts/verify-server.sh`); BUG-1061 fails on both PostgreSQL and YugabyteDB.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-1060 | high (**fixed**) | secret shares / compromised server | **Fixed:** the clone that runs `vl secret share` records the share (its id, the last version it sealed) in `.veil/local/secret-shares.json`; `vl secret unshare` forgets it there before telling the server, and the background pass updates only shares it records, built on its own last version; a share the server lists as ended, paused or shared again elsewhere is forgotten too. An unshare from another of the sharer's devices, and a departure a compromised server hides from a recipient confirmed only by `--confirm`, are documented residual risks (HUB.md §13.17). **A share the sharer ended comes back, and the sharing device seals the rotated secret for the person it cut off.** Attacker: a compromised server, with the person the share was taken from (dev keeps their confirmed device). Controls: the share's row: it puts back the last record (genuine, signed by fin's device) at its version and lists the share as active. Harm: fin ran `vl secret unshare … --from dev`, then rotated the secret as the command advises; fin's next background pass sees "its" active share whose file changed, and seals the **new** secret for dev's devices (version 2 in the test). The same holds for a recipient who left the project if no key ring ever recorded their devices (they were confirmed with `--confirm`): only the server's `active` says they left. HUB.md §13.17 row 12 ("an update never revives an ended share") holds only for an honest server. | `share_cmd::update` (`share_cmd.rs:378`, `:396`, `:402`) decides **which** shares to keep up to date from the server's listing (`s.active`, `s.version`); every other field comes from the signed record, but nothing on the device remembers the unshare (`unshare`, `:256`, changes only the server; `State` holds `received` only). Suggested fix: the clone that runs `vl secret share` records the share (its id and the last version it sealed) in `.veil/local/secret-shares.json`, `vl secret unshare` removes it there, and the updater touches only shares in that local set, built on its own last version. This closes BUG-1063 too. An unshare run from another device stays a residual to document | `bug_1060_an_unshare_the_server_forgets_never_seals_a_later_version` |
| BUG-1061 | medium (**fixed**) | server / limits / concurrency | **Fixed:** a new share reserves one on a quota row (`secret_share_quota`, migration 0009) in the READ COMMITTED transaction that inserts it (an insert that finds the share gives it back), and the row starts from the stored count. **Concurrent new shares pass the limit of 256 per person and project:** with 255 stored, 24 new shares sent at once stored **258** (PostgreSQL) and **267** (YugabyteDB). Attacker: any member who may read a secret path. Controls: how many requests they send together. Harm: the bound on stored rows (each up to ~130 KiB of ciphertext) holds only for requests sent one at a time; one burst stores as many shares as it has requests. | `shares::put` (`shares.rs:178`–`200`) counts in one autocommit statement and inserts in another; nothing serialises the two. Suggested fix: make the count part of the insert (`INSERT … SELECT … WHERE (SELECT count(*) …) < 256`), or take a per-(project, sharer) row lock first | `bug_1061_concurrent_new_shares_never_pass_the_limit` |
| BUG-1062 | medium (**fixed**) | secret shares / delivery | **Fixed:** "absent" is decided from the path's own directory entry (`symlink_metadata`): only nothing at all, or a regular file holding the version written before, is ever written over; a link, folder, FIFO or unreadable file is skipped with a note. **A received version replaces the recipient's own symlink at its path:** dev keeps `bill/.env -> ../dev-own.env`; fin's next version is written as a regular file in place of the link. HUB.md §13.17 promises that a copy is written only if the file is absent or holds the version written before, and that "the person's own edit is never written over". Harm: dev's setup is replaced, and dev's tools now read the sharer's values. The same class: a path whose read fails (a FIFO, a file dev made unreadable) also counts as absent and is replaced. | `share_cmd::receive` (`share_cmd.rs:553`): `ctx.fs.read_opt(&vp).ok().flatten()`. Reads never follow links (a link reads as `NotFound`, `vfs/disk.rs:742`), and an error is turned into `None`, so "nothing readable" passes as "nothing there". Suggested fix: decide "absent" from the entry itself (no entry of any kind, `symlink_metadata`), and skip on any read error | `bug_1062_a_share_never_replaces_the_recipients_symlink` |
| BUG-1063 | high (**fixed**) | secret shares / updates | **Fixed** with BUG-1060 (only the clone that ran `vl secret share` updates it). Review cycle 3 went further: a new content is sealed only by `vl secret share`, so no pass can roll the recipient back (BUG-1064). **Every clone on the sharing device keeps the share "up to date" from its own copy:** fin shares `bill/.env` from clone `f`, and also has clone `f2` of the project (another checkout, an agent's). A command in `f2` runs the background pass, which seals `f2`'s **older** `bill/.env` as a new version (version 3 in the test): dev is rolled back to the old secret, and the two clones then take turns. If `f2` is at another revision where `bill/.env` holds a different secret (production rather than staging), that secret goes to dev, who may not see `bill/` at all and whom fin never meant to give it. (A checkout of another revision in `f` itself does the same, by the "its copy changed" rule; whether that is intended is a design question for the owner.) | `share_cmd::update` (`share_cmd.rs:402`) takes a record as "made here" when it is signed by this **device's** key (`load_identity`), which every clone on the device shares; the comment guards against two devices taking turns, but not two clones. Suggested fix: as for BUG-1060, only the clone that ran `vl secret share` updates the share (its local record) | `bug_1063_another_clone_on_the_sharing_device_never_rolls_the_share_back` |

### Secret shares cycle 2

Security review (layer 3, tier A, Deep), cycle 2 of person-to-person secret shares: the fixes of cycle 1 (`t3/efec787d` @ `b36c5bb`, merged with `integration` @ `d981dba` as `8de9cd8`): the local record of shares made (`.veil/local/secret-shares.json`), the forward-only rule, the quota row, BUG-1062's directory-entry check, and the hold-back before a snapshot (`Ctx::record_into`). All four are introduced by that commit. Reproductions are in `crates/veil-server/tests/server_hub/shares.rs` (the container suite; BUG-1066's gate uses PostgreSQL's advisory locks); each fails deterministically. All four are fixed in "secret shares: review cycle 3" (BUG-1064 and BUG-1065 by making content updates explicit), and their reproductions run un-ignored.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-1066 | high (**fixed**) | secret shares / compromised server / concurrency | **Fixed** (review cycle 3): every change of `secret-shares.json` (`vl secret share`, `unshare`, the pass, `vl pull`, the hold-back) is made under an exclusive lock of `.veil/local/secret-shares.lock`, held for no network call; the command re-reads the record there and applies only its own change: a pass bumps or forgets an entry only if it is still at the version it acted on, never re-inserts one, and what is received only moves forward (`share_cmd::with_state`; also `concurrency_the_share_record_under_concurrent_commands`, a pinned-seed property of concurrent commands). **A background pass in flight brings back a share the clone just unshared, and the server then gets the rotated secret sealed for the person cut off (BUG-1060's attack, by a new route).** `vl secret unshare` forgets the share in `secret-shares.json`, then tells the server; a background pass that read the file before (passes start after any `vl` command, at most every 10 s) writes its old copy back at its end, the unshared share in it, whenever it changed anything (here: it forgets another share the server lists as ended). Attacker: a compromised server. Controls: when it answers the pass (here it holds `GET /v1/people/dev/devices` until the unshare is done: the test's database gate), what the listing says (an ended or paused share makes the pass save; so does accepting the pass's own update), then BUG-1060's step: it puts back the last record and lists the share as active. Harm: fin unshared and rotated the secret, as the command advises; fin's next pass seals the **rotated** secret for dev (version 2 in the test). The same lost update between two passes, or a pass and `vl secret share`, silently stops a share's updates (the stale version makes the next pass drop it as "shared again elsewhere"), and between `vl pull` and a pass loses `received`/`seen` (a later version is then taken for the person's own edit). | Every user of `secret-shares.json` (`share_cmd.rs:102`, `:110`) reads it, changes its copy, and writes the whole copy back, with no lock and no re-read: `update` holds it across network calls (`:526`, `:632`), `unshare` writes its removal first (`:381`), `share` (`:305`) and `receive` likewise. Suggested fix (the class: read-modify-write of the record): under a lock of the record file, re-read it just before writing and apply only this command's own changes to the entries still there (never re-insert one that is gone, nor a version older than the one recorded) | `bug_1066_a_pass_in_flight_never_brings_back_an_unshare` |
| BUG-1064 | high (**fixed**) | secret shares / updates / workspaces | **Fixed** (review cycle 3, by a change of approach, owner's delegate): content updates are explicit. A new content is sealed only by `vl secret share`; the background pass seals the same content again for new devices, from the sharer's own last version, never from the working copy. The forward rule and its revision tracking are gone. **The forward rule checks one workspace and seals the file of another: a `vl ws switch` to an older workspace rolls the recipient back.** The admin (whose commits record `bill/.env`) rotates the secret, commits, shares the rotated value with dev, then `vl ws new before -r @--; vl ws switch before`: the directory holds the value from before the rotation, and the next pass seals it (version 2). HUB.md §13.17 ("never a changed file while the working copy is on an older revision") and the commit's "pauses content updates while the working copy is on an older revision" do not hold. A pass racing a `vl ws switch` is not caught either: a switch changes the directory's checkout record, not the view that `fresh_bases` re-reads. | `update` (`share_cmd.rs:557`) takes the revisions from `made.workspace` (the workspace `vl secret share` ran in, `:318`) but reads the file from the project directory (`ctx.fs`), which may be attached to any workspace. Suggested fix: no content update while the directory is attached to another workspace than `made.workspace` (the checkout record's workspace, read before and after the file, as the revisions are) | `bug_1064_switching_the_directory_to_an_older_workspace_never_rolls_the_share_back` |
| BUG-1065 | medium (**fixed**) | secret shares / updates | **Fixed** with BUG-1064 (no automatic content update exists). **An earlier version of the change the last value was sealed on counts as "forward".** The admin commits a value (never shared), squashes a newer one into the same change, shares it, then `vl new <the earlier snapshot>` (where `vl evolog`, or an undo of the squash, lead): the earlier value is back in the directory, and the next pass seals it (version 2). The review skill's step 5.6 in reverse: a change id says "the same change", not "newer". | `forward` (`share_cmd.rs:144`, `:163`) accepts any ancestor whose change id is the base's, so every version of that change, older ones too, passes. Suggested fix: accept a change-id match only for a version that succeeds the recorded one (the change's rewrite history), or record the operation the value was sealed at and require the current view to descend from it | `bug_1065_an_earlier_version_of_the_same_change_is_an_older_revision` |
| BUG-1067 | medium (**fixed**) | secret shares / delivery / data loss | **Fixed** (review cycle 3): a held copy is never written over (`create_new`, the next free `-<n>`), and once a received copy is held back its path is no longer "received": a later file there is the person's. **Holding back a received copy writes over what it held before, and moves whatever is at the path, every snapshot.** dev edits the copy they received (their own edit, which a later version never overwrites), joins the file's folder: the next snapshot moves the edit to `.veil/local/secret-shares-held/<share id>`. dev then writes a file of their own at that path: the next snapshot moves it too, to the same name, and dev's edit is gone. dev can also never record a file of their own at that path while the received record stays. | `hold_back_received` (`share_cmd.rs:808`) decides by the path alone (any regular file there, `:811`), and writes to one fixed name per share (`:830`, `:833`) over whatever is there. Suggested fix: never write over a held file (a new name each time), and forget the received path once its copy is held back (later files there are the person's) | `bug_1067_holding_back_a_received_copy_never_loses_what_it_held` |

### Found by the secret-shares stream

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-1068 | medium (open; **pre-existing**: `integration` @ `d8cf2b2`; found running `verify-server.sh` for secret shares cycle 3) | server / derived history / YugabyteDB | **The S12 reproduction `bug_868_many_snapshots_slow_every_later_landing` fails on YugabyteDB with a statement timeout** (`57014`, `derive::catch_up` at `derived.rs:252`), in the `hostile-yugabyte` step and alone on a fresh database, on this branch and on `integration` @ `d8cf2b2` itself (built from a clean worktree): 397 s, then the timeout. It passed in this branch's `verify-server.sh` run of 2026-11-07 before `integration` brought S10 and S4 (`8de9cd8`, hostile-yugabyte 9/9). The machine's load was high (load average 76 to 141) during both failing runs, so load may be part of it. | not investigated (outside the shares stream): a derive statement that exceeds `STATEMENT_TIMEOUT` on YugabyteDB for this history | `server_hub` `derived::s12_adversarial::bug_868_many_snapshots_slow_every_later_landing` (YugabyteDB) |
## S10 commits through the API

Found building S10 (`integration` @ `0fbe207`), both in the push checks S1 built.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-930 | low (open; **pre-existing**: S1) | server / secrets at rest | **An object uploaded through `POST …/objects` is stored before any check, so a plain-text secret a push then refuses stays in the object store, unreferenced.** Attacker: none outside the boundary (a member's own, or their buggy client's, secret). Harm: the invariant "secrets are only ever stored encrypted" fails for refused uploads: the server, which is never trusted with secrets, holds one in the clear. Nobody can read it (unreferenced objects are never served). `vl` never uploads one (it scans and seals first), and a commit through the API holds its inline text in memory until the checks pass (§13.13). | Uploads are stored first and checked when a push reaches them (S1's "invisible until a push reaches them"); nothing removes what a refused push uploaded. Suggested fix: hold uploads in a staging area that a refused or abandoned push's cleanup deletes (with S29's background job), or scan secret-looking uploads at upload. | `server_hub` `changes::bug_930_a_refused_upload_leaves_no_plain_text_secret_stored` |
| BUG-931 | medium (**fixed**; **pre-existing**: S1) | server / secrets / push | **Fixed:** checks 8 and 9 run on every term of a conflict entry as on a file. **A pushed conflict entry skipped checks 8 and 9:** its terms could hold a plain-text `.env` (at a secret path) or a token in an ordinary file, and the push landed. Attacker: a hostile client (a member's agent bypassing `vl`). Harm: a shared secret stored and served in the clear to everyone who reads the folder, and the secret scan bypassed. | `check_changes` skipped entries whose kind does not hold a blob (`EntryKind::Conflict`), so its terms' objects were never classified, label-checked or scanned. | `server_hub` `changes::bug_931_a_conflicts_terms_get_the_secret_checks` |

## Adversarial pass: S10

Branch `t3/5a7dd43b` @ `1a51b65` (S10: commits through the API; includes `integration` @ `a97af94`), reviewed at merge `5fe3ba5`. Tests in `server_hub` `adversarial_s10`.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-1050 | high (**fixed**; introduced by S10) | server / API commits / closure | **Fixed:** an object counts as known at a path only when it is the entry there in the **caller's projection** of `base` (or the caller uploaded it), so at a path they cannot see every object gets the missing answer, before anything reads it, whatever it names. **`put.object` at a path the caller cannot see confirms a guess of the hidden file's content.** A dev names, at `bill…/acme/invoice.txt`, the id of the content they guess is there (the hash of "the invoice says 5,000"): a right guess answers 403 "you do not have access to 'bill…'", a wrong one 400 "missing object". Same for a nested hidden folder (`src/sec…/key.txt`). A push naming the same objects answers both alike (the test's control). Attacker: a member without the folder. What they control: `put.object` and `path`. Harm: BUG-824's class, through a new route: confirms a low-entropy guess of hidden content (an amount, a name, a flag) and that the path exists. | `changes::entries` (`crates/veil-server/src/changes.rs:253`) takes an object as known when it is the entry at that path in the **real** `base` tree, and then reads it, before the graft refuses the hidden path; the push judges closure only after the graft. Suggested fix: judge "the entry at that path in `base`" on the caller's projection of `base` (or run closure after the graft, as the push does). | `adversarial_s10::bug_1050_a_put_at_a_hidden_path_never_confirms_a_guess_of_its_content` |
| BUG-1051 | medium (**fixed**; introduced by S10) | server / API commits / policy | **Fixed:** access lines are judged against the actual parent only (`sync::complete`); a change of the default branch's access lines is accepted only when the branch moves, and only from an admin (anyone else's change is kept, `rejected`); after every lost race the attempt reads the accepted policy and the caller's view again, so a rebase is never built under a stale view; an admin's rebased commit lists "access accepted" only for its own change. **An API commit whose move is refused, on a base older than the accepted policy, fails with 403 "only admins may change who has access", and the change is not kept.** A dev's change on an old base (before an admin's access change that also touched a file they see), with a stale `expected` or a `rebase` that cannot apply, should answer `rejected` and keep the change on its ref. Attacker: none (a wrong answer). Harm: a false, alarming reason, and the change is lost, for agents working on older bases. **The fix must keep a guard this check gives by accident:** a rebase onto a tip that changed the policy is built with the request's **stale** `Plan.project` (its write check uses the old access); today this check refuses it on the default branch. | `changes::build` (`changes.rs:435-445`) compares the change's tree policy with the accepted policy whenever `onto` names the default branch, even when the change is not on the tip (refused moves build on `base`). Suggested fix: judge access lines against the parent (as `sync::complete` does), accept only on a move, and reload the project (accepted policy) for each rebase attempt. | `adversarial_s10::bug_1051_a_refused_move_on_an_older_base_is_rejected_not_an_access_change` |
| BUG-1052 | medium (**fixed**; the API route introduced by S10; the push route **pre-existing**: S1) | server / DoS | **Fixed:** every thread that runs the server's work has a 64 MiB stack (`veil_server::THREAD_STACK`, set on the runtime by `main.rs` and the tests; address space only), which fits the recursive walks at `MAX_TREE_DEPTH`; the API takes exactly the tree's limit (1,024 folders above a file). Both routes are tested at the limit (they land) and one folder past it (refused). **A path 500-1,024 levels deep overflows a server worker's stack and aborts the whole server** (`fatal runtime error: stack overflow`, SIGABRT), for everyone. Through the API: one ~1 KB request (`{"path": "src/d/d/…/x"}`, 600 levels) from any participant, even a read-only one, and even under a folder they cannot see (it overflows before any check). Through a push: a crafted tree 600 levels deep. §13.13 documents up to 1,024 levels as accepted (and `MAX_TREE_DEPTH` is 1,024). Attacker: any participant. Harm: denial of service of the whole server, repeatable at will. | `changes::apply` recurses once per level (`changes.rs:163-231`), as do `Graft::dir`/`diff` and `Projector::dir_at` (`veil-core/src/hub/project.rs`), all on tokio's 2 MiB worker and blocking-pool stacks, which hold about 500 levels; the depth checks allow 1,024. Suggested fix: run `sync::blocking` work on threads with a stack sized for `MAX_TREE_DEPTH` (the runtime's `thread_stack_size`, or a dedicated pool), with a test at `MAX_TREE_DEPTH` for both routes; or make the recursions iterative. | `adversarial_s10::bug_1052_a_deep_edit_never_takes_the_server_down` (runs the attack in a child process, `deep_edit_in_a_child`) |
| BUG-1053 | medium (**fixed**; introduced by S10) | server / API commits / S3 | **Fixed:** objects are stored at most 64 at a time (`sync::put_all`, the uploads' own loop, now shared), and the S3 client tries a 429 again as it does a 5xx. **An API commit stores all its objects at once, one task each, with no bound,** so a change within the documented limits sends tens of thousands of S3 PUTs together: MinIO answers HTTP 429 and the request fails with 500 "internal error". 10,000 flat edits fail about 1 run in 3; 1,000 edits 100 folders deep (100,000 objects from a 236 KB request) fail every time. Attacker: any writer, by accident or on purpose. Harm: changes within the limits fail; every such request also floods the shared S3 store, so other requests get 429s. | `changes::store_all` (`changes.rs:452-462`) spawns one task per object in a `JoinSet` with no cap. Suggested fix: store with bounded concurrency (as uploads do), with S3's retry on 429. | `adversarial_s10::bug_1053_a_change_of_ten_thousand_files_lands_on_s3` (fails on MinIO, as `scripts/verify-server.sh` runs it) |
| BUG-1054 | medium (**fixed**; introduced by S10) | server / API commits / hidden history | **Fixed:** an `expected` move is judged on the caller's view only: `base` must be the tip as they see it, or a stack of up to 64 of their own unlanded changes on it, each writing only paths they may still write. When changes they cannot see landed in between, their stack is made again on the real tip (each change their view of it grafted onto the new parent, with the push's checks; their projection of each keeps its id), so the answer and what they receive are the same either way (`changes::a_stack_lands_over_a_hidden_change_which_it_keeps_and_never_shows`, `a_stack_writing_where_its_author_lost_access_is_rejected`). **An `expected` move is refused as "not a fast-forward" when a change the caller cannot see landed on main, which reveals that change.** A dev stacks a change on their own unlanded one (§13.13: "to stack on an unlanded change … use `expected`"), expecting main where they see it. Without a hidden change: `ok`. After an admin's billing-only push, which is not in the dev's history and leaves the dev's main unchanged: `rejected`, "not a fast-forward". A push maps a projected tip to the real tip and lands. Attacker: a member without the folder, who probes. Harm: learns that a hidden-only change landed, and when (S1 keeps such changes out of their history, §13.4); and the documented stacking workflow fails. | `changes::create` (`changes.rs:651`) checks `is_ancestor(real tip, real base)` in the **real** history, so a hidden-only tip on top of the base's parent is not an ancestor of the base. Suggested fix: judge the fast-forward on the caller's view (the projected tip is an ancestor of the projected base), and build on the real tip, as a push does. | `adversarial_s10::bug_1054_a_hidden_change_on_main_does_not_refuse_an_expected_move` |

**Held up, as tested:**
* The push refactor (`sync::complete`, `project_one`, `perm::accept_policy`, `accepted_listing`): the old and new check order is the same line by line (graft, merge check, trees stored, closure, checks 2/8/9, access lines, then the branch's compare-and-swap); only the conflict-term checks (BUG-931) and check 9 are new. Every S1 suite passes on both databases (`scripts/verify-server.sh`, below).
* Rebasing over a hidden change in a folder the change edits (a delete and an add in `src/`, over a new version of the hidden `src/sec…/key.txt`) keeps it, and the change lands with exactly its edits (`s10_rebase_keeps_hidden_changes_in_edited_folders`).
* A file over a hidden folder, a delete of a hidden file, and a folder under a hidden file's name answer exactly as for missing names (`s10_kind_changes_across_a_boundary_answer_alike_for_hidden_and_missing`).
* 50 agents rebasing onto one main, under load: 8 copies at once on PostgreSQL and 3 on YugabyteDB, with MinIO; every change landed once, with exactly its edit.
* Change refs: the id is the server's (no squatting), revisions only by the creator (also in the upsert), and the same "no change … of yours" answer for others' changes and missing ones.
* Check 9 uses `vl`'s own scanner on the decoded blob, so no encoding is caught on one side and missed on the other; symlinks and conflict terms are scanned; errors and audit rows carry only the path, rule and line; refused inline text is never stored.

**Out of scope (trusted):** a member's own crafted push whose secret entry carries a plain-text `fallback` (checks 8 and 9 read only the entry's object), and a "sealed" object that is plain text under an envelope header: both need the member's own client to be hostile toward their own secret.

**Nit** (not filed; **fixed** with BUG-1051: each attempt reads the accepted policy again; from the code, `changes.rs:438-440`, not tested): when a rebase moves an admin's API commit onto a tip whose policy another admin has already accepted, the answer lists "access accepted …" as if this admin had changed access (the request's `Plan.project` is stale; same root as BUG-1051).

### S10 cycle 2

Branch `t3/5a7dd43b` @ `e443637` (the fixes for BUG-1050..1054 in `b5189d5`, and format 8 merged in `b3772e3`/`e443637`), reviewed at merge `aab97dc`. Tests in `server_hub` `adversarial_s10`, run on PostgreSQL + MinIO.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-1055 | medium (**fixed**; introduced by S10's format-8 check, `e443637`) | server / push and API commits / format 8 | **Fixed:** one rule, `policy::sealed_secret`, decides for `vl upgrade` and the server's push checks (pushes and API commits): a sealed file is a secret's when its path names one, or its content descends from one (a parent held a secret sealed at that path, or a secret of the same scope left its path in this snapshot; `vl upgrade` also counts an object that was a secret's before). **A push of a renamed secret, which `vl` itself keeps sealed, is refused: "'settings-from-env.txt' is not a secret file: only secret files are encrypted".** Format 8 keeps a secret sealed when it is moved to a path no rule names ("protection follows a secret across a rename", `veil-core/src/builder.rs` `protect_moves`; `docs/FORMAT.md` §5 "a renamed secret that format 8 keeps sealed"; `upgrade.rs` accepts it). The admin of the test project runs `mv billing/.env settings-from-env.txt`, then commits (the entry stays `cloaked`) and pushes: refused. Every later push of that history is refused too, and so is the same entry through the API. Attacker: none (a wrong answer to a member's own work). Harm: a member's legitimate history cannot reach the server. The only way out is to rewrite the rename, or to store the secret in plain text, which check 9 then refuses. | `sync::check_changes` (`sync.rs:770`) accepts a sealed object at a `Tier::Public` path only when `policy::names_secret(path)`. That is the built-in names, not where format 8 seals: the server judges by the path, while `vl` and `vl upgrade` judge by descent. Suggested fix: accept a sealed entry at a non-secret path also when it descends from a secret, by `upgrade::removed_content`'s rule (a parent held a sealed entry at that path, or a sealed entry of the same scope that this snapshot removes). Share one helper between the two. | `adversarial_s10::bug_1055_a_renamed_secret_that_vl_keeps_sealed_is_accepted` |
| BUG-1056 | medium (**fixed**; introduced by BUG-1054's fix, `b5189d5`) | server / API commits / hidden history | **Fixed:** `restack` also counts as known the folders of the caller's view of their change on the way to each path it changed (a new folder, or one they see whole). **A stack over a hidden change fails with 400 "missing object" when one of its changes holds a folder its maker sees whole.** Two cases: a dev's unlanded change adds `src/newdir/first.txt`, or the dev sees everything in the fork (a project with only `src/` and `.veilpolicy` granted to devs). After an admin's change the dev cannot see lands on main, the dev's next change with base = that change and `expected` = main (as they see it) answers `400 missing object <id>` instead of `ok`. Without the hidden change the same request lands (BUG-1054's own test passes only because every folder of its change is one the projector rebuilt). Attacker: a member without the folder, who probes. Harm: the documented stacking workflow fails, and the different answer reveals that a change they cannot see landed on main, and when (BUG-1054's class, through the fix). | `changes::restack` (`changes.rs:478-489`) passes as `known` only the objects of the leaf entries the change made, plus the trees the projector created. A real tree of the caller's (a new folder, or any folder of a wholly visible change) is neither, so the graft's `check_pushed` (`veil-core/src/hub/project.rs`, `Graft::proving`) answers `Missing`. Suggested fix: count as known every tree of the caller's view of the old change (their own change, checked when it was made), e.g. the folders along each changed path. A local probe that adds them makes both cases land, with the caller's ids unchanged. | `adversarial_s10::bug_1056_a_stack_over_a_hidden_change_lands_when_it_adds_a_folder` |

**Held up, as tested or traced:**
* BUG-1050..1054's reproductions all pass un-ignored on PostgreSQL + MinIO (`bug_105[0-4]`).
* **BUG-1050:** `entries` reads nothing before the caller's projection of `base` names the object at that path; an uploaded guess at a hidden path gets the graft's "no access", whatever the guess.
* **BUG-1051:**
  * The accepted policy changes only together with a compare-and-swap of the default branch (`perm::accept_policy` is called only from the push's and `commit`'s CAS), so no policy change can fall between an attempt's reload and its CAS.
  * Group-membership changes are not serialised with a commit, as for a push (a race equal to "landed just before").
  * The loop runs at most `MAX_REBASES + 1` builds: `keep_on_loss` after that, and immediately for `expected`.
* **BUG-1054 restack:**
  * The graft keeps every hidden change of the tip (the caller's view of each change only replaces what they see), and `complete` re-runs checks 2/8/9 and the access-line rule.
  * A non-admin's stack cannot carry access-line edits: each change was refused them against its parent.
  * Admins see everything, so they never restack.
  * Write access lost since a change was made is refused per file (Graft changes are leaves).
  * The caller's ids are unchanged: Ed25519 is deterministic and the projection is server-signed.
  * A hidden finance-branch snapshot a no-`onto` change was resolved onto is dropped by the restack, not landed.
* **64 MiB stacks:** on the runtime's workers and blocking pool; derive's recursion (`derive/changes.rs` `walk`) is depth-limited and runs on workers. Address space only: tokio's 512 blocking threads come to 32 GiB virtual.

**Out of scope (trusted):** a member's own client sealing a `.env.example` through `cloak .env.*`. This is deliberate (`policy.rs` `classify_rules`: "the only paths a `cloak` line seals"), and it is their own content.

**Not filed:** timing. A stack made again takes a few extra round trips when a hidden change landed, but every request whose tip moved already computes a new projection (`tips` included), so the class is pre-existing and no new channel opens.

## Server S4 (request-path caches, one commit per push)

Found while building S4 (`HUB.md` §13.14).

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-910 | low (**fixed**, S4; **pre-existing**: S1) | server / push | **Fixed:** a push whose every branch update is refused (the branch moved since the pusher pulled) commits nothing. **A refused push recorded its snapshots as committed history** though no branch reaches them, and cost a database commit per lost race: `resolve` then treated them as history, so their projections could be fetched by id (by someone who learns it; ids are unguessable hashes, and everything served is still projected for the asker). | `sync::push` ran its transaction (snapshot, origin and audit rows) whether or not any compare-and-swap was planned. | `server_hub::caches::tips_follow_pushes_on_every_server` (the snapshot count after a refused push) |
| BUG-911 | high (**fixed** by the BUG-911 fix; see its row in "BUG-911 and BUG-912" below; **pre-existing**: `integration` @ `7a3a9b0`, S12) | server / derive worker / database pool | **Landings across 100 projects stop: none lands in 15 s** (`veil-bench load --mode land --projects 100 --clients 1`, local objects, PostgreSQL; with and without S4), and the server's log shows `deriving the history of project N: Db(PoolTimedOut)` for most projects. Landings across 10 projects work. Every request waits for a database connection the derive runs hold. | `derive::landed` starts one background run per project that landed; each run takes the project's advisory lock and makes many autocommit statements on the server's one pool (64 connections in the benchmark), so 100 concurrent runs hold or queue for every connection, and pushes and pulls wait until the pool's 30 s acquire timeout. Suggested fix: bound the derive runs that hold connections at once (a semaphore well below the pool size), or give the deriver its own small pool, so request handlers always find a connection. | `scripts/bench-server.sh load` (`land`, 100 projects) |
| BUG-912 | medium (**fixed** by the BUG-911 fix; see its row below; S12's test) | server / derive / YugabyteDB under load | **`derived::s12_adversarial::bug_868_many_snapshots_slow_every_later_landing` fails in the full container suite on YugabyteDB:** `canceling statement due to statement timeout` (`57014`) inside `derive::catch_up` (`derived.rs:252`), twice in the S4 branch's runs (`scripts/verify-server.sh`, and the suite alone on a fresh database, both with the machine at load 40–60). Run alone it passes (630 s). | The test derives 30 snapshots of more than 100,000 paths each, then times one landing; on one loaded YugabyteDB node a deriving statement runs past the 15 s statement timeout (`db::STATEMENT_TIMEOUT`). Suggested fix: smaller batches in the deriver's statements for very wide snapshots, or a test size that fits the timeout on YugabyteDB. | `server_hub::derived::s12_adversarial::bug_868_many_snapshots_slow_every_later_landing` (YugabyteDB, full suite) |

## Adversarial pass: S4

Branch `t3/7da9a2a5` @ `aba3af2` (S4: request-path caches and one commit per push; includes `integration` @ `7435b69`, S10). Tests in `server_hub` `adversarial_s4`. No finding lets a removed or narrowed person be served anything after the change commits, on either server.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-913 | medium (**fixed**, S4 review fixes; introduced by S4) | server / caches / DoS | **Fixed:** every entry is weighed by what it holds (strings, a parsed policy's rules, a view's group set, branch rows, the cache's own bookkeeping), each cache has a byte budget (sessions 16, projects 32, permissions 32, snapshot resolution 64, tips 32 MiB; an entry heavier than its budget is not kept), expired tip rows are freed as time passes, and the server bounds branches per project (10,000), branch updates per push (1,000) and groups per project (1,000) (`HUB.md` §13.14). The reproduction, in a process of its own: expired tips of 100 projects keep 33 MiB (was 264), 50 permission entries 19 MiB (was 227), and a project entry weighed 39 MiB holds 30 MiB. **The caches are bounded by entry count, not bytes, and the values are sized by the attacker, so they can grow a server's memory without limit.** Three cases, measured in one process: (1) **branch tips**: 100 projects of 10,000 branches each (one push of about 1.6 MB of JSON per project; no limit on updates per push), each read once, keep **264 MiB** after they expire. Expired rows are never freed; only a later read of the same project replaces them, and the map holds up to 100,000 projects. (2) **Permissions**: 50 entries for a person in 250,000 groups keep **227 MiB**, and each entry counts as 1 of 100,000. There is no limit on groups per project, and an admin may add themselves to them. (3) **Projects**: a 1 MiB accepted policy of five-byte lines is weighed at 3 MiB and takes **49 MiB**, so the "64 MiB" budget holds about 1 GiB. Attacker: anyone who signs up (they create projects, groups and branches of their own). What they control: branch count and name length, group memberships, policy text. Harm: one server, or every server that serves their requests, runs out of memory for everyone. Before S4 this memory lasted one request; now it stays. | `caches.rs:177` and `:214` (viewers: count 100,000, weight 1 each, whatever `groups` holds). `caches.rs:259` and `:296` (tip rows stored with no weight; expiry only refuses them, it never frees them; `MAX_TIP_PROJECTS` at `:132` counts projects). `caches.rs:203` (the project weight counts text, not the parsed `AccessPolicy`, whose rules and warnings take 10–30 bytes for each byte of a short line). Suggested fix, for the class: weigh every entry by what it holds, and give each cache a byte budget (tips as an `LruCache` by bytes, dropped once expired; viewers by group count; projects by parsed rules). | `adversarial_s4::bug_913_caches_are_bounded_by_bytes_not_by_what_an_attacker_writes` (run alone: it measures this process's memory) |
| BUG-915 | medium (**fixed**, S4 review fixes; introduced by S4) | server / snapshot resolution / API commits | **Fixed:** a resolution entry is keyed by view key too, `(project, view key, projected) → real`, and filled only from that view's memo; another view's is never used, and without one the database's whole list is (`sync::resolve` takes the asker's view key). **The resolution cache keeps one real snapshot per projected id: whichever view key's memo was cached last. An API commit on a base the caller was shown is then refused with 404 "no snapshot … that you can see".** Example: devs and finance both see only `src/` before billing exists, so they share one projected snapshot. After a billing-only change, devs' projection of it is the same id. Once devs' memo for it is cached, the id stands for the billing-only change on that server. Finance's projection of that change is a different snapshot, so `resolve_base` finds nothing and refuses finance's commit on the base finance was shown. A server with a cold cache accepts the same request (the database lists every real snapshot the id stands for). §13.14 says the remembered real snapshot gives "the same tree and history" "for the viewers it is a projection for". The code never checks that the asker is one of them. Attacker: none (a wrong result). Harm: legitimate commits through the API fail, depending on which server answers and what others asked before. | `caches.rs:238` (`put_memo` overwrites `resolved` for the projected id with each view key's real snapshot), `sync.rs:437` (`resolve` answers that one real snapshot from memory), `changes.rs:696` (`resolve_base` relies on getting the database's list, newest first, and tries 16). Before S4, `resolve` always read the full list. Suggested fix: cache resolution per view key (key the `resolved` entry by `view_key` as well, as memos are), or cache only `committed`, and correct §13.14's sentence. | `adversarial_s4::bug_915_a_base_two_views_share_is_refused_once_the_other_views_memo_is_cached` (the cold server beside it is the control) |
| BUG-914 | low (**fixed**, S4 review fixes; **pre-existing**: S1; BUG-910's fix closes one route of its class) | server / push | **Fixed:** a push records only the new snapshots the updates that land reach (their snapshot and origin rows, and the server's cache of committed snapshots). **A push that moves one branch and is refused another still records the refused update's snapshots as committed history,** which no branch reaches. BUG-910's harm applies: their projections can be fetched by id by anyone who learns it (ids are unguessable, and everything is still projected), and every lost race in a push of several branches grows `snapshots`. A push with `main` refused (it moved) and a new branch accepted adds two `snapshots` rows; it should add one. | `sync.rs:1285`: `new_reals` is every completed snapshot, not those that the planned (accepted) moves reach. Suggested fix: write snapshot, origin and memo rows only for the snapshots reachable from the accepted updates' new tips. | `adversarial_s4::bug_914_a_partly_refused_push_records_the_refused_updates_snapshots` |

**Held up, as tested** (code read for each case; `caches::` run under load: 8 copies of the binary at once on PostgreSQL, 48/48, and 3 on YugabyteDB, 18/18):
- **Every access change bumps an epoch in its own transaction.** I checked every write route (`api.rs` group, member, admin, invite, device, bar and vouch routes; `auth.rs` sign-in and pending pruning; `perm::accept_policy`; `create_group`'s rebinding) against the triggers on all eight tables. Each input a cached decision reads (`Project`: name, default branch, policy text, bindings; `Viewer`: admin, groups, bar by signing key; session: device removed or pending, signing key, person name, expiry) is covered. Group managers, rings, key requests, invites, change refs, `device_vouches` and `device_expected` decide nothing cached; they are read fresh where they are used. A new group or person never inherits a rule (bindings are fixed at acceptance), so inserts that bump nothing are correct.
- **Read order:** the session row and its epoch come from one statement, and the project row and its epoch from one statement. `load_viewer` runs only after both epochs were read. So no entry is tagged with an epoch newer than its data, and an in-flight build after a change is tagged too old, so it misses. The `Caller` extractor runs after `retry_requests` buffers the body, so the time between reading the epochs and deciding is not the attacker's to stretch (a slow body does not delay the decision).
- **Multi-server:** the builder's two-server tests pass on both databases under load. Viewer entries are per device. Each view's memo is keyed by its view key. `committed` entries hold rows that are never deleted (no `DELETE` or `UPDATE` of `snapshots`, `projections` or `origins` anywhere).
- **Tips:** a cached tip is at most 100 ms old (its age counts from before the read), and its rows are always projected for the asker's current view. Push and API-commit `expected` decisions read the branches from the database (`sync::push` `current`, `changes::tip`), never the cache.
- **One commit per push:** objects are stored before the transaction. Snapshot, origin, memo, audit, branch and policy rows, and the notice, are in it. The deriver resumes at start. The checks (completion, scan, write and policy) all run before the compare-and-swap.
- **Epochs:** `gen_random_uuid()` is drawn from `pg_strong_random` in PostgreSQL 17 and in YugabyteDB 2025.2 (PostgreSQL 15 based). 60 bits, compared only for equality. No response, error or `stats.json` carries an epoch.

**Out of scope (trusted):** an operator's `TRUNCATE`, `session_replication_role = replica` or deleting `access_epochs` rows skips the row triggers or brings back an old epoch (0). The triggers cover every row write; the operator is trusted.

**Not filed:** a PostgreSQL `NOTIFY` serialises the commits of every notifying transaction in the database: the database lock is held until after the commit record is flushed. So pushes across all projects commit one at a time on PostgreSQL. This is throughput, not safety, and S4's own load gates would show it. Also, anyone can bump a victim's person epoch by adding them to and removing them from a group of the attacker's own project. That only costs the victim cache misses (deadlocks and conflicts are retried).

## Op log: concurrent recorders of one directory (race wave)

Branch `t3/1e08930a` (base `integration` @ `d8cf2b2`). Reproduced first with `oplog_cli::concurrent_status_loops_lose_nothing` run 24 at a time beside 40 CPU-bound loops (load 60–150), then deterministically: the test holds a workspace's working-copy lock itself, lets a real `vl` read the repository and block on it (seen in `/proc/locks`), changes what the other `vl` would have changed, and lets it go. **No content ever left the repository:** checkout only overwrites or removes a file that still holds what a recorded snapshot put there, so whatever left the disk is in a snapshot (the side change, or a rewritten `@` the op log keeps). But edits left the directory and `@`.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-1084 | high (**fixed**, race wave; **pre-existing**: the lock-free op log, format 6) | working copy / op log / concurrency | **Fixed:** `Ctx` re-reads the view and the checkout record after it takes the working-copy lock (`Ctx::lock_dir`, for recording and for `Ctx::finish`). **Concurrent `vl status` in one directory invented "recovered working-copy edits" changes and took edits off the disk.** A `vl` read the op heads just before another one published its snapshot and `checkout.json` just after that one rewrote it, then waited for the lock. Under it, the record named a snapshot newer than the view's `@`: the directory looked stale (the BUG-361 check compares the record with `@`, and fails the wrong way round). So it saved the directory as a side change and checked out the older `@`, removing the edit just recorded and those made since. That left a divergent change id (the side change's parent and the newer `@`). A record read while another `vl` was moving the directory had the same effect: the move was "finished" again from a stale `pending`. In one traced run, loop 3's last edit (`5`) was only in the side change; the disk and `@` held `4`. Failed 6 of 240 runs on the base under load (10 of 120 earlier), 0 of 480 after the fix at a higher load. | `Ctx::open` reads the view (`Repo::open`) and `checkout.json` (`load_state`) before any lock; `Ctx::record_into` and `Ctx::finish` took `WcLock` and then acted on those values. The lock serialised the recorders, but each judged the directory by what it saw before waiting. | `oplog_cli::bug_1084_a_recorder_judges_the_directory_under_its_lock` (deterministic: failed every run before the fix), `oplog_cli::concurrent_status_loops_lose_nothing` (stress) |
| BUG-1120 | high (**fixed**, race wave; **pre-existing**) | workspaces / `vl ws write` | **Fixed:** `ws write` reloads the view after it takes the workspace's lock, writes over the `@` it reads then, and records with `expected_wc` (looking again, up to 16 times, if superseded). **Two `vl ws write` to one workspace at once lost one write:** the second built its overlay on the `@` it read before waiting for the lock, so its snapshot recorded the workspace without the first one's file. The file was only in the rewritten snapshot the op log keeps. A command that changed the workspace's tree without the lock (an auto-rebase, `squash`) could be reverted the same way. | `cmd_ws` `Write`: `workspace_overlay` read `ctx.repo`'s view as `Ctx::open` loaded it, and `snapshot_workspace` without `expected_wc` records whatever tree the overlay holds over the current `@`. | `oplog_cli::bug_1120_ws_write_keeps_a_write_that_landed_while_it_waited` |
| BUG-1121 | medium (**fixed**, race wave; **pre-existing**) | working copy / `vl ws switch` / concurrency | **Fixed:** `Ctx::finish` moving the directory to another workspace holds the locks of the workspace it leaves and the one it goes to (in name order), and a recorder whose directory was attached elsewhere while it waited locks that workspace instead (`Ctx::lock_dir`). **`vl ws switch` moved the project directory while another `vl` was recording it into the workspace it was leaving**, which could record half-moved files there (BUG-113's class). Likewise, a recorder that found a live switch's journal under its own lock "finished the interrupted move" alongside the switch. | The project directory's lock is its workspace's (`ws-<name>.lock`), and `finish` took only the target's: the workspace being left was not locked, and a recorder never noticed it held the wrong one. | `oplog_cli::bug_1121_ws_switch_waits_for_a_recorder_of_the_workspace_it_leaves`, `oplog_cli::bug_1121_a_recorder_waits_for_a_live_move_of_the_directory` |

## Review: working-copy race fix

Branch `t3/1e08930a` @ `90209a4` merged with `integration` @ `b2478b5` (layer 3, tier A, Deep). Each reproduction is in `oplog_cli` and holds a workspace's working-copy lock itself, as the race wave's tests do.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-1125 | medium (**fixed**, race wave 2; **pre-existing**) | working copy / concurrency | **Fixed:** every command that writes into the project directory or records it holds `Ctx::lock_dir` from before it reads the directory until its closing snapshot: `vl renormalize` (and `Ctx::snapshot_with_line_endings`, which also finishes an interrupted move and refuses a directory another command moved), `vl restore`, `vl expose`/`cloak`/`isolate`/`ignore`, `vl upgrade --sync-secrets`, and, found by the same sweep, `vl access` and `vl upgrade --rule-keys` (both rewrite `.veilpolicy`) and `vl secret recover` (writes the file back). Nothing else in `vl` writes the directory's files or `checkout.json` outside the lock (the sandbox, mount, hooks, identity and confinement writes are outside the project directory's files). **`vl renormalize` and `vl restore` still act on the project directory without the working-copy lock:** the BUG-1084 class stays open for commands that write or record the directory outside `Ctx::record_into` and `Ctx::finish`. While `vl ws switch other` is moving the directory (it holds both locks, its journal is in `checkout.json`), `vl renormalize` records the half-moved directory into `other@`, so a file of `default`'s lands in the workspace being switched to (BUG-113's class). `vl restore` rewrites files while another `vl` holds the lock to record or check out the directory. Nothing leaves the store (the op log keeps every snapshot), but a workspace records the wrong files. The same pattern, one file each: `vl expose`/`cloak`/`isolate`/`ignore` (`cmd_policy_rules`) and `vl upgrade --sync-secrets` (`cmd_sync_secrets`) write `.veilpolicy` without the lock. | `Ctx::snapshot_with_line_endings` (`ctx.rs`) calls `snapshot_workspace_with` and `save_checkout` with no `lock_dir`, trusting the view and checkout record that the opening snapshot read under a lock it has since released. `cmd_restore` writes through `Ctx::restore` (`restore_paths`, and `save_checkout` of the in-memory record) and `ctx.fs` before `snapshot_restored` takes the lock. | `oplog_cli::bug_1125_renormalize_waits_for_a_live_move_of_the_directory`, `oplog_cli::bug_1125_restore_waits_for_another_recorder`, `oplog_cli::bug_1125_policy_rules_wait_for_another_recorder` |
