| BUG-1126 | medium (**fixed**, race wave 2; **introduced** by the race wave) | op log / `Ctx::finish` | **Fixed:** `finish` prints the merge warnings again after `lock_dir`'s re-read. **The merge warnings of `Ctx::finish`'s re-read are never printed.** `finish` prints the merge warnings, then `lock_dir` re-reads the view (`refresh` → `Repo::reload`), which merges heads that landed while the command waited for the lock. Only the process that writes a merge prints it, and later commands find it merged. In the test, two concurrent `vl describe` both moved `default`. `vl log` merged them in `finish` and checked out the winner ("Working copy now at: … first side"). Neither it nor any later command said that the workspace "was moved by concurrent commands" and the other working copy stayed a visible change. On the base the next command's open writes the merge and prints it. | `Ctx::finish` calls `print_merge_warnings` before `lock_dir`. Before the race wave, `finish` never reloaded. | `oplog_cli::bug_1126_finish_prints_the_merge_it_wrote` |
| BUG-1127 | low (**fixed**, race wave 2; **pre-existing**; a test defect) | tests / conventions | **Fixed:** the test looks for "two" as a whole description line (the pattern `undo_again_goes_further_back` already used), and so does `op_restore_is_undoable`, which had the same check. **`conformance_worktree::undo::op_restore_returns_to_an_earlier_state_keeping_edits` fails about 1 run in 240** (2 of 480 here, and once in `verify-fast`): its last assertion, `!r.out(&["log"]).contains("two")`, also matches a random change id. Change ids are made of the letters k–z, and `t`, `w` and `o` are among them (the BUG-105 class, which `CLAUDE.md` forbids). | `undo.rs:207` substring-matches `vl log` output that holds random change ids. Fix: look for the description on its own line, or use `log --json` descriptions. | the test itself (`cli_conformance`, `undo.rs:207`): 2 failures in 480 runs, 24 at a time |

## Server: the deriver apart from requests (BUG-911, BUG-912)

Filed by S4 (`t3/7da9a2a5`); fixed on `integration` @ `d981dba`. When S4's branch merges, its two unfixed rows give way to these.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-911 | high (**fixed**, bug911; **pre-existing**: `integration` @ `7a3a9b0`, S12) | server / derive worker / database pool | **Fixed:** at most 4 runs derive at once in a server process (`derive::DERIVERS`, a semaphore), on the deriver's own pool of 8 connections (`derive::DERIVE_CONNECTIONS`, apart from `--db-connections`, opened when first needed); a run holds a connection only while it holds its project's lock, and gives back its permit and connection between tries of a lock another server holds; a derive run's statements go to its pool (`Cx::pool`), `memo_tips` runs under a permit on the requests' pool (at most 4 of its connections). `veil-bench load` reports the deriver's lag (`derive_lag_s`). Across 100 projects (`--mode land --projects 100`, local objects, PostgreSQL): 1,364.8 landings/s, p50 68 ms, p99 198 ms, no errors, everything derived 0.42 s after the agents stopped. **Landings across 100 projects stopped: none landed in 15 s** (`veil-bench load --mode land --projects 100 --clients 1`, local objects, PostgreSQL; with and without S4), and the server's log showed `deriving the history of project N: Db(PoolTimedOut)` for most projects. Landings across 10 projects worked. Every request waited for a database connection the derive runs held. | `derive::landed` started one background run per project that landed; each run took a connection of the server's one pool for the project's advisory lock and needed a second for each statement, so with more runs than connections every connection was held by a run waiting for another (until the pool's 30 s acquire timeout), and pushes and pulls waited behind them. | `server_hub::derived::bug_911_landings_across_many_projects_while_derivers_run` (a run for each of 48 projects at once on 16 request connections, then a pull and a landing in each, eight at a time: on the base, a landing waited 30.06 s, until the derivers' `PoolTimedOut`, and with 48 at a time they failed `503 busy`; fixed, the slowest 68 ms on PostgreSQL and 160 ms on YugabyteDB, at load 36); `scripts/bench-server.sh load` |
| BUG-912 | medium (**fixed**, bug911; S12's test) | server / derive / YugabyteDB under load | **Fixed:** the file-history lookup of a change (`Batch::last_changes`) is one statement per 1,000 paths (`ROWS_PER_STATEMENT`, as the inserts are), not one for all of them. On the shared YugabyteDB (load 21), one statement for 100,000 paths with 8 rows each took 8.7 s; one for 1,000 took 85 ms. The statement that timed out is the deriver's, not the fixture's: `derived.rs:252` is `Hist::derive`, which unwraps `derive::catch_up`; the fixture writes one row a statement (`again`, which retries `57014`). The test is unchanged. Under the machine-wide hostile lock on the shared YugabyteDB: the base server (`integration` @ `d1fc6f9`) failed at `derived.rs:252:82` with `57014` after 422 s (load 12 rising to 130); with the fix it passed three times out of three, in 772, 929 and 1,000 s (load 37 to 186 during the runs), the landing after 30 `many` changes 1.52, 1.77 and 1.86 s against 2.05, 1.14 and 0.85 s listed. **`derived::s12_adversarial::bug_868_many_snapshots_slow_every_later_landing` failed in the full container suite on YugabyteDB:** `canceling statement due to statement timeout` (`57014`) inside `derive::catch_up` (`derived.rs:252`), twice in the S4 branch's runs (machine at load 40–60). Run alone it passed (630 s). | The test derives a change of 99,990 listed paths: its `prev` lookup was one `UNNEST … CROSS JOIN LATERAL` statement over every path, which on a loaded YugabyteDB ran past the 15 s statement timeout (`db::STATEMENT_TIMEOUT`) on every retry until the deriver's 120 s deadline. | `server_hub::derived::s12_adversarial::bug_868_many_snapshots_slow_every_later_landing` (YugabyteDB, full suite) |

## Adversarial pass: S5

Security review (layer 3, tier A, Deep) of milestone S5, read grants, the object routes and access epochs (`t3/b6663ac2` @ `df60d52`, merged with `integration` @ `d58a952`): `veil_core::hub::grant`, `crates/veil-server/src/grant.rs`, `migrations/0007_access_epochs.sql`, the grants in `GET …/tips`. All four findings are introduced by S5. Reproductions are in `crates/veil-server/tests/server_hub/grants.rs` (the container suite, `scripts/verify-server.sh`; ignored until fixed). Two new tests that pass record what held: `grants_are_void_after_a_group_deleted_a_name_rebound_and_a_device_removed` and `a_grant_racing_a_removal_never_outlives_it` (32 rounds, the tips request and the removal each started first; 8 copies at once).

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-1080 | important (**fixed**, S5 review cycle 1; a leak of hidden names, needing a guess) | server / grants | **Fixed:** the grant has no `view` (nothing read it); no value a client receives (tips, grants, epochs, errors) is a view key, and `HUB_VIEW_KEY` stays a server-side cache key. **Every grant hands its holder, anyone on Earth included, an offline oracle for the project's hidden access lines.** The grant body's `view` is `View::key`: an unkeyed BLAKE3 (`derive_key` with the public context `HUB_VIEW_KEY`) over the path of **every** access line of the accepted policy, hidden ones included, in order, each with whether the viewer reads it. Anyone who fetches the tips of a project with something public, without signing in, can test guesses of the whole `.veilpolicy` offline, without limit, and learn the names of folders they cannot see (`bill-77a1c3d9/`, `src/sec-9d0e4b12/`, `carol-only-3e8f21aa/` in the test: the right guess matches the grant's `view`, a guess with one name wrong does not). Policies are short and folder names guessable (`billing/`, `hr/`, `legal/`, `src/<x>/` under a public `src/`), so a small dictionary over the few positions confirms them. Teammates without access can do the same with theirs. Before S5 the view key never left the server (a projection cache key, CRYPTO.md: "not secret" because it was never sent). `docs/HUB.md` §13.4 promises hidden entries are left out "names included", and §13.8 accepts only online probing by writers. | `crates/veil-server/src/grant.rs:128` and `:138` put `View::key` (`crates/veil-core/src/hub/access.rs:209`) in the signed body; nothing reads it back (`grant::check` never compares it). Suggested fix: leave `view` out of the grant (nothing needs it; an unknown field is refused, so a later layer that needs a cache key can get a server-keyed one: e.g. keyed BLAKE3 of the view key under a server secret like the receipt key). | `bug_1080_a_grants_view_key_confirms_a_guess_of_hidden_folder_names` |
| BUG-1081 | important (**fixed**, S5 review cycle 1; broken invariant, `VISIBILITY.md`; metadata only) | server / epoch feed | **Fixed:** `GET /v1/epochs?projects=a,b` needs a session and answers the caller's own person epoch and the epoch of each named project the caller can see (others, and projects that do not exist, are left out alike). A grant carries its project's and person's epochs as `perm::open_at` built its view under them: S4's epochs (`0006`), random values new at each change with no server-wide row, so a grant shows neither a count nor a rate of changes, here or elsewhere. **The unauthenticated epoch feed shows anyone on Earth projects that have nothing public, and when their access changes.** `GET /v1/epochs?since=0`, without signing in, lists every project whose policy or bindings changed (its numeric id) and every person who left a group or removed a device (theirs), with an epoch that orders them; polling it gives the times. The test makes a project with nothing public (anyone's tips: 404) and finds its id listed. Ids are sequential: the feed gives the number of projects and people on the server, and which projects are active. Anyone's grant of a public project names that project's id, so the private ids are the others; a former member knows a private project's id from an old grant and can watch its access changes (people removed, devices lost). Every grant's `epoch` (the global clock) also gives anyone the server-wide count and rate of access changes. `VISIBILITY.md`: anyone on Earth "does not see a project with nothing public". §13.16 lists the feed's ids as a residual, but no owner decision accepts it. | `crates/veil-server/src/api.rs:59` routes `/v1/epochs` with no `Caller`; `grant.rs:410` answers every row. Suggested fix: require a session and list only the caller's own person and the projects they take part in (what a daemon needs, S6); regional caches (S9) authenticate as the server's own (inside the boundary). The clock in grants is then visible only to signed-in people; the owner decides whether that is acceptable or whether grants carry a per-project epoch instead. | `bug_1081_the_epoch_feed_shows_anyone_a_project_with_nothing_public` |
| BUG-1082 | medium (**fixed**, S5 review cycle 1; latent until caches use the feed, S6/S9) | server / epoch feed | **Fixed** by changing what the feed answers, not its paging: it gives every key asked for its current epoch (S4's table holds each key's current one), with no `since` and no pages, so no change can be passed over; a grant holds only while both its epochs equal the current ones. (No append-only log is needed for that; one would be for a server-wide delta feed, S9's to decide.) **The feed passes over a change it never lists when its key changed again:** `access_epochs` keeps one row per key, the newest epoch only. A cache reading from `since` gets a full page of 10,000 rows and moves to `now` = the page's last epoch; a key that changed inside that range and again after it is listed only at its later epoch, beyond the page, so the cache has passed the first change without seeing it and treats that person's or project's grants from before it as current until it reaches the newer row. In the test: project 1 changes (epoch 1), 10,000 other keys change, project 1 changes again; the cache's first page ends at `now` = 10,001 without project 1. The same happens without paging when a key changes again between the handler's clock read and its row query. §13.16 says "every epoch up to `now` is listed". Whenever more than 10,000 changes wait (a cache that restarts or fell behind, or a burst: anyone can create projects and push policies), the cache passes over the earlier change of every key that changed again, until it reaches that key's newest row; the owner-approved bound (5 seconds at most) then no longer holds. The server's own check (`newest_epoch`) is not affected. | `migrations/0007_access_epochs.sql:34` (`ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE` keeps only the newest epoch); `grant.rs:420` sets `now` to the last listed row's epoch. Suggested fix: append one row per bump (pruned after the grant lifetime plus the feed's lag) and page over those, so every epoch up to `now` is listed; `newest_epoch` reads the newest per key as it does now. | `bug_1082_the_epoch_feed_skips_a_change_whose_key_changed_again` (in a database of its own) |
| BUG-1083 | medium (**fixed**, S5 review cycle 1; availability) | server / tips | **Fixed:** an error making a branch's grant (the store failing to read or write) is logged and that branch gets no grant; the tips, and every pull, answer as before. **When the object store cannot be written, every pull by everyone but admins fails:** `GET …/tips` now stores the projected trees of each grant's root, and an error from `exists` or `put` fails the whole request (500 `internal`), so `vl pull`/`clone` break, though S1 needed no write to pull (its projected snapshot was memoised). The test makes the store's directories read-only after the deriver memoised dev's projection: dev's tips answer 500; with the error ignored in `grant_root` (tried), they answer 200. The comment at `grant.rs:117` says "a branch whose projected root cannot be stored gets none (the server logs it; S1's fetch still works)". A full disk, a read-only bucket policy or an outage of writes stops every non-admin's pull. | `grant.rs:182`–`183` propagate the store's errors with `?`, and `:132` propagates `grant_root`'s. Suggested fix: on a store error, log it and give that branch no grant (`Ok(None)`), as the comment says. | `bug_1083_a_store_that_cannot_be_written_fails_every_pull` |

**What S4's permission cache must guarantee** (S4 is built in parallel; phase-2 design §5.2: `(person, project, epoch) → groups, admin, view key, readable rules`): (1) an entry records the clock (`grant::clock`) read **before** the rows it holds were loaded, and a grant made from a cached entry carries **that** epoch, never the clock at the time of the grant: a grant stamped with a newer clock over an older cached view would outlive every removal in between; (2) an entry is used only while the newest epoch of its person and project (`grant::newest_epoch`, one indexed query, or a feed the server itself has read up to that request) is not above its recorded epoch, so that S1's routes (`tips`, `history`, `fetch`, `push`) still apply a removal at the next request; (3) an entry is keyed by the device as well, or re-checks the device bar (`device_bars` is per device; the person-wide epoch covers it only if the entry is dropped on that bump); (4) nothing else that decides a view (the accepted policy text and bindings, group membership, admin rows, device rows) may be cached outside such an entry. *Resolved with S4 (cycle 2): grants take the epochs `perm::open_at` built the view under; `grant::clock` is gone.*

**Held up** (tested or traced): every object route checks the grant (signature under the grant key and `hub-grant` only, then project, expiry, the device's request signature, the epoch) before any object is looked at, and reads only trees already reached from the grant's root (`Chains::check`: a step needs a directory entry naming the next tree, the last element may be a conflict object only when a conflict entry names it, and a conflict's terms are files or symlinks only, `Conflict::validate`); a hidden object and an absent one get the same 404 body, `Range` included, and are left out of a batch alike, in order; a tree shared by a visible and a hidden path, or a hidden subtree equal to a visible one, is the same bytes, so serving it through the visible path shows nothing more; an admin's grant is the real root, anyone else's the stored projection, and no other route serves a stored projected tree by id (`fetch` re-projects for its caller; push's closure check needs a receipt; S12's deriver works on real trees and has no route); embargoed snapshots never reach the server (the push refuses them); a grant is bound to its project's name (and reads that grant's project id only), its device's key (a copy is useless without it), its time, and its epoch; signature contexts carry `SIG_PREFIX ‖ context ‖ 0` and are pinned unique (`domains.rs`), the grant key is a key of its own, and a device's `hub-request` message (72 bytes) cannot be another context's; every change that takes access away bumps an epoch in its own transaction (a member removed, a group deleted, an admin row removed (no route removes one yet), a device removed by its person or barred by an admin, a policy or binding change, a group created under a name a line bound to a person); none of manager changes, branch policies or group creation without a binding takes access away, and no route deletes or renames a project, person or group's name; a grant racing a removal either carries the narrower view or is void once it commits (`a_grant_racing_a_removal_never_outlives_it`, 8 copies under load); the ±5-minute replay of a signed request returns only the same objects, and only until the grant's epoch or expiry ends it.

**Not filed, bounded in cycle 1:** a batch's read-ahead now counts a conflict's term by the size it records, and a tree or anything else recording no size as 1 MiB until it is read, then its real size; a `Range` read reads only the bytes asked for from the store (`BlobStore::get_range`; the directory store and S3 read the range alone). Before: the read-ahead of a batch counts a tree and a conflict's term as 0 bytes (`Chains::check`), as S1's `fetch` already does (`sync::check_wants`), so 64 large trees or conflicted files can be read at once, each whole (objects over 256 MiB are never cached); a `Range` read loads the whole object first, as `fetch` does. Counting a term's recorded size would close the first.


**Found while verifying S5 (pre-existing: the engine and CLI code is `integration` @ `15fe147`'s; S5 changes only `veil-core/src/hub/`, which neither path uses):**

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-1084 | medium (open; **pre-existing**) | working copy / concurrency | **Eight concurrent `vl status` loops in one directory leave stray "recovered working-copy edits" side changes under load:** in `verify-fast.sh` (machine load about 60) the log held 16 changes where 3 were expected, each a side change of `@` with description "recovered working-copy edits"; no edit was lost and `fsck` passed. Alone the test passes; 12 copies at once fail 3 times in 36. | Not diagnosed: the class of BUG-361/362 (a recording that finds the working copy moved under it, and keeps its edits as a side change instead of seeing that `@` already holds them), reached only when a `vl status` is slow enough to overlap others. | `cli_adversarial` `oplog_cli::concurrent_status_loops_lose_nothing`, 12 copies at once, 3 rounds (`for r in 1 2 3; do for j in $(seq 12); do $BIN oplog_cli::concurrent_status_loops_lose_nothing --exact -q & done; wait; done`) |
| BUG-1085 | low (**fixed**, fix1; **pre-existing**; the class of BUG-970) | tests / confinement / timing | **Fixed:** the storm's children wait on a FIFO instead of `sleep 4`, so they pile up however slowly the machine forks; they are let go once a fork is refused (systemd), and otherwise the shell waits until vl stops the storm (poll). The cause: under load the 32 children could not be forked within the 4 s each lived, and the run met its 8 s timeout first, which hides the process-limit warning (vl reports one stop reason). `--timeout 60s` stays only as a hang guard; the CPU case's `took < 20 s` is gone (vl's own report says what stopped it). **`confine_cli::limits_stop_runaways_and_vl_still_records` fails under load:** its systemd-scope case (`pids 32, timeout 8s`) did not finish as expected while the machine's load was about 60; alone it passes in 5 s. | A wall-clock limit (8 s) on a command that starts 400 background processes, on a loaded machine. Suggested fix: as BUG-970, assert on the limit being hit, not on the time. | `cli_workspaces` `confine_cli::limits_stop_runaways_and_vl_still_records` under load |
| BUG-1089 | medium (open; **pre-existing**: `integration` @ `b2478b5`; a regression after the allocator measurement at `f7e9aac`; found by S5's 1 GB memory gate) | server / memory / push | **The server's peak during the 1 GB profile's initial push is 1,341 MiB, over the 1 GB server gate** (`BASELINE.md`, "The server's allocator": 984 MiB at `f7e9aac`). The memory is kept afterwards, so every later case reads over 1 GB too (1,250–1,298 MiB). Measured the same evening on `integration` @ `b2478b5` (1,341 MiB, 238 s) and on S5 @ `ac92a54`, which adds S5 to it (1,334 MiB, 211 s), so S5 does not cause it. The client is within its gate in both (388 / 333 MiB). | Not diagnosed. Merged since `f7e9aac`: S4 (request-path caches), S10, S12 (derived data at each push) and S27 (compressed uploads, decoded and re-encoded by the server). | `bash ~/.cache/veil-orch/bench-quiet.sh scripts/bench-server.sh 1g` (the gate fails the run) |

### S5 cycle 2

Security review (layer 3, tier A, Deep) of S5's review-cycle-2 commits (`t3/b6663ac2` @ `d873721`, merged with `integration` @ `d8cf2b2`: S4 and S10 in; `git log --first-parent 4fd8446..d873721`): the fixes of BUG-1080–1083, grants on S4's epochs, bounded read-ahead and ranged reads. Reproductions are in `crates/veil-server/tests/server_hub/grants.rs` (the container suite, `scripts/verify-server.sh`; ignored until fixed). BUG-1086 and BUG-1088 are introduced by cycle 2 and S5; BUG-1087's class is S1's (`sync::fetch`), repeated by S5's batch route.

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-1086 | medium (**fixed**, S5 cycle 3; introduced by S5 cycle 2) | server / object routes / S3 | **Fixed:** an open range is sent open (`bytes=N-`), and a range past the end answers 416 with the length from a `HEAD`; a `Range` of an object stored compressed (S27) is decoded, then sliced, never a slice of the stored bytes. **On S3 (MinIO), a `Range` read of an object the server has not cached reads the whole object when the range is open, and fails with 500 when it starts past the end.** An open range (`bytes=START-`, what a resumed download sends) is sent to S3 as `bytes=START-18446744073709551615`; MinIO does not parse a position that large, ignores the range and answers the whole object (200), which `get_range` then slices: in the test, the last 10 bytes of a 4 MiB object read 4,194,890 bytes from the store. §13.16 says "a `Range` reads only the bytes asked for from the store". A range starting past the end (`bytes=LEN+5-LEN+9`) gets 416 with no `Content-Range`, which `S3Store::get_range` refuses as an error ("no length"), so `GET /v1/objects/…` answers `500 internal` where the directory store, and a cached object, answer 416. The client and the cache layer the routes are for (S6, S9) see a server error. No object leaks: the grant and chain are checked first. | `crates/veil-server/src/store.rs:234` formats `bytes={start}-{end}` with the `u64::MAX` that `parse_range` (`grant.rs:361`) uses for "to the end"; `store.rs:236`–`:243` require `Content-Range` on a 416. Suggested fix: send `bytes=START-` when the range is open, and on a 416 take the length from a `HEAD` (or the error's `ActualObjectSize`), so the S3 store answers as the directory store does. | `grants::bug_1086_s3_ranges_read_only_the_bytes_asked_for_and_answer_as_the_directory_store` (S3 only: compares `S3Store::get_range` with `FsStore::get_range`, and counts the bytes MinIO sends through a proxy) |
| BUG-1087 | medium (**fixed**, S5 cycle 3, in both routes; DoS; the class is **pre-existing** in S1's `fetch`) | server / object routes / memory | **Fixed** in the batch route and in `fetch`: a push or an API commit whose entry records less than its object holds (plus 4 KiB) is refused, so recorded sizes bound reads; an object that records none costs what its encoding declares (S27 frames declare their size); every read takes its cost from one server-wide budget (`store::DECODED_IN_FLIGHT`, shared with S27's upload decoding) and waits only while its request holds none of it; a repeated id is read once (the batch sends it once; `fetch` keeps it for its later wants inside its window). Measured: 64 wants of one 8 MiB file, unread: 0 MiB (batch), 38 MiB (`fetch`), against 858 MiB before. **A batch's read-ahead is bounded by sizes the pusher wrote, which the server never checks, so one request holds hundreds of MiB.** `Chains::check` returns the size the naming tree entry records, and the batch admits objects by it (at most 64 objects and about 8 MiB ahead). A push is accepted whatever its entries' sizes say. The dev records an 8 MiB file as 1 byte in `src/` and asks for it 64 times in one batch (the same chain each time) on a server of the deployment that has not cached it: all 64 reads start at once, each its own copy, and stay held while the client does not read. Peak growth for that one request: **858 MiB**; the same file with its true size: 57 MiB (the control). Attacker: anyone who signs up (they push to a project of their own) or any writer. What they control: entry sizes, repeated ids, and when they read the answer. Harm: objects up to 2 GiB are accepted (upload body limit), so a few requests run a server out of memory for everyone. S1's `fetch` (`sync::check_wants` sizes, the same window) is open to the same lie; trees (counted as 1 MiB until read) and conflict terms (sizes the conflict object records) are the same class. | `crates/veil-core/src/hub/grant.rs:262` (the entry's size), `crates/veil-server/src/grant.rs:287` and `:290` (admission by it); `sync.rs:709` and `:621` for `fetch`; nothing at push compares an entry's size with its object. Suggested fix, for the class: bound read-ahead by sizes the server knows: check at push that each file entry's size is its object's, or record each object's length at upload and admit by that. Also read a repeated id once per request. | `grants::bug_1087_a_batch_reads_ahead_by_sizes_the_pusher_wrote` (runs in a process of its own: it measures memory) |
| BUG-1088 | low (**fixed**, S5 cycle 3; introduced by S5; metadata only) | server / grants | **Fixed:** a grant holds no database id (`project` by name, `device` by key); checking it looks both up, with the epochs, in one statement. **A grant names its project and person by their sequential database ids, so anyone counts the server's private projects and people.** The grant body carries `project_id` and `person` (`BIGSERIAL`). Anyone who signs up creates a project and reads its id in their own grant. Two of them, made before and after others create projects, give how many projects everyone else created in between: exactly 5 in the test, on a database of its own. A person's own id gives how many people signed up before them. Anyone on Earth reads the same from the grants of public projects, though `VISIBILITY.md` says anyone on Earth does not see a project with nothing public. No other answer of the server shows these ids (BUG-1081's fix removed them from the feed). Nothing is known of a project but that it exists. | `crates/veil-core/src/hub/grant.rs:67` and `:70` (`project_id`, `person`), filled at `crates/veil-server/src/grant.rs:133`–`:134`. Suggested fix: leave both out of the body, and resolve the project by name and the person by the device key when the grant is checked (both are in S4's caches); or the owner accepts it as a residual in §13.16. (On YugabyteDB, sequences hand out ids in cached blocks, so the count is coarser there; the test is exact on PostgreSQL.) | `grants::bug_1088_a_grants_ids_count_the_servers_private_projects` (in a database of its own) |

**Held up** (tested or traced):
- **Grants and S4's caches.** A grant's epochs are the ones `perm::open_at` built its view under. The session check reads the person's epoch (and the path's project epoch, when that project is cached) in one statement, before any cached entry is used. A cached project entry is used only while its epoch is the one read, and a cached viewer only while both its tags are. A viewer loaded from the database is read after both epochs and tagged with them. So a tag is never newer than the data under it, and neither is a grant made from it. The object routes check the grant against the database's current epochs on every request (`Grants::current`, never a cache), so a removal committed on any server voids the grant at the next request. Every trigger in `0006` that takes access away bumps the key that `check` reads: group members, admins, sessions and devices bump the person, bars bump the device's person, and policy, bindings and group rename or delete bump the project. No route deletes a project or a person. `a_grant_racing_a_removal_never_outlives_it` and the four void-grant tests pass.
- **Epochs reveal nothing.** Each is 60 random bits from `gen_random_uuid`, per key, with no server-wide row. `changed_at` is never sent. A grant's person epoch is the holder's own. Anyone on Earth's grants carry person epoch 0. The project's epoch shows only what §13.16's residual already accepts: that some access change happened.
- **The feed.** It requires a session (401 otherwise) and returns only the caller's own person epoch, so it cannot show when someone else's access changed. A named project the caller cannot see, one that is barred, one that does not exist, and any error during the lookup are all left out the same way, through the same `perm::open_at` the tips use. In steady state each name costs one query whether it exists or not (once a viewer is cached). The extra query for an existing project on its first lookup is the tips route's, design row 14's residual, and is not new.
- **No view key or clock left.** No `clock`, `newest_epoch`, epoch token or view key appears in grants, tips, errors or headers. `HUB_VIEW_KEY` is used only inside the server (`sync::resolve`).
- **Ranged reads.** The grant and chain are checked before any range is read. A hidden object's `Range` gets the same 404. The directory store and a cached object handle `START-`, `START-END`, a start at or past the end (416 `bytes */LEN`), an end past the length, `u64::MAX`, and a suffix, reversed or multiple range (the whole object, 200) without off-by-one errors.
- **Batch memory with true sizes.** With true sizes a batch stays within about 8 MiB read ahead, plus one object and the four-chunk channel: 57 MiB measured for 64 wants of an 8 MiB file with a stalled client.

**Interactions to watch (not filed):** S27 (compressed objects, not merged): `BlobStore::get_range` reads bytes of the stored key directly and `Objects::get_range` skips any decoding, so once objects are stored compressed a `Range` would return a slice of the compressed bytes, and their length, unless S27 routes ranges through its decoding (or keeps ranged objects uncompressed). The same holds for S28's packs. Batches have no server-wide bound: N concurrent batches hold N times their window, as `fetch` does.

## Fix round 1: client bugs and flaky tests (fix1)

| ID | Sev. | Area | Finding | Root cause | Reproducing tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-1170 | low (**fixed**, fix1; **pre-existing**: BUG-1053's reproduction; the class of BUG-970) | tests / server / timing | **Fixed:** the 10,000-file change runs first and is the baseline; the 100,000-object change may take 60 times as long (it took about 22 times as long at load 99) before it counts as hung, never less than the 120 s of every other request (`Api::post_json_within`). **`bug_1053_a_change_of_ten_thousand_files_lands_on_s3` fails at a load above 100 and passes alone:** the deep change (100,000 objects stored on MinIO) took 88 s at load 99, and the test client gives every request 120 s. | A fixed wall-clock limit (the test agent's `timeout_global`, a hang guard) on a request whose duration grows with the machine's load. | `server_hub` `adversarial_s10::bug_1053_…` under load |
| BUG-1171 | low (**fixed**, fix1; **pre-existing**) | tests / conventions | **Fixed:** each check matches a phrase no change id can contain: the descriptions through `log --json` (`split_cli`), `record the mount of workspace` (`mount_props`), `/work` (`confine_cli`), `root snapshot` (six conformance tests). **Tests matched words spelled only in the letters k–z against output that also prints random change ids** (CLAUDE.md: never substring-match random change ids): `split_cli::squash_paths_and_hunks_into_parent_and_elsewhere` (`log.contains("work")`), `mount_props` (`!out.contains("mount")` on `vl undo`'s "Undid: commit change …"), `confine_cli::without_overlayfs_…` (`contains("work")` beside "Recorded …"), and six refusals of the root (`contains("root")`). A change id containing the word passes a positive check vacuously, and fails a negative one at random (one in about 10⁵ to 10⁶ runs per check). | Short words matched as substrings of whole command output. Not in the class: matches of 8-character id prefixes (a chance match is one in 16⁸), the root's fixed id `zzzzzzzz`, and outputs that print no change id (`resolve --list`, `identity new`, `files`). | `grep -rnE 'contains\("[k-z]+"\)' crates/*/tests` |
